# pim/psi: the PSI side, starting with the Librarian's loader

This directory is the PSI software side of the SIMPOS reconstruction.
It is reference software in Python and needs no hardware. Its plan is
`../docs/simpos/README.md` §4-§5. The first piece is step 1 of the
adopted route (`../docs/simpos/layers/OPTIONS.md`): **the Librarian's
loader**, which turns package templates (`.ptf`) into class objects.

**Everything here is OURS.** SIMPOS's own loader is lost. Each rule the
code applies is labelled SOURCED (with the printed source and page),
MEASURED (on the held files, with the probe named) or OURS (a choice
made here). The labels are repeated in the docstring of `librarian.py`.

## Files

| File | What it is |
|---|---|
| `librarian.py` | The loader. It reads templates, resolves and linearises natures, combines methods, builds the slot tables and the Fig. A-1 hash tables, and runs the "needs no SIMPOS" closure. |
| `plaintext.py` | The known-plaintext check: templates against the ESP source they were compiled from. |
| `held.py` | Where the eleven held `.ptf` files and their sources are. It reads them in memory from the repo archives, or extracts them as data into a new directory. |
| `test_librarian.py` | Tests: the TM-388 worked examples, Fig. A-1, slot rules, pinned facts about the loaded classes, and pinned agreement with the known plaintext. It is in `../run_tests.sh` and takes about 10 s. |
| `plaintext_disagreements.tsv` | Every disagreement between template and source, one row each, with its attributed cause. Generated by `plaintext.py --tsv`. |
| `kl0.py` | Step 2: a KL0 interpreter (OURS) running the compiled method bodies the loader builds. Its `convert`, `census` and `call` commands are below. |
| `kl0_opcodes.tsv` | The interpreter's opcode table and its dispatch: one row per opcode, each with a known-plaintext witness (compiled cells next to the held ESP clause). Hand-written. |
| `test_kl0.py` | Tests of `kl0.py`: table against handlers, `convert/3` against PIMOS, paragraph classes against their source, the census pinned. It is in `../run_tests.sh` and takes about 2 s. |
| `espc.py` | Step 3: an ESP compiler (OURS) producing templates and KL0 code that `librarian.py` loads and `kl0.py` runs. Its `compile`, `check` and `behave` commands are below. |
| `standin/` | SCAFFOLDING stand-ins for lost `simpos##` classes, compiled by `espc.py` into package `simpos`, each method labelled PRINTED or INTERPOLATED: `process.esp` (TM-100 Fig. 3.1 printed source plus four interpolated methods), `stream.esp` (TM-100 Fig. 3.6 printed source, three marked corrections; since step 3g also the interpolated `see/2`, `try_to_see/2`, `try_to_get/2`), the interpolated `list`, `post_queue`, `waiting_queue`, `process_clock`, `with_link` and `waiting_frame` that the printed code needs, and the interpolated `string` (`append/3`, `copy/3`) and partial `symbolizer` (`string_to_list/3`, `list_to_string/3`); since step 3c, the printed `channel`, `bounded_channel` and `bounded_post_queue` (TM-100 Figs. 3.7, 3.10, 3.11), the interpolated `post_queue_for_message`, `semaphore`, `list_index`, `hash_index` and minimal `standard_editor`, and more of `process`; and three HELD-SOURCE classes, `name`, `e_string` and `pathname`, carried over from morphana's `_simpos/` copies (ICOT Free Software, each method labelled `HELD-SOURCE: file:line`); since step 3d, ten more interpolated classes: `as_class_with_lock`, `with_lock`, `as_message`, `message`, `trace`, `common_network`, `network_listener_buffer`, `alarm_watch`, `time` and a minimal `pmacs_window`; since step 3e, the printed `as_program` (TM-100 Fig. 3.3) and the interpolated `network_node_name`; since step 3i, the interpolated `boot_process`; since step 3j, the interpolated `context` and `as_resource` (basis table in "Step 3j"); since step 3k, the interpolated `date_time` (basis table in "Step 3k"). |
| `test_boot_route.py` | Step 3o: the boot route's B8 top level, the debugger stand-in as the listener with TM-134 p71's dialogue typed, and the timer's alarms. It is in `../run_tests.sh` and takes about 10 s. `--png DIR` writes its screen. |
| `test_live.py`, `live_sessions/` | Window M11: the live view on `../tools/winview` (`../tools/simpos_live.py`). Recorded sessions (`live_sessions/*.script`) run through the live path give the scripted pins; the real viewer runs headless. It is in `../run_tests.sh` and takes about 40 s. See "Window M11: the live view". |
| `test_espc.py` | Tests of `espc.py`: emission against the opcode table, the settled language points, structure and behaviour against `paragraph.ptf`, macro banks against kappa2, the stand-ins. It is in `../run_tests.sh` and takes about 17 s of CPU (2026-11-24; wall time varies with the machine's load, 36-43 s at a load average of 116). |

## How to run

From `pim/`:

    python3 -I psi/test_librarian.py              # the tests (~10 s)
    python3 -I psi/librarian.py report            # per-package results
    python3 -I psi/librarian.py free              # the "102 classes" check
    python3 -I psi/librarian.py class paragraph##time_graph_window [--listing]
    python3 -I psi/librarian.py report --after forward   # the thesis's after-demon order
    python3 -I psi/plaintext.py --tsv psi/plaintext_disagreements.tsv
    python3 -I psi/test_kl0.py                    # the interpreter's tests (~2 s)
    python3 -I psi/kl0.py convert                 # convert/3 against pimos.vmac
    python3 -I psi/kl0.py census [--tsv FILE]     # every method of the 104 classes
    python3 -I psi/kl0.py census --fep [--tsv FILE]   # every method of pimfep and pimscsi, stand-ins loaded
    python3 -I psi/kl0.py census --fep --recipes      # the same, receivers made by their held creators
    python3 -I psi/kl0.py call paragraph##paragraph_version get_version/2 _
    python3 -I psi/test_espc.py                   # the compiler's tests (~8 s)
    python3 -I psi/test_boot_route.py             # step 3o, the debugger's listener (~10 s)
    python3 -I tools/simpos_live.py live          # SIMPOS live in a window (build tools/winview first)
    python3 -I psi/test_live.py                   # window M11, the live view headless (~40 s)
    python3 -I psi/espc.py check paragraph kappa2 utter [--tsv FILE]
    python3 -I psi/espc.py behave [-v]            # compiled paragraph against paragraph.ptf
    python3 -I psi/espc.py compile psi/standin/stream.esp --package simpos --listing

None of these needs an extraction: `held.py` decompresses the archives
in memory. To work from files instead, run
`python3 -I psi/held.py extract DIR` (DIR must be new or empty; 1,048
files in under a second) and pass `--held DIR`. Nothing from an archive
is executed. Members with absolute or `..` paths, and non-regular
members, are refused.

## Inputs (held)

| Package (declared) | File | Archive | Source held |
|---|---|---|---|
| pimfep, pimscsi, pimmcsp, pimmutil | `INSTALL.DIR/*.ptf` | `docs/nonlocal/psdpim.tar.Z` | no |
| mpsicsp | `INSTALL.DIR/mpsicspR.ptf` | same | no |
| paragraph | `object/paragraph.ptf` | `pimosobj.tar.Z` | `pimos.tar.Z` `src_util/paragraph` (83 `.esp`; release 3200 against the object's 3500) |
| kappa | `kappa2/ptf/kappa.ptf` | `ifs/kbms-clp/simpos/kappa2.tar.Z` | same archive (690 ESP files) |
| fts, qxt, sancho2, tour | `PTF/*.ptf` | `ifs/natural-lang/simpos/utter.tar.Z` | same archive (59 ESP files) |

That is 1,455 classes. Packages are keyed by the name each file
declares, so `mpsicspR.ptf` is package `mpsicsp`.

## What the loader builds

**Templates** (MEASURED on all 1,455 classes, no failures). The class
trailer is 13 terms anchored on the atoms `<class id>, template`:

1. the id and `template`;
2. two stamps;
3. the class-predicate code references;
4. the class slots as (name, flag, init) triples;
5. the instance-predicate code references;
6. the instance slots;
7. the local-predicate offsets;
8. a dynamic-predicate field;
9. the natures;
10. the referenced classes;
11. their union.

What was measured about these fields:

- **Code references.** Each one names the predicate whose header
  `(atom, kind<<8|arity, predh, 0)` points at that offset. Every
  reference resolves, and no header is left unreferenced.
- **Cell numbering (settled 2026-11-24).** A hash table spans
  `mask+1 + 2*keys + 2` cells (`ptfdump.table_span`), not its size
  word + 1 as ptfdump counted before.
  - The test: renumber every cell of the eleven packages with that span
    and count the headers whose head (predh) cell is not at offset - 2.
    Result: **0 of 32,901**. With the size word, 3,662 heads in 91
    classes were displaced.
  - Every local call (pmi `60`/`61`/`62`, target cell + 2 + signed 16
    bits) lands on a predicate's code: 35,542 of 35,542 (pinned by
    `test_cell_numbering`; with the old span that test fails).
  - Every class's code now ends exactly at `L` (554 of 554 in the six
    psdpim/paragraph packages; `ptfdump.py --check`, pins updated).
  - The size word is not a size. It is the miss offset: a key that is not
    found goes to cell + 2 + the word (`psi/fepread/ptfread.py`). In
    149 of 212 tables that is the cell after the table, where the word
    equals `mask + 2*keys + 1`. In 53 of the other 63 it is an
    instruction of the same predicate, such as a catch-all clause. In 10
    it is a cell tagged 0x96, which is UNKNOWN; `kl0.py` would refuse
    there. This is an ad hoc count over the eleven packages,
    2026-11-24.
  - The loader still finds each predicate by its header and renumbers
    from the header's offset; that is now the identity.
- **Term tags.** The trailer's term tags confirm more of the tag pairing
  in `layers/KL0.md` §2: list 2, strg 3, flt 10, svct 23, hvct 24 and
  mod 27, with code references as loc/cod plus a GC bit.
- **Slot flags.** 0x10 marks an attribute. Without it the slot is a
  component, and its name is `pkg##class$slot`, the renaming TM-388 §3.5
  describes. 0x02 means the initial value comes from the slot-init
  predicate (kind 0x22 or 0x32) at the code reference. 0x01 marks
  `slot is Class`. All three are witnessed against source (below). A
  slot declared without a value compiles to the constant 0.
  `slot := #Class` compiles to an init predicate, and `#"c"` compiles to
  a constant.
- **Header kinds.** All of them are witnessed by source: 0x25/0x35
  primary, 0x26/0x36 before-demon, 0x27/0x37 after-demon, 0x22/0x32
  slot initialisation, 0x11 local, 0x12 `$or$N`. Kind 0x26, a class
  before-demon, occurs once, in kappa. Kind 0x37 is matched to source
  119 times in kappa (ad hoc count over plaintext.py's matches, 2026-11-23).
- **The dynamic-predicate field.** It is 0 in every class but one,
  `tour##tt_belief_system`. There it is 19 pairs of (local offset, flag),
  and the source declares 19 `:- dynamic(P/N);`. What the flag means is
  UNKNOWN.

**Linearisation** (SOURCED: TM-388 §3.2 p.39). The order is depth-first
and the first occurrence wins. The class itself goes where `*` is, and
first if `*` is omitted. Loops are refused.

- Every held nature vector is fully qualified. The `.ptf` files contain
  no unqualified nature.
- One class writes `*` in the middle: `pimmcsp##csp_write_messages`
  (`_before, *, _after, ...`).
- A nature that is not loaded stays in the order as an opaque node, and
  the class is marked **partial**, because what that node inherits is
  unknown. Every unloaded nature in the held packages is a `simpos##`
  class.
- The implicit root, class `class` (TM-388 §3.6), is a flag on the
  class object and not a node in the order.

**Method combination** (SOURCED: TM-388 §3.4 pp.42-43). The before-demons
run in inheritance order. The primaries are tried as alternatives in
inheritance order. Then come the after-demons. Class methods and
instance methods combine separately, and locals are not inherited.

**Slots** (SOURCED: TM-388 §3.5). Attributes with the same name collapse
into one slot, and the first initial value in inheritance order wins.
Components stay distinct. OURS: slot numbers follow the order of first
appearance.

**Method and slot tables** (SOURCED in structure: Nakashima's thesis
App. A, Fig. A-1 p.241 and p.242).

- Each table has an index of 2^N entries, each a (base of synonyms,
  number of synonyms) pair, and a contents part in which a key's
  synonyms are contiguous.
- The hash is the key's low N bits.
- The method key is `k = n*2^24 + a + n`, and the slot key is the
  name's atom number.
- OURS: the table size (the smallest power of two that holds the keys),
  the atom numbers (interned in load order, 28,399 atoms), counting the
  receiver in `n` (the template's arity does), and the layout in words.
  With these choices the longest synonym chain is 6 (ad hoc probe over all class objects, 2026-11-23). The thesis reports
  1-2 for its own sizes, so its sizing rule is not ours.
- `Librarian.send` looks up a selector through the table.

**Method bodies** stay KL0. Each predicate keeps its cells as ptfdump
decodes them, for the KL0 interpreter of step 2.

## The after-demon order: a switch

`Librarian(after_order=...)`, or `--after` on the command line:

- **`'reverse'`, the default.** TM-388 p.42 says "what is first
  inherited is executed last". Its worked example on p.43 is
  `b1,b2,b3,(p1;p2;p3;fail),a3,a2,a1`. TR-044 p.20 agrees.
- **`'forward'`.** The thesis, p.243, prints `m :- b0..bn, p, a0..an`.

The TM-388 page images (PDF pp.54-55) were read for this.
`layers/ESP.md` §5 reported a conflict inside TM-388, but there is none:
the "in the order of the inheritance" sentence describes the AND
operation, and the next paragraph states the order explicitly. The
conflict is TM-388 and TR-044 against the thesis. In the held code it
matters for 11 combined methods, those with two or more after-demons (ad hoc count over `build_all()`, 2026-11-23).

### The "102 classes that need no SIMPOS" claim

The claim is in `../docs/simpos/README.md` §3 and `layers/FORMATS.md`
§2. Recomputed from the resolved graph over the same six packages
(554 classes):

| Edges followed | Free of SIMPOS |
|---|---|
| natures only (inheritance) | **338** (paragraph 105, pimmcsp 185, pimfep 12, pimmutil 13, mpsicsp 13, pimscsi 10) |
| natures + the trailer's referenced-classes vector | **104** (paragraph 56, pimmcsp 34, mpsicsp 7, pimmutil 4, pimscsi 2, pimfep 1) |

**The claim of 102 holds approximately, not exactly.** The comparable
measure is the second row, and it gives 104. The first row answers a
different question.

Where the old probe (`deps.py`) differs:

- **It is not deterministic.** Its loop memo depends on Python's set
  order. Rerun five times today, it gave 103, 102, 102, 102 and 102.
- **It treats slot names as class references.** It counts any trailer
  atom that equals a local class name. In `pimmcsp##dummy_kl1_value_cell`
  the slot `network_output_handler` is such an atom, so the probe calls
  that class dependent. That class is the only difference between its
  103 and our 104.
- **Its "unqualified natures" are not natures.** The two pimmcsp
  classes it reported (`kl1_remote_abortion_device`,
  `as_physical_device`, with `my_node_manager`/`node_manager`) were read
  from the atoms after the slot value `self`. Those atoms are slot names.

Over all eleven packages: 1,051 classes are free by natures and 369 by
natures plus references.

### Known plaintext (`plaintext.py`)

| Program | Templates with source | All aspects agree | Nature lists | Slots | Methods | Demons | Locals |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| paragraph | 109 of 121 | 107 | 109/109 | 607/608 | 557/558 | 5/5 | 712/712 |
| kappa2 | 739 of 797 | 734 | 739/739 | 2,957/2,957 | 5,539/5,693 | 206/206 | 7,562/7,570 |
| utter | 69 of 104 | 67 | 69/69 | 109/109 | 382/382 | 3/3 | 874/878 |

**Why "with source" is 739 for kappa.** Of kappa's 797 templates, 739
match an ESP class by name. 55 more are macro banks whose source is
held: their natures include `simpos##esp_macro_expander_for_compiler`,
and `plaintext.py` does not compare macro banks. The other 3 have no
source.

The earlier figures count differently:

- `required/NOTES.md`'s 794 is 739 + 55.
- The 696 in `archive_search_notes.md` is a case-folded file-level
  count.

utter has 69 matched templates, 6 macro banks with source, 3 more
classes with the macro-expander nature, and 26 with no source.

Five kappa classes and one paragraph class are defined more than once
in the source. Each is compared with its closest definition, which
biases those six toward agreement.

The 168 disagreements, each listed in `plaintext_disagreements.tsv`.
Their causes were **assigned by rule (INFERENCE), not checked row by
row**. A method or local row in a class that uses a macro bank is
attributed to the bank. A row in a class with an unparsed source item
is attributed to the parser.

- **159 in classes that use a macro bank.** The bank's generated
  clauses are not modelled. 153 of these are in two kappa
  `kappaRITR_*_interface` classes.
- **8 in classes where `esplex` failed to parse a source item**:
  `h_grade`'s `draw1/1`, and 7 locals in two kappa classes.
- **1 from the version skew**: `paragraph_version` has `"3200"` in the
  source and `"3500"` in the object.

No row falls outside these rules, and the test pins that. The rules
are coarse, though: a real disagreement inside a macro-bank class would
be attributed to the bank too.
Template names in two-byte JIS are decoded before comparison, and both
sides are NFKC-normalised.

## The KL0 interpreter (step 2, 2026-11-24)

`kl0.py` runs the method bodies of the loaded classes. It is OURS: the
PSI's KL0 firmware is lost, and nothing here emulates it. It is a
WAM-shaped machine (`../docs/simpos/layers/KL0.md` §3) whose
instruction set is exactly the rows of `kl0_opcodes.tsv`.

**Opcodes are named only from known plaintext.** Each of the 132 rows
(100 at step 2, nine more at step 3c, four at step 3d, twelve at step 3e,
seven at step 3g)
cites a predicate whose compiled cells sit next to the held ESP clause
they came from (`pkg##class pred/arity @cell`, source file, clause).
An opcode without a row is refused loudly (`Refusal`, cause
`opcode`). So is an operand pattern the witnesses never showed, such
as a 16-bit immediate with its top bit set, or a three-register
built-in whose result register is not its first operand (where no
witness shows it). The rows are
77 class-0 (`pmi`), 44 class-1 (`blt`) built-ins, 3 atom-cell forms
and 1 hash table (`cd00`). A few class-0 rows are opcodes whose
difference from a sibling is UNKNOWN (e.g. `24` beside `20`, `de`
beside `dd`). They execute alike, and their rows say so.

`fepread/witness.py`, written separately for `docs/fep_services.md`
§6, witnesses 40 readings. Where its rows and these overlap, they
agree, and 44 rows here cite it as an independent witness. Five rows
come from it: `pmi 37` (get_vector of n), and blt `25` (class object),
`62`/`64` (==) and `65` (\==). Its count of hash tables (0 misses over
142) agrees with the cell numbering above. Its finding that a `0308`
string's count is in bytes, not words, is now applied in
`librarian._term`.

**The string rows** (2026-11-24): blt `0b` string/3 (S, ^L, ^T),
`1c` new_string/3 (^S, L, T), `23` string_element/3 (S, I, ^E) and `2b`
set_string_element/3 (S, I, E), from paragraph's `time_graph_window`,
`g_dev`, `history_manipulator` and `view_select_menu`. new_string's
witnesses include S != T (`view_select_menu change/3`: `1c 04 00 01`,
the string out in A4), which settles which operand is the output. The
elements of a new string are 0 and their type is what T says (OURS:
neither is witnessed beyond the type). kl0's string object keeps its
type. In the six-package census, two methods get further:
`csp_default_write write/2` stops at `string/3` on an unbound argument,
and `state_pe reflesh/3` at a `draw_string/6` sent to an unbound
receiver.

**`:refute(Object, Method, Args)`** (TM-388 p.46, PDF p.58: class
`class`'s dynamic call) is modelled (OURS): the vector's elements become
the arguments after the receiver, and the method is sent to Object. It
is the second class-`class` method kl0 runs, after `new/2`.

MEASURED facts the witnesses settled:

- A predicate finds its entry cut barrier in register A_arity.
  kappa##kappaDBC_interface `pic/1` keeps A1 with `20 01 00`, cuts to
  it with `dd 01 01` and, after a call, reloads it from Y0 and cuts
  with `de 01 01`. Over the eleven packages:
  - in 1,464 of 1,464 `dd A n`, A is the predicate's arity and n the
    preceding allocate's size;
  - in 4,255 of 4,255 `de A n`, n is that size, and A is the arity in
    4,254.
- `blt 66 A B` succeeds if A_A < A_B, and `blt 67 A B` if A_A >= A_B.
  So `Y =< H/2` compiles to `67 H Y`.
- A hash table's entry target is the stored offset + 1.

PRINTED (since 2026-11-26): a disjunction `$or$N` (header kind 0x12) is
entered with its own barrier, so its `!` (d0) cuts only the
disjunction's alternatives, not the clause's. TM-388 §4.6 p.111
(Example 2) and App. G pp.161-162 say so ("differently from ! of
Prolog"). Held code relies on it: kappa2 stwin.esp `get_number/3`
answers `Number = abort` after an inner `!, fail`, and pb/hash.esp
`search_and_get/5` writes `!, relative_cut_and_fail(1)`, which would be
plain `fail` if `!` cut the clause. Until then kl0 kept the caller's
barrier (OURS: `pic/1`'s `$or$3/1`, `!` as d0 with A1 overwritten, fits
both readings). `test_espc.py` NestedDisjunction discriminates it.

**Method dispatch** goes through `Librarian.send` and runs TM-388
§3.4's combined method: before-demons, then the primaries as
alternatives, then after-demons. Class `class`'s `new/2` is OURS. It
builds an instance whose slots take their constant initial values. The
loader's `Slot.term` keeps a constant's pool tag, so a string initial
value stays a string (`paragraph_version` answers `string#"3500"`).

**Slots initialised by code** (2026-11-24). `new/2` runs them.

- The calling convention is MEASURED. All 424 slot-initialisation
  predicates of the eleven packages (kind 0x22: 33, kind 0x32: 391)
  have arity 1 and no receiver argument, and the value comes back in
  A0. The witness is `paragraph##time_graph_window display_pos/1`,
  compiled from p_tgraph.esp `(display_pos:=L :- :create(#list_index,L))`:
  `a9 01 ..` puts the class into A1, then `blt 25` and `pmi 74 01 02`
  send `create/2` with L still in A0. `espc.py` compiles this
  predicate cell for cell.
- The semantics are SOURCED from TM-388 §3.5, pp.49-50 (PDF pp.61-62,
  page images read).
  - An instance slot's value is computed when `new` makes the instance.
    A class slot's value is computed "when the class is generated".
  - If the predicate fails, `new` fails, or the class is not generated.
  - The order "is uncertain".
  - The predicate cannot use the object being made.
  - A slot may not hold an unbound variable (p.48).
  - `slot is Class` stores "an instance object for the class" (p.49,
    (3)). kl0 used to store the class object. It now makes the instance
    with `new/2`. Only kappa has such slots (102).
- OURS choices:
  - Each predicate runs on its own sub-machine, to its first solution.
    The sub-machine shares the code and the class slots.
  - The slots are filled in template order (the held templates list them
    in reverse source order).
  - Class slots are computed at the class's first class-slot access,
    standing in for load time.
  - An unbound value refuses (`slot init unbound`). A failing class-slot
    predicate refuses (`class not generated`).
- Tests are in `test_kl0.py` on kappa alone, against its source.
  - `kappaMDM_hash_table`'s `new_heap_vector(Table,128)` gives 128
    zeros.
  - `kappaMDM_access_right` makes two distinct hash tables, each with its
    own vector (a nested initialiser), and `faot` is
    `@max_faot_entry+1` = 21 long.
  - `kappaTBO_SDB_RUC_control`'s two-goal initialiser gives
    `{1,0,...}` of 21.
  - `kappaHLC_selection`'s `is` slots are instances, one with its own
    `is` slot.
  - `test_espc.py` checks the failure, unbound, class-slot and
    after-demon cases on compiled classes.
- No new opcode was needed. Of the 424 held predicates, 308 use only
  opcodes in the table. The other 116 use opcodes with no row: blt 1c
  (84), blt 3e (31), blt 35 (2), blt 54 and blt 2b (1 each). This is an
  ad hoc count, 2026-11-24.

**Results** (pinned in `test_kl0.py`):

- `pimfep##fep_command_code_table:convert/3`, run both ways, against
  the 104 `fep#name => code` macros of PIMOS's `pimos.vmac`:
  - 96 names agree, and none disagrees.
  - 4 names are only in the FEP table: `creation_fail` 900,
    `all_device_end` 901, `remote_echo_check` 902, `device_end` 903.
  - 8 names are only in PIMOS: `window`, `listing`, `deleted`,
    `next_filename`, `symbol`, `remote_socket`, `packet_last`,
    `packet_continued`.
  - Every clause ends in a cut. So code -> name answers one name per
    code, and 5 names lose to an earlier clause with the same code:
    `attention` (3, `control_c`), `euc` (1, `normal`), `jis`,
    `jiskanji`, `sjis`.
  - `convert(_,_)` has no solution.
- The paragraph classes against their held source:
  - `predicate_num_switch_icon` `get_code/4` gives `inc_predicate_num`
    up to Y0 = y + height/2 and `dec_predicate_num` past it
    (p_itool.esp, p_icon.esp).
  - A `list_index_buffer` goes through create, clear, add_last,
    add_first, count, get_contents(_with_key), search, search_num,
    remove (once found, then failing), get_partial_contents and
    remove_num. Every answer is the one p_buf.esp predicts.
  - `paragraph_version` gives "3500" where version.esp says "3200".
    This is the known version skew, not a fault.
  - `scroll_bottom_icon` `code/4` has two primaries, its own and
    `predicate_bottom_icon`'s. They answer as alternatives:
    `scroll_bottom`, then `bottom_predicate` (p_icon2.esp).
  - The 104 classes have no before- or after-demons, so demons are run
    but untested on held code. 14 of their methods have two primaries.
  - `time_graph_window:go/1` refuses at its send of `:create/3`,
    naming the class and its `simpos##` ancestors.
- The census. Every method of the 104 classes that need no SIMPOS (103
  have methods) is called once with unbound arguments. The receiver is
  the class, or a fresh instance. Of 530 methods, 303 succeed, 11 fail
  cleanly and 216 are refused. The refusals by cause:

  | Cause | Methods |
  |---|---|
  | no method (not in the receiver's class: a mixin's call left to the class it joins, or class `class`) | 55 |
  | no slot | 30 |
  | built-in on a bound argument of the wrong type | 24 |
  | unbound argument to a built-in | 17 |
  | unwitnessed operand | 17 |
  | unbound receiver | 13 |
  | receiver not an object (e.g. `send get_pes/2 to 0`) | 8 |
  | unnamed opcodes | 52 |

  The 52 come from 13 opcodes. The most common are blt e1 (14), pmi c8
  (8), pmi 6c (5) and pmi ca (5).
  - Most of the first five causes come from the census's own inputs
    (unbound arguments, a fresh instance of a mixin), not from the
    code.
  - No refusal is a `simpos##` call: these classes need no SIMPOS by
    construction.

## The ESP compiler (step 3, 2026-11-24)

`espc.py` is OURS; ICOT's compiler is lost. It parses with the census
reader (`../asm/simpos_census/esplex.py`) and emits a `Template`, in the
form the loader builds, carrying its own constant pool. The code-shape
rules are in its docstring: registers, permanent variables, clause
chains and cut operands, each measured on the held code.

**What it may emit.** Only opcodes with a row in `kl0_opcodes.tsv`.
Anything else is refused as `CompileRefusal(construct, detail)`, and a
predicate that calls a refused local is refused with it. The largest
refusals over paragraph are: calls a refused local (215), `X+Y` on two
registers (157), `heap_vector/2` (101), `X/Y` (41), the
`double_bytes#` macro (37), a head term (35) and `=\=` (34). Over kappa2,
all 739 classes with source compile now that `with_macro` is expanded
(below); before, 404 were refused whole for it.

**Macro banks (`with_macro`, TM-388 §3.7.3 pp.72-75, page images).** A
class `with_macro B` has its clauses rewritten by bank B's clause-level
rules, `Pattern => Expansion`, before compilation: head arguments, body
goals and their arguments, top-down, the result expanded again. A bank
inherits its `nature` banks' rules; its own are tried first
(INFERENCE: TM-388 states the inheritance, not the order).
`esp_macro_expander`, which every bank inherits, is SIMPOS and not
held; its standard macros are the compiler's own. Not modelled, and
refused by name as `macro rule` for the predicate that meets one: a
rule with a `when`/`where`/`with`/`by`/`:-` tail (15 in kappa2's
banks), `==>` (one), and a backquote. Measured over kappa2's banks:
about 3,811 rules, nearly all `@name => constant`. A bank whose source
is not held refuses the class as `with_macro` (utter: `cil_macro`,
`travel_fts_macro`, 10 classes). The structural witness: of kappa2's
1,323 exact predicates (measured when `with_macro` landed, before the
string built-ins), 822 are in the 406 classes with a bank, and 109
have source clauses a bank rule changed, so the expansion meets
`kappa.ptf` cell for cell.

**Structure** (`espc.py check`, against the held `.ptf`). Each class is
checked for natures, slots with their flags, the declared method and
local sets (refused ones included) and the demon set. Each predicate
pairs compiled cells with held cells in a position-independent form.
A disjunction is paired by where it is called from (`parent#k`), not by
its `$or$N`. The held numbering does not follow source order: in
`paragraph##receiver`, `send/7`'s disjunctions are numbered before
`draw_graph/6`'s, although the source has them after.

| | paragraph | kappa2 | utter |
|---|---|---|---|
| classes with source / compiled | 109 / 109 | 739 / 739 | 69 / 59 |
| natures, slots, methods, demons, locals agree | 109, 108, 108, 109, 109 | 738, 738, 736, 739, 736 | 59, 59, 59, 59, 58 |
| predicates compared | 1,051 | 10,641 | 521 |
| exact | 247 (23.5%) | 1,361 (12.8%) | 101 (19.4%) |
| registers only | 36 | 171 | 13 |
| moves and environment only | 196 | 2,651 | 107 |
| clause order | 73 | 313 | 9 |
| held indexes (switch tables), and other | 114 | 701 | 19 |
| held uses an opcode with no row | 245 | 3,828 | 193 |
| other (unclassified) | 140 | 1,616 | 79 |
| exact / all held predicates of the classes with source | 11.6% of 2,123 | 6.5% of 21,027 | 6.9% of 1,454 |

(Before `with_macro`, 2026-11-24: kappa2 335 classes compiled, 3,387
compared, 501 exact; utter 52, 464, 94. With `with_macro`, before the
string built-ins below: paragraph 991, 243; kappa2 9,971, 1,323; utter
515, 101.)

**The string built-ins** (2026-11-24): `string/3`, `new_string/3`,
`string_element/3` and `set_string_element/3` now have rows (below)
and are emitted. `new_string(S,L,T)` is emitted with S and T in one
register, the form of `make_num_string/3`. Adding them exposed an
older bug, now fixed: a permanent head variable met after a structured
head argument had already allocated the environment was never saved
(e.g. `total([X|R],L0,L)`). The fix alone moves paragraph from 246 to
247 exact.

There are no key collisions and no `$or$` paired with another arity in
paragraph or utter. Kappa2 has 14 pairs with another arity. The two
paragraph aspect misses are `paragraph_version`'s version slot
("3200" in the source, "3500" in the `.ptf`) and `h_grade`'s methods
(two items of `h_grade_n.esp` do not parse). The "other" class was
sampled by hand. In `receiver set_pred_pool/2`, the held code matches
a head vector element by element and cuts through a register. The
compiled code does neither.

**Behaviour** (`espc.py behave`). The 109 compiled paragraph classes
are loaded alone into a library, so a call that reached any class not
compiled here would refuse. Every call `test_kl0.py` makes gives the
same answer as `paragraph.ptf` alone:

- `predicate_num_switch_icon` gives inc/dec at 210/228/229/240;
- the `list_index_buffer` battery agrees;
- `scroll_bottom_icon`'s two primaries agree;
- `paragraph_version` gives "3200" (source) against "3500" (`.ptf`).

kl0's census of `paragraph.ptf` was rerun on the compiled classes with
the same calls. 88 methods have the same outcome and 87 are refused on
both sides. 46 are refused only on the compiled side: 35 for
`double_bytes#`, 5 for a refused local, 5 inherited by
`wshell_buffer` from `array_index_buffer`, where they are refused,
and 1 in a class not compiled (`rmonitor_version`, no source). 6 are refused only on the held side, at the unnamed opcodes
pmi ca and 36, so their equivalence is unchecked. No method runs to a
different outcome.

**Stand-ins** (`standin/`, scaffolding). `standins()` compiles them
into package `simpos` and flags each template. `kl0.py` then refuses
a method a stand-in lacks as a SIMPOS call, not as "no method". That
flag check is the second `kl0.py` change for this step. The first is
that `Code` takes a compiled template's own pool. `load_six()` does
not load the stand-ins, so `test_kl0.py`'s pins are unchanged.

Each stand-in method is labelled in its source as PRINTED (verbatim,
with the figure and page) or INTERPOLATED (ours, with what it was
interpolated from: the interface, the call sites in held programs,
the manuals). This is the owner's rule of 2026-11-24, so that any
method can be replaced if real source turns up. `test_espc.py` checks
that every method carries a label.

- `process`:
  - PRINTED: TM-100 Fig. 3.1, verbatim. That is the natures, the two
    class slots, `create/2`, `create_core/3`, the 18 instance slots
    (five of them initialised by code), `activate/2`, `resume/2` and
    `suspend/2`.
  - The printed `create/2` passes `PROCESS` in capitals, as printed. It
    is a fresh variable, and it is kept that way.
  - INTERPOLATED: the class slot `current`, and `self/2`. `:self(
    #process,P)` gives one instance, made with `:new` on first use and
    kept in `current`. It was interpolated from the call sites in the
    printed `stream:get/2` and `process:create_core/3`, and from 46
    pimfep senders.
  - INTERPOLATED: `inhibit_interrupt/1` and `allow_interrupt/1`, which do
    nothing. One process is modelled, with no interrupts. They come from
    the paired call sites in Figs. 3.1 and 3.6 and from the slot
    `mask_count`.
- `stream`: every method and slot is PRINTED, from TM-100 Fig. 3.6.
  Three printing errors are corrected and marked, since without the
  corrections it is not ESP.
- The classes the printed code creates or inherits are INTERPOLATED:
  - `list`: 13 methods, from the call forms in TM-100, TM-388 p.50 and
    the selectors in `required/classes.tsv`. Its elements are one
    Prolog list (OURS).
  - `post_queue` (`add_object`, `remove_object`) and `waiting_queue`
    (`empty`, `hook`, `unhook`), from TM-100 §3.2 (1)'s prose (PDF
    p13-14) and the printed `stream`. Both are first in, first out, as
    TR-056 pp.12-13 says a stream is. The waiting queue is a list, not
    the printed doubly-linked list through `waiting_frame`.
  - `process_clock` (`create/2`).
  - `with_link` and `waiting_frame`: empty. TM-100 says only what they
    are for.
- `process`'s `tag_catch/5`, `/6` and `/9` are INTERPOLATED: each sends
  the method to the object through `:refute` (above) and catches
  nothing. The call forms come from kappa2 `inwin.esp:301` (/5), cal-esp
  `cm.esp:320` (/6) and pimfep's `on_after/4` registers (/9: object,
  method, arguments, tag, then a second object, method, arguments and
  tag for the abort path, then `pimos_abort`). TR-285 §4.2 (PDF p9-10)
  says catch/throw is multiple-level cut plus enforced failure; with no
  `throw` modelled, catching is the plain call.
- `string` (INTERPOLATED): `append/3` (a list of strings in, a new
  string out; 1,039 call sites in 14 programs) and `copy/3` (7), on the
  four string built-ins. Lengths are counted one by one, since `X+Y` on
  two registers has no witness.
- `symbolizer` (INTERPOLATED, PARTIAL): `string_to_list/3` and
  `list_to_string/3`, on the four string built-ins; their argument
  order (`List` before the string) comes from the call sites (kappa2
  `kap_serv.esp:677/681`, `motif.esp`, `retrieve2.esp`, `SRVCNF.ESP`;
  utter `tour_sit.esp:61`). The other 25 selectors are not stood in:
  most convert atoms or numbers and strings (`enter_atom/3`,
  `get_atom/3`, `get_number/3`, ...), and no witnessed built-in makes
  an atom from a string or back, while number conversion needs `X/Y`
  and a remainder, which espc refuses. The rest (`in/2`,
  `is_object/2`, the variable-tree methods) have no source or
  specification held. No pimfep or pimscsi method reaches
  `symbolizer` first in the census.

**Step 3c stand-ins** (2026-11-24), for the next blockers of the
census below:

- `channel` (PRINTED, TM-100 Fig. 3.7, verbatim): nature `stream`,
  the code-initialised slot `post_queue` that overrides stream's, and
  `send/3`, `receive/3`. Its `create/2` is the printed `stream`'s,
  inherited. Not stood in: TR-056 p14's `send/2`, `receive/2`, and class
  `message`'s `set_sender/2`, `get_sender/2`, which the printed bodies
  call.
- `bounded_channel` and `bounded_post_queue` (PRINTED, Figs. 3.10 and
  3.11, verbatim; no held code sends to them). The plain `attribute
  post_queue;` overrides channel's initialiser; `bounded_post_queue`'s
  before- and after-demons run on stand-ins (a third `put/2` on a channel
  bounded at 2 fails until a `get/2`). With `post_queue_for_message`
  (INTERPOLATED: nature `post_queue`, from TM-100 p15's "must be
  compatible") and `semaphore` (INTERPOLATED: nature `waiting_queue`
  from Fig. 3.9; tokens from TR-056 p11; `p_operation/1` fails at none,
  since one process could not be woken).
- `list_index` and `hash_index` (INTERPOLATED). Their representation is
  evidenced: kappa2's `kernel/flm/KPPOOL.ESP` (`kappa_list_index`, nature
  `list_index`) and `kernel/svm/SVHSH_IDX.ESP` (`kappaSVM_hash_index`,
  nature `hash_index`) read the base classes' slots directly. A
  list_index holds `first`, `'$$void'` or an entry `{Next, Object, Key}`;
  a hash_index holds `size` and `vector`, a vector of `size`
  list_indexes. The interface is TR-056 p8's `(3) Index` (object before
  key). `get_contents_with_key/2` gives `{Object, Key}`, as
  SVHSH_IDX.ESP builds and paragraph's `p_wshell.esp` reads. OURS: new
  entries go last, keys match by unification, and every key goes to
  bucket 0 (kappa.ptf's `get_list_index/4` compiles `hash/2` and `mod` to
  blt 6b and blt 44, but the firmware hash is lost).
- `process` (INTERPOLATED additions): `create/3` (a dormant child of
  the calling process; the four-atom area list, e.g.
  `[free,default,free,small]`, is ignored), from kappa2's `ITRSES.ESP`,
  `LOADER.ESP` and pimfep's `as_pimos_device process/4`, and the printed
  `create_core/3`; `allow_forced_event/1` and `inhibit_forced_event/1`
  (no-ops: one process, no abort); the accessors `sub_priority/2`,
  `user/2`, `world/2`, `password_list/2` and their setters, read off
  `as_pimos_device`'s `setup_process/3` (`password_list` is not a
  printed slot).
- `standard_editor` (INTERPOLATED, minimal): `pimos_window_editor`'s
  natures are `simpos##standard_editor, pimfep##as_pimos_device`, so
  `get_window/2` is standard_editor's (window.md §2.11). It gives a
  `window` slot that stays 0, since `pmacs_window` is not stood in: the 21
  methods now refuse at their next send, to 0.
- **An espc bug fixed on the way.** In a call, an argument computed into
  a register in step 1 was read from that register after a later
  argument's move had cleared it, so `fill(I+1, N, V)` passed N for
  I+1. `clear_way` now updates the placed argument. `espc.py check` of
  paragraph, kappa2 and utter is the same with or without the fix, so
  no pin moved; the hash_index stand-in's `fill/3` found it.

With them loaded:

- `pimfep##pimos_timer:on_after/4` gets past `simpos##process:self/2`
  and, through `tag_catch/9` and `:refute`, into the timer's own
  `do_on_after/3`; it refuses at
  `simpos##process:allow_forced_event/1`.
- `pimos_timer:stream/2`, which used to stop in kl0's `new/2` at the
  printed `post_queue` slot, now succeeds.
- The printed `put/2` and `get/2` run first in, first out
  (`test_espc.py`). `get/2` on an empty stream fails. The printed
  `suspend/2` fails for a process whose status is `nil`, and the
  stand-in's one process is never activated.

the tree at a214477a; after is the slot initialisation and the
stand-ins above. Both were run 2026-11-24 and compared method by
method.

### Step 3c's opcode rows and HELD-SOURCE classes (2026-11-24)

- **Nine rows** (`kl0_opcodes.tsv`). Each comes from a single-construct
  held predicate whose only unrowed opcode is the one named, found by a
  miner over paragraph, kappa2 and utter:
  - blt 40 `add`;
  - blt 01 `integer`, blt 10 `unbound`, blt 16 `bound`: tests that fail;
  - blt 06 `stack_vector`;
  - blt 68 `equal_string`;
  - blt 33 `string_tail`;
  - blt 32 `substring` and blt 5c `search_character`. These two are two
    cells long: the fourth and fifth operands are the high bytes of the
    `integer` cell that follows.
- **Effect on `espc.py check`.**

  | Program | Compared / exact | Before |
  |---|---|---|
  | paragraph | 1,234 / 256 | 1,051 / 247 |

  Compared method by method, no exact predicate was lost.

    same known weakness as the PCM pair, and it is there with the fixes

  - kl0's census of the six held packages (530 methods) keeps 303 / 11
    / 216.
- **Two more espc register bugs**, found by `substring(S,H,T-H,P)` and
  `(S+1)-S`. `builtin()` and `arith()` marked a variable's register as a
  value while later operands were put, so a later operand reading the
  same variable raised KeyError. A variable's register now stays the
  variable's; its uncounted uses keep it live.
- **pmi b0**: this step reported no witness, naming only two held
  predicates with source. That was wrong: 12 kappa predicates use it, and
  `typewin.esp:259` and `PCAACCA.ESP:93` settle it as `{}` (step 3d,
  below).
- **HELD-SOURCE stand-ins.** `standin/name.esp`, `e_string.esp` and
  `pathname.esp` are morphana's `_simpos/` files: ICOT Free Software,
  (C)1992 ICOT, `ifs/natural-lang/cesp/morphana.tar.Z`.
  - Their stamps name SIMPOS's `world` directory, so they are SIMPOS's
    own classes as copied for Common ESP.
  - They are byte for byte as held except for:
    - the character set (EUC-JP to UTF-8);
    - one `% HELD-SOURCE: file:line` label above each method;
    - in `pathname`, the CESP port's changes undone where SIMPOS's form
      is evidenced, each marked `% [SIMPOS form restored N]`: the `/`
      separator back to `>`, and three commented-out clauses (logical
      names, `]`, Patch 6.13).
  - Kept and labelled `% [CESP, kept]`: `use_package(lax).` and
    `append/4`'s `convert_to_8(#cesp##string, ...)`. Its SIMPOS form is
    not evidenced, and kl0 refuses the send.
  - What runs:
    - `name`'s `ident/3`, `type/3`, `version/3` and `ident_type/3`;
    - `e_string`'s `split/4`.
  - What does not:
    - `pathname`'s `get_last/4` refuses. It searches backwards (B > E),
      which no witness shows.
    - `set_names/3` (`set_substring/4`) is refused, and pinned. The
      `qsort`/`partition` pair (`->`) compiles since 2026-11-26
      (section "If-then-else").
- **Symbolizer** (INTERPOLATED). `get_number/3` (number out, string in)
  and `get_number_string/3` (number in, string out) work on integers,
  in decimal with a leading `-`, from their call sites (cil
  `ms_cdn.esp:613`, morphana `name.esp:138`, ...).
  - A `.`, or a number that is not an integer, sends `float_number/3`,

  - The atom methods (`enter_atom/3`, `get_atom/3`, ...) stay unstood:
    cil's KL0 list (`cil/src/cil_kl0.esp`) has only `new_atom/1` for
    atoms.

### Step 3d's opcode rows (2026-11-24)

The step 3c blockers blt 6e, pmi b0 and pmi b4 all have witnesses in
kappa2, paragraph and utter. They were found by listing every held use
of each opcode (43, 85 and 346 uses) and reading the source of the
predicates in packages with source. Four rows were added, and three
rows gained a witnessed operand pattern:

- **blt 6e `vector_to_list(V, Tail, ^L)`.**
  - Witness: utter `dict_io.esp:53-56` (`sancho_dict get_value/4`,
    `6e 00 03 00` after `put r3, []`).
  - The operand roles are settled by kappa2 `BCMSEQUEN.ESP:432`
    (`devide_value/5`, `6e 03 00 00`: the vector in X3, the list out in
    A0).
  - TR-044 App A.8 (p.A-4) lists the 1984 two-argument form
    `vector_to_list(Vector, ^List)`. The held code's middle argument is
    the tail. That is INFERENCE: every call site passes `[]` or `_`.
  - Only `[]` is run. Another tail, and a heap vector, refuse.
  - The pimfep use, `fep_message_assembler @50`, passes `[]`.
- **blt 34 `set_subvector(V, P, L; S)`.** Two cells long, as substring.
  - Witness: `p_tgraph.esp:299` `set_subvector(Hv,0,4,{X,Y,W,H})`.
  - The argument order is TR-044 App A.4 (p.A-3).
- **pmi b0: put `{}`.** Witnesses: `typewin.esp:259`
  `:refute(PBEDIT,Item,{})` (`b0 01`) and `PCAACCA.ESP:93`
  `set_status(...,{})` (`b0 03`). Two more kappa uses agree.
  - sancho5's `b0 02` sits beside a `bind_hook/2` (blt 8a, no row) with
    no `{}` in its source. It neither confirms nor contradicts.
  - espc now puts `{}` with b0, not as a pool constant (OURS: only in a
    body put position).
- **pmi b4: put a four-element vector.** Witness: the same
  `p_tgraph.esp:299` goal, `b4 03` and four unify_value_p. espc now
  builds four-element vectors.
- **Wider operand patterns on old rows:**
  - blt 45 add_immediate with R != A: `bar_v.esp:384`, `45 00 01 06`.
  - blt 46 subtract_immediate with R != A: `as_pred.esp:254-256`,
    `46 04 0b 00`.
  - pmi a1 put_variable_t with A != X: `p_tgraph.esp:70-72`, `a1 00 02`.
    The one new variable goes into both registers.
- **Not extended by analogy.** pmi b1, b5, b6 and b7 have no row; each
  needs its own witness. Neither does `list_to_vector` (blt 6f).
- **Effect on `espc.py check`** (compared predicate by predicate, no
  exact predicate lost):

  | Program | Compared / exact | Before |
  |---|---|---|
  | paragraph | 1,241 / 258 | 1,234 / 256 |

  | utter | 568 / 110 | 559 / 109 |

  - In `espc.py behave`, `array_index_buffer` and `wshell_buffer`
    `put_contents_with_key/2` now compile and agree with the held code.
  - The six-package census keeps 303 / 11 / 216. Six methods stop
    later: five at divide_immediate with R != A, still unwitnessed.
- **Effect on the FEP census.** The totals stay 607 / 101 / 521, but
  93 refused methods get further:

    string built-ins. They stop there on the census's own unbound

### Step 3e: pmi ca, the first-clause guard (2026-11-24)

pmi ca was the first missing opcode of the FEP census (23 methods). Its
row is witnessed in paragraph, with source:

- `h draw_string/6` @122, `ca 00 00 05`, atom `nil`, then a try chain
  (h.esp:189-190 `draw_string(nil,...):-!; draw_string(S,...):- ...`);
- `color_grade_example set_pattern/4`, `ca 00 00 01`, integer 256, then
  `get_integer`, `proceed` and no try chain (c_grade_ex.esp:125-126
  `set_pattern(256,_,_,_):-!; set_pattern(N,S,P1,P2):- ...`);
- `what_manipulator_shell show/2` (p_wshell.esp:370-374, `[]` against a
  list).

The reading: if A0 is bound and not the constant, jump to cell + 3 +
s16, which is the second clause; if it is the constant, or unbound, fall
through into the first clause. The unbound case is INFERENCE from the
source: both witnesses must bind an unbound first argument to the
constant, and a jump would skip that. MEASURED over the eleven packages
(a scratch survey, 2026-11-24): 1,025 uses, all with R = 0. The target
is always the cell after the first clause's last instruction. kl0 runs
only R = 0 and refuses any other register.

Tests (`test_kl0.py` `test_if_not_constant`): the handler on its
operands; `array_index_buffer set_all_at/2`, through `set_all/5`, the
form with no try chain (p_buf4.esp:98-102), sets one element of every
row; `h draw_string/6` takes clause 1 for `nil` and for an unbound
argument, and clause 2 for a string.

Effect, compared method by method:

- Six-package census: 303 / 11 / 216 becomes 307 / 12 / 211.
  `array_index_buffer` and `wshell_buffer` `set_all_at/2` and
  `reset_all_at/2` succeed. `csp_fep_main_program get/4` fails.
- `espc.py behave`: those four methods now agree with the compiled code
  (same outcome 94 -> 98). `espc.py check` is unchanged: espc does not
  emit ca.
- FEP census: 1,265 methods, 665 / 113 / 487 becomes 666 / 113 / 486.
  - `remote_pimfep_executor check_status/3` succeeds.
  - 22 refused methods stop later: 10 at "receiver not an object" (8
    in the `scsi_communicator` family, `cmt` and `scsi`, which send to a
    0 slot), 3 at blt 51, 3 at blt 3e, 2 at blt 50, 2 at `string/3` on
    0, 1 at an unbound receiver, and `pimos_window
    create_pimos_window/3` at the slot `activity` of the window, which
    is not in the `pmacs_window` stand-in.
  - No method moved out of success.

### Step 3e: blt f2, `==` as a test-and-branch (2026-11-24)

blt f2 (10 FEP methods) has 3,067 held uses. It is witnessed in
paragraph by `g_window.esp:15-17` and `:24-27`, two disjunctions of the
form `( X == c, !, Then ; Else )`:

- `$or$0/3`: put `color`, `f2 02 03 00`, then the execute of
  `create_pairs`. The jump lands on the `true` branch's `f0`.
- `$or$1/3`: put `act`, `f2 02 03 02`. The jump lands on the else
  branch's allocate.

In the same file, `Obj!color = color,!` (`=`) compiles to get_atom and a
cut, so f2 is `==`. It reads exactly as blt f0, `if_identical`: equal
goes on, anything else jumps to cell + 2 + c. How f2 differs from f0 is
UNKNOWN. Both usually follow a constant put into B, and they execute
alike, as 62 and 64 do. MEASURED (a scratch survey of all 3,067 uses):

- A != B in every use;
- the cell before the target is a proceed, an execute, a `fail` or a
  send's selector in all but about 130 uses, which are data cells that
  were not examined.

Test: `test_kl0.py` `test_if_identical_f2` runs both disjunctions on the
held code, for an equal and for an unequal value.

Effect on the FEP census (compared method by method): 666 / 113 / 486
becomes 667 / 120 / 478.

- 2 methods move from refused to success: `pimos_window_editor
  abort_getl/1` and `cmt show_status/1`.
- 6 move from refused to failure: the socket table's `add_at/3`,
  `get_at/3` and `remove_at/3` on an empty table, `scsi_device close/1`
  and `polling/1`, and `scsi_load_test create/4`.
- 2 stop later: `net_spawn_user spawn/7,8` at
  `network_node_name:get_my_node/2`.
- 1 moves from success to failure: `pimos_remote_login_socket_table
  locked/1`. This is a census order effect.
  - Before, `add_at/3` ran first and refused at f2 with the table still
    locked, so `locked/1` then succeeded.
  - Now `add_at/3` fails, its `:unlock` alternative runs, and `locked/1`
    fails.
  - On a fresh machine, `locked/1` fails with or without f2, as the
    other tables' `locked/1` do.

### Step 3e: `as_program`, PRINTED, and the program stack (2026-11-24)

`simpos##as_program` was the largest single SIMPOS blocker. Grouped by
ancestor, 30 FEP refusals were sends that fell to it: `create/2` 19,
`exit/1` 5, `class_object/2` 3, and others. A body is printed: TM-100
Figure 3.3, "Part of class 'as_program'" (PDF p11, transcribed in
`docs/simpos/printed/listings/tm0100_p11.esp`).

- **`standin/as_program.esp`** is that figure verbatim: `create/2`,
  and the instance methods `goal/1` (the default, a fact), `assign/1`,
  `resign/1`, `resign_core/1` and `invoke/1`. Every method is labelled
  PRINTED; nothing is corrected.
- **`process`** gains four INTERPOLATED methods, which the figure sends:
  - `add_program/2` and `remove_program/2` push and pop the printed
    `invocation_stack` list, with the top first (OURS);
  - `no_program/1` tests that the stack is empty;
  - `die/1` sets the status to `nil`, the printed slot comment's
    "nil(=dead)". Nothing is released.

  The sources are TM-100 p11's prose ("pushed onto the invocation stack
  ... popped ... If the stack becomes empty, the process terminates")
  and the figure's own comments.
- **kl0: a missing primary under a stand-in ancestor stays SIMPOS.** A
  stand-in is only part of the lost class. So when a class with a
  stand-in in its inheritance order has no primary for a selector, kl0
  now refuses the send as `simpos` ("no primary in the held classes;
  stand-in ancestors ..."), as it does for an unloaded ancestor.
  - Without this rule, loading `as_program` turned 7 refusals (the
    demon-only `exit/1`, `reset/4`, `carriage_return/1`) into failures,
    and one (`as_pimos_login_requester goal/1`) into "no method".
  - On the tree before `as_program`, the rule changes no census row.

Tests (`test_espc.py` `test_step3e_as_program`):

- `assign/1` pushes and leaves the process alive.
- `resign_core/1` pops and leaves it alive.
- `invoke/1` of the printed default goal pushes, runs it, pops, and
  kills the process (status `nil`).
- `create/2` inherited by `pimos_timer` makes a `pimos_timer`.
- `pimos_window_editor carriage_return/1` (demons only) refuses as
  SIMPOS.

Effect on the FEP census, compared method by method: 1,265 / 667 / 120
/ 478 becomes 1,403 / 782 / 121 / 500 (methods / success / failure /
refused).

- **138 new methods**: the inherited `create/2` (26 classes),
  `assign/1`, `invoke/1`, `resign/1` and `resign_core/1` (28 each).
  - 109 succeed.
  - 28 refuse, all `invoke/1`. They stop in the class's own `goal/1`:
    13 at a `put/2` to an unset 0 slot, 3 at `library:assign/1`, and
    others.
  - 1 fails: `pimos_timer resign_core/1`. The census runs every method
    on the one process. `pimos_timer`'s `resign/1` has just emptied the
    stack, so `resign_core/1` finds it empty. Later classes'
    `resign_core/1` succeed, because each refused `invoke/1` leaves its
    program on the stack.
- **6 move from refused to success**: the create methods that stopped at
  `create/2`. These are `fep_scsi_output create/7`,
  `pimfep_message_receiver create/8`, `pimfep_message_sender create/6`,
  `pimos_fep_superviser create/5`, `pimos_socket_receiver create/5` and
  `pimscsi_fep create/3`.
- **24 stop later**:
  - 11 at `process:status/3`, which the printed `activate/2` sends
    (`directory/10`, `create/9`, the `*_open/10`, `create_timer/9`,
    ...);
  - 11 at the stand-in rule above;
  - 1 at `process:invocation_stack/2` and 1 at
    `abort_handler:create/2`.
- No method moved out of success.

### Step 3e: the interrupt brackets, the vector puts, the bit operations (2026-11-24)

Ten rows, and R != A for two old ones. Each has a held predicate whose
cells sit beside their held source (re-read here; the merged
`docs/simpos/source_check_2026-11-24.md` listed most of them as leads).
MEASURED counts are scratch surveys of the eleven packages.

| Row | Reading | Witness (held source) |
|---|---|---|
| blt 9a, 9b | `inhibit_interrupt`, `allow_interrupt`: no-ops here (OURS: no interrupts are modelled) | TERMCODE.ESP:181-193, two brackets in source order; 14 and 17 uses, all `00 00 00` |
| pmi b1 | `put_vector_1` A | HLCAPRSUB.ESP:426-427 `{Group}`; 342 uses, one element cell after each |
| pmi b5 | `put_vector_5` A | receiver.esp:680-681 (A = 0), KP_MDMUGT.ESP:92-94 (A = 1); 144 uses, five elements after each |
| pmi b6 | `put_vector_6` A | p_MAIN.esp:443-444 (A = 0), KP_MDMSCT.ESP:142-144 (A = 1); 174 uses, six elements |
| pmi b7 | `put_vector_n` A, 0, n | p_icon.esp:226-229, n = 7; 67 uses, n elements after each, second byte 0 in all. A != 0 is not witnessed with source and refuses |
| blt 50 | `and` A, B, ^R | TERMCODE.ESP:188 (R = A), :85 (R = B) |
| blt 51 | `or` A, B, ^R | KP_TIMER.ESP:44, CONVERTER.ESP:41-45 (R != A) |
| blt 54 | `shift_left` X, N, ^R | CONVERTER.ESP:41-45 (R != X), KP_TIMER.ESP:43 |
| blt 55 | `shift_right` X, N, ^R | KP_TIMER.ESP:45 |

| blt 40, R != A | as before | p_wwin.esp:418-422 `40 00 01 01`, `40 00 02 02` |

What the 32-bit word does at its edges is not printed, so the bit rows
refuse it rather than guess. A negative operand refuses, as does a
shift count outside 0..31 and a left shift whose result reaches 2**31.
Two held idioms depend on it: KP_TIMER.ESP:43 `shift_left(Time1,31,T1)`
and the `set_integer` of KP_DBOFE.ESP:376-378. They are leads, not
witnesses.

Not added:

- blt 6f, `list_to_vector`, is witnessed (BCMSETSEQ.ESP:346-348, hash.esp:152,
  sancho1.esp:102), but what its third argument receives is not settled
  (NO SOURCE FOUND in the merged source check).
- The 9a/9b pairs of `pimmcsp##pseudo_pe_process` were not examined. In
  six of its predicates, a 9a has no 9b after it, or a 9b has none
  before it.

Tests (`test_kl0.py` `Step3eRows`):

- `test_interrupt_brackets`: the no-ops, and `pimfep_message_log log/2`
  run to its one solution.
- `test_vector_puts`: HLCAPRSUB.ESP's `make_nest_vector(2, a, F)` gives
  `{{a}}` on the held code. The handlers for b5, b6 and b7 are checked

### Step 3e: the census's slot constants and uncaught exceptions (2026-11-24)

Two FEP refusal causes turned out to be the census's own doing, or not a
gap at all.

**"Receiver not an object": 153, of which 139 are a census artifact.**
The census makes each receiver with new/2, not through the method that
creates it in SIMPOS. So a slot that the creator would have filled still
holds its template constant. A send to it, or a slot read from it, then
stops. A scratch probe (2026-11-24) measured the cases:

- 124 were sends to an integer slot that still held its template
  constant;
- 13 were sends to a slot whose constant is the atom `none`, and 1 to a
  `nil` slot.

(The probe ran before this milestone's opcode rows, when the total was
138. After the rows it is 153, and 139 of them are slot constants.)

kl0 now marks an integer that a slot's template constant put there
(`SlotConstant`, an `int` subclass, OURS). Arithmetic on it gives plain
integers. Only the refusal changes: a send to it, or a slot read from
it, is refused as **`receiver a slot constant`**.

- After this milestone's rows, 139 FEP refusals carry the new cause.
  The 14 atom cases stay `receiver not an object`: an atom slot is not
  marked, since `none` and `nil` are values a program also assigns.
- The six-package census's 8 such refusals are all slot constants.
- The fix the cause points at is to make the receivers through their
  creators. That is not done yet: it is per class (for example
  `pimos_window create_pimos_window/3` for `pimos_window_editor`).

**`undefined_method:raise/2` (16) is an exception, not a gap.**
`scsi_low_if`'s class `reset`, `initialize` and `shutdown` send
`:raise(#undefined_method, {Class, undefined_method, Class, {Method,
ArgVec, Addr}})` unless a subclass overrides them. TM-388 p.59 says a
missing method "generates an exception" through `:undefined_method/4`.
No exception handler, and no situation stack (TR-285 p.10), is modelled,
so nothing can catch it.

- kl0 now ends such a send with `Raised` (OURS), carrying the
  information term.
- The census records it as a new outcome, **`exception`**, an uncaught
  exception. Like success and failure, it counts as running to
  completion.

Tests (`test_kl0.py` `Step3eCensus`):

- `test_slot_constant`: an `array_index_buffer`'s `size` is marked, and
  `size + 1` is a plain int. A send to it gets the new cause; a send to a
  plain 0 gets the old one.
- `test_uncaught_exception`: `cmt`'s inherited class `reset/1` raises,
  with `undefined_method` and `reset` in its information. The census
  rows of `reset/1` and `initialize/1` are `exception`.

Effect on the FEP census, compared method by method: 1,403 / 784 / 123
/ 496 becomes 1,403 / 784 / 123 / 480, plus 16 `exception`.

- The 16 move from refused to exception.
- 148 change only their refusal's cause or detail. 139 take the new
  cause. In 9 built-in refusals (`string on 0`) the detail now names the
  slot constant.
- No method moved out of success.

The six-package census keeps its totals. 32 rows change only their
refusal: 8 take the new cause, and 24 have details that now name a slot
constant.

### Strings unify by storage (2026-11-25)

kl0's unification and `==` compared strings by contents. The PSI's do
not. Read from the page images:

- TM-388 PDF p79 (folio 67): `'abc'='abc'`, `'abc'=='abc'` and
  `'abc'=:='abc'` all fail; `equal_string('abc','abc')` succeeds; two
  references to one string succeed.
- TM-388 PDF p117-119 (folio 105-107): unify rule (iii)b and Table 4-3.
  Two strings are equal when they have the same logical address of the
  first element, its position in the word, element length and count.
- TM-388 PDF p169 (folio 157): in ESP, `"ABC" = "ABC"` fails. TM-115 PDF
  p97 (folio 92) says the same; the ESP manual (`program-e.tex`:7174)
  says it of `=:=`.

So kl0 now unifies a string, and holds it `==`, only with the same `Str`
object (one `Str` is one stored string). Python's `Str.__eq__` still
compares contents, for tests and tables; KL0 never uses it.

Two changes go with it:

- `string/3` (blt 0b) is a type test. TR-044 App. A.2 (PDF p39) lists
  `string(?String, ^Length, ^Size)` under "Data Type and Attributes".
  kl0 now fails on a non-string, unbound included, where it refused
  (INFERENCE: no failing call is printed).
- `list_index` compares keys with a local `equal_key/2`. TM-161 PDF p18
  names it; its body is not printed. Strings are compared by contents
  (`equal_string/2`), anything else by unification. Held callers need
  this: paragraph `p_wshell.esp` sets `"$from"` at :102 and reads it
  with another literal at :396, through `add_at`/`get_at` on a
  list_index (:95-99). The body is INTERPOLATED, and that the contents
  are compared there is INFERENCE.

A string constant in a clause stays a fresh string at each load
(`POOL_STR_PER_CELL = False`, INFERENCE: no source says whether it is
one stored string per constant).

Censuses, row by row, against the tree before this change:

- Identity alone (string/3 as before): no row changes in either census.
  The census calls methods with unbound arguments, so no two strings
  meet. One string per pool cell instead: also no change.
- With the type test, six packages: `csp_default_write write/2` goes
  from refused to the step limit. Totals 310 / 12 / 207 refused / 1 step
  limit.
- FEP, 40 rows: 789 succeed, 154 fail, 443 refused, 16 raise, 1 step
  limit (was 787 / 123 / 476 / 16 / 1).
  - Refused to failure (30): `simpos_user_device` io/4, io/5, do_io/6;
    `simpos_user` login_password/2; `remote_stream` put/2; `pimos_window`
    kl1_message/2; `pimos_window_editor` putb/4, ungetb/4;
    `fep_message_assembler` get_command/3, divide_message/4,
    connection_flag/3, get_device_id/3, disassemble/3;
    `message_converter` convert/3; `remote_pimfep_executor` open/1;
    `pimos_file` putb/4; `pimos_string_io_invoker` create_string_io/10;
    `pimos_fep_initiation_table` initialize/2,
    set_initiation_file_name/2; `net_spawn_user` server_name/3;
    `pimos_tcpip_socket` putb/5, connect/4; `pimos_standard_io_window`
    io/4, io/5, do_io/6; `pimos_fep_superviser` command_menu/2;
    `scsi_stream` put/2; `hmcb_log` message/3; `scsi_load_test`
    send_request/1, receive_request/1.
  - Refused to success (3): `remote_pimfep_executor` goal/1, receive/3,
    invoke/1.
  - Still refused, later (6): `pimos_window_process` goal/1,
    initiate/1, invoke/1 at a slot constant; `packet32` set_message/2
    at blt 0c; `scsi_handler` take_buffer/3 and take_TMSG/2 at blt 7f.
  - Success to failure (1): `pimos_file resign_core/1`. This is a census
    order effect. Run alone on a fresh machine it fails both before and
    after the change: the invocation stack it pops is empty. In the
    census it had succeeded on a program left there by an earlier call.

What the moves are: all 41 come from the string/3 type test (an
INFERENCE), none from the sourced identity rule. Twelve of them leave a
refusal `string on 0 (a slot constant)`, including the three new
successes: a census artifact of the step 3e kind (receivers made with
new/2), so 789 is not progress in itself. The two step-limit rows,
traced: FEP `packet32 initiate/1` is a long finite loop (its
`initiate/3` makes `pool_size` = 1,000 packets, the slot's template
constant, about 25 cells each, past the 20,000-step limit);
`csp_default_write write/2` does not terminate on an unbound argument
(its list clause binds the argument to a one-element list and recurses
on the unbound element; 11 cells repeat, the same at 400,000 steps).

With `Str.__eq__` and `__hash__` deleted, the only failure in both test
files is test_kl0's Python-side `assertEqual(sol[0], K.Str('3500'))`,
and neither census changes: no KL0 path compares strings by content.
The stand-ins' string constants are output arguments or held SIMPOS
source (`name`, `pathname`), so none relied on content comparison but
`list_index`.

Tests: `test_kl0.StringIdentity` and `test_espc.test_string_keys`. Four
injections are recorded in `test_kl0.py`'s header.

Open:

- Table 4-3 makes `==` element-wise on stack vectors and address-only on
  heap vectors. kl0's `==` (pmi 62, blt f0/f2) is object identity for
  both.
- kl0's unification of heap vectors is element-wise.
- A substring that shares its parent's storage is not modelled. kl0's
  string built-ins make new strings.

### Step 3f: census receivers made by their held creators (2026-11-25)

**The slot constants are census artifacts, with exceptions.** Before
this step the FEP census had 142 rows refused as `receiver a slot
constant` (not 139: the string type test moved three
`pimos_window_process` rows onto one). A scratch probe named the slot
behind each row: 22 distinct slots in 30 classes. The slot was then
looked up in the held cells, for a `set_<slot>/2` call or a `blt 2f`
slot write:

| Slot (rows before) | Held writer | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| `as_pimos_device`'s `input_stream`, `output_stream`, `creation_status_stream` (59 in 15 classes) | the device creators: `pimos_timer create_timer/9` @19-48, `pimos_file create/9` @19-52, `pimos_directory directory/10` @17-4e and `make_directory/10` @63-9a, `pimos_tcpip_socket create_socket/9` @21-69, `pimos_window_process create_with_buffer/10` @56-86, `pimos_string_io_invoker` local `initiate_string_io_device/7` @9d-b6 | artifact |
| `pimos_window_editor` `window` (21), `pimos_window` `editor` (1) | `pimos_window create_pimos_window/3` @19-34, through the `pmacs_window` stand-in | artifact |
| `remote_pimfep_executor` `accept` (1) | its own class after-demon on new/2, @40-48 (`accept` := the class slot `socket`, whose template constant is the string `'pimos_scsi_fep_control'`) | artifact: `make_instance` runs no new/2 demon; with it, one template constant replaces another |
| `fep_scsi_output` `report_stream` (2), `pimfep_message_receiver`/`_sender` `fep` (6), `scsi_stream` `handler` (3), `pimos_socket_receiver` `data_stream` (2), `pimos_scsi_front_end` `input_stream` (1) | a creator class method that stores its caller's argument (`fep_scsi_output create/7` @16-3b, `create/8` @16-2e, `create/6` @22-30, `scsi_stream create/4` @2b-46, `pimos_socket_receiver create/5` @14-25, `pimos_scsi_front_end create/6` @41-5c) | artifact; the value is the caller's, not supplied |
| `pimos_tcpip_socket` `receive_message_stream` (2), `pimos_window_process` `device_process` and `pimos_window` (2), `pimos_login_requester` `window` (4), `simpos_user` `user` (3) | the object's own process body or set-up (`initiate/1` @a8-c3, `initiate/1` @f0-10d, local `initiate/1` @ad-b8, `setup_process/2` @e3-ed), which runs only once the process does | artifact of a process that never runs |
| `cmt`, `scsi` `channel` and `sender` (14), `scsi_device`, `scsi_device_bus_management` `handler` (8), `scsi_handler` `devices`, `request_devices`, `channel` (7), `scsi_load_test` `scsi`, `window` (5), `pseudo_scsi_handler` `memory` (1) | for `scsi_load_test` `window`, its class `create/2` and `create/4` (`blt 2f` @59, @72); `pseudo_scsi new/3` sets `sender` (@5c); the creators `scsi_common_if create/3`, `/4` need bound bus and unit numbers (`not_less_than` on the census's unbound argument) | artifact where a writer is found; for `channel`, `devices`, `request_devices` and `memory` no writer was found by name (UNKNOWN) |

**Recipes (OURS).** `kl0.RECIPES` gives the census a way to make a
receiver the way held code does: it runs the class's held creator,
through the stand-ins, and takes the object it made. No slot is written
from Python. `census --fep --recipes` uses them; plain `census --fep`
is unchanged and keeps its pins.

- The device creators share a prefix: `:create(Class, Dev)`,
  `:process(Class, .., Dev)`, `:stream(Class, S)`,
  `:set_input_stream(Dev, S)`, `:set_output_stream(Dev, A0)`, a second
  `stream/2`, `:set_creation_status_stream(Dev, S2)`,
  `:set_device_id(Dev, A3)`, then `:activate(Process, Dev)`.
  activate/2 is the PRINTED `process:activate/2` (TM-100 Fig. 3.1). It
  needs `status/3` (not printed) and the hardware `context`. So kl0
  stops the creator before that send (`Machine.stop_before`,
  `CreatorStopped`) and takes Dev, its first argument: the device as its
  creator left it, not yet running.
- The creators' A0 is the stream the device answers on, the caller's.
  INFERENCE: it is the front end's `fep_output_stream`.
  `pimos_scsi_front_end create_device/4` reads `fep_output_stream/2`
  (@42e-431) into the first of the three elements it adds to the message
  vector before `:refute` (@433-447). In each creator, A1 and A2 then
  take the device's input stream and its process, which the front end
  enters in its `device_table` (`add_at/5` @448-452). The front end's
  stream comes from `fep_scsi_output create/7` (@32-3b), whose class kl0
  resolves to `simpos##stream`. So the recipe passes a new
  `simpos##stream` (OURS: a fresh one). The other arguments are unbound,
  as in the plain census.
- Recipes run for `pimos_timer`, `pimos_file`, `pimos_directory` and
  `pimos_window_process`. `pimos_window` and `pimos_window_editor` come
  from `create_pimos_window/3` and the stand-in's `get_editor/2`.
  `remote_pimfep_executor` is made by sending `:new`, so that its
  after-demon runs.
- Not given a recipe:
  - `pimos_tcpip_socket create_socket/9` reads the calling process's
    invocation stack (`:invocation_stack(P, L)`, `:get_first(L, _)`,
    @4d-59) before activate/2. The process stand-in has no such
    accessor, and the census's process runs no program.
  - `pimos_string_io_invoker create_string_io/10` (it makes
    `pimos_remote_login_replier`, `_server`, `simpos_user_device` and
    `pimos_standard_io_window`, `fep_services.md` §6.4) stops at
    `symbolizer:enter_atom/3` and `library:assign/1`.
  - The argument-supplied slots and the process-body slots of the table
    above: a recipe would have to invent the caller's value or run a
    process.

Effect, compared method by method with plain `census --fep` (1,403
methods, 789 / 154 / 443 / 16 / 1):

- With recipes: 791 succeed, 154 fail, 441 refused, 16 exception, 1 step
  limit. 101 rows are still `receiver a slot constant`; 41 of the 142
  left it.
- Where the 41 went. None of the moves is progress in itself: each is a
  real held path that now runs further on a census receiver.
  - 30 stop at a SIMPOS call.
    - 19 `pimos_window_editor` rows stop at `pimos_window`'s missing
      `pmacs_window` protocol: `get_interpreter/2`, `set_position/3`,
      `get_position/3`, `activate/1`, `flash/1`, `beep/1`,
      `set_title/2`, `get_text_size/3`, `get_font/2`, `get_title/2`,
      `set_text_size/3`, `reshape/5`. `pimos_window_process` `goal/1`
      and `invoke/1` stop there too, at `get_interpreter/2`.
    - The device `loop_main/1` rows stop at `see/2`, which the
      `stream` stand-in lacks (timer, file, directory; the window
      process's own stream is the process itself). The timer's `goal/1`
      and `invoke/1` stop there as well.
    - `pimos_directory` `initiate/1`, `goal/1` and `invoke/1` stop at
      `simpos##directory:retrieve/4` (class not loaded).
  - 5 stop at `set_string_element` on an unbound argument (the
    `attention/1` rows; see the recomputed accounting below for what
    that argument is).
  - 4 succeed: `pimos_window set_output_stream/2`, `pimos_window_process
    initiate/1`, and `pimos_file goal/1` and `invoke/1`. pimos_file's
    goal/1 takes its error clause (`put(error)` on the creation-status
    stream). Its `initiate/1` (1 row, now `failure`) runs `open` on the
    census's unbound pathname and fails.
  - 1, `remote_pimfep_executor close/1`, is now `receiver not an
    object`: it sends `close_socket/3` to `accept`, which now holds the
    class slot `socket`'s template constant, the string
    `'pimos_scsi_fep_control'`.
  - (Not among the 41: `pimos_window_editor attention/1` is still a slot
    constant, one hop later, at the editor's `pimos_window_process`
    slot.)
- 3 move out of success:
  - `remote_pimfep_executor goal/1` and `invoke/1` stop at
    `network_node_name:to_tcpip_address/3`, and `open/1` moves there from
    failure. With the after-demon run, `accept` holds the class slot's
    value, so `open/1` gets past the test that used to fail. That value
    is itself a template constant: one template constant (0) replaced by
    another (the string `'pimos_scsi_fep_control'`). Whether a class
    method sets `socket` before new/2 in real use was not traced. The
    plain census's successes were on an instance that new/2, with its
    after-demon, could not have made.
  - `pimscsi_fep resign_core/1` is a census order effect: run alone on a
    fresh machine it fails, as it now does.

Test (`test_espc.py` `test_step3f_census_recipes`): the timer's three
streams are `simpos##stream` objects and its device id is unbound; the
editor and executor recipes; the recipe census totals and its 101 slot
constants; rows that go on (timer `loop_main/1` at `stream:see/2`, the
editor at `get_interpreter/2`) while `as_pimos_device` (no creator)
still refuses. Four injections into `kl0.py`, each from a backup copy,
restored by cp and checked with cmp. Observed: each fails only this
test, and `test_kl0.py` passes.

- The `pimos_timer` recipe dropped: errors. Measured separately, the

- A0 left unbound: fails.
- No stop before activate/2: errors. The creator runs into
