- The executor made by `make_instance`: fails.

### Step 3f: the library and the symbolizer's atom table (2026-11-25)

SIMPOS's `library` (the Librarian) and `symbolizer` are not held. Four
of their class methods cannot be ESP stand-ins: they answer from the
loader's tables and from the atom table. So kl0 models them in Python
(`SIMPOS_MODELS`, OURS). Each docstring cites its sources, and any
other method still refuses as SIMPOS.

- `:assign(#library)`. INTERPOLATED from its call sites: it is the
  first library send of its clause, before `check_info/3`,
  `get_class_object/3` or `make_package/2` (kappa2 HLCLIB.ESP:29,
  LOADCH.ESP:64, LOADER.ESP:251; the pimfep and pimscsi sends that the
  plain census stopped at, 12 rows). What it
  binds is UNKNOWN (`subsystems/gaps.md`, librarian row 4). It succeeds
  and changes nothing.
- `:check_info(#library, Name, Kind)`. INTERPOLATED from kappa2
  LOADER.ESP:252-256 and `pimos_string_io_invoker`'s local
  `get_class_object/3` (@5a-80). Both try `template_file` (then
  `make_class_object/2`) and `object`, then `get_class_object/3`.
  (Step 3g's re-check: LOADER tries `object` first, pimfep
  `template_file` first. The model answers the same in either order.)
  OURS:
  every class here is loaded as a class object, so `object` succeeds
  for a loaded class and `template_file` fails.
- `:get_class_object(#library, Name, Class)`. The class an atom names:
  `pkg##c` exactly; else `c` in the sending class's package; else the
  one loaded `*##c`. A class not loaded, or a name two packages share
  with no sender, refuses.
  - INTERPOLATED: "own package first" is the static rule for a bare
    class name (`subsystems/librarian.md` §1.4, TM-388 pp92-101).
  - INFERENCE: that the run-time lookup follows it, and that "own"
    means the sender's package, not the process's default package
    (HLCLIB.ESP:30-31 `:get_package(Process, P)`).
  - The rule is needed: pimfep and pimmcsp both define
    `pimos_remote_login_replier`, `_server`, `simpos_user_device` and
    `pimos_timer`.
- `:enter_atom(#symbolizer, Atom, String)`. PRINTED interface: TR-283
  p18 (folio 16), Table 4, lists `:enter_atom/c2` (atom <-> string).
  The argument order is from paragraph `p_wshell.esp`:211 (`substring(
  C0,1,Len-1,C1), :enter_atom(#symbolizer,Var,C1), :get_at(Obj!var,St,
  Var)`: the atom first, then used as a key) and `create_string_io/10`
  @28-2d. The atom's name is the string's text (OURS).
- `NAMED_NOT_DEFINED.md`'s `:status/2` and kl0's `process:status/3` are
  the same method. The doc counts the arguments without the receiver.

**The string-I/O recipe.** `pimos_string_io_invoker
create_string_io/10` (@15-45) makes the program PIMOS names by command
601 (`fep_services.md` §6.4):

1. It tests A8 as a 16-bit string (@1d-1e).
2. It makes the string an atom (@28-2d) and takes the class from the
   library (local `get_class_object/3`).
3. It creates the object (local `create/3`) and gives it its streams
   and device id (local `initiate_string_io_device/7` @9d-b6).
4. Then it sends activate/2.

`RECIPES` now passes the class name as that string for
`pimos_remote_login_replier`, `pimos_remote_login_server`,
`simpos_user_device` and `pimos_standard_io_window` (PIMOS's
`pass_body.kl1`:68). It stops before activate/2, as the device creators
do.

Effect, method by method:

- Plain `census --fep`: 789 / 154 / 443 becomes 790 / 154 / 442.
  - `remote_stream create_to_fep/3` goes from refused to success.
  - `create_from_fep/4` stops at an unbound receiver (its
    `convert_con_req/6` is sent to an argument).
  - 11 others stop later:
    - `create_from_remote_fep/4` at
      `network_node_name:network_type/3`;
    - `create_to_remote_fep/5` at `nline:connect/5`;
    - `initiate_to_fep/1` at `get_class_object/3` of `nline` (not
      loaded);
    - `pimscsi_fep`'s and `pseudo_scsi_fep`'s `goal/1` and `invoke/1`
      at `standard_input_file:create/3`;
    - `nise_scsi_fep`'s three at `mpsicsp##nise_scsi` (not loaded);
    - `remote_pimfep_invoker goal/1` at its `convert_con_req/6`
      (SIMPOS).
  - None leaves success.
- With recipes: 791 / 154 / 441 becomes 792 / 154 / 440, and the slot
  constants go from 101 to 85. The 16 rows of the four string-I/O
  programs move:
  - `goal/1`, `loop_main/1` and `invoke/1` (12) to `stream:see/2`;
  - `attention/1` (4) to `set_string_element` on an unbound argument.
- Not progress in itself. `stream:see/2` is now the commonest stop
  (every device `loop_main/1`), and no held or printed source defines
  it: TM-100 Fig. 3.6 prints `create/2`, `put/2` and `get/2` only, and
  the kappa2 `:see(Buffer,Size,MDU)` sends (mdu TABLE.ESP:518) are to a
  buffer.

Test (`test_espc.py` `test_step3f_library_and_symbolizer`): each model
on its own (assign, both kinds of `check_info`, `get_class_object`
qualified and ambiguous, `enter_atom` and its refusal), and the replier
made by `create_string_io/10` in pimfep with its three streams.
`test_step3f_census_recipes` pins the four programs' `loop_main/1`
refusal at SIMPOS, and the abstract `as_pimos_string_io_device` still at
a slot constant. Four injections into `kl0.py`, from a backup, restored
by cp, cmp-checked. Observed, `test_kl0.py` passing each time:

- assign/1 failing: test_fep_census, test_step3f_census_recipes and
  test_step3f_library_and_symbolizer fail.
- The own-package rule dropped: test_step3f_census_recipes fails and
  test_step3f_library_and_symbolizer errors.
- `template_file` succeeding as `object` does: test_step3f_census_recipes
  and test_step3f_library_and_symbolizer fail.
- enter_atom reading its first argument as the string:
  test_step3f_census_recipes fails and test_step3f_library_and_symbolizer
  errors.

### Step 3f: `pmacs_window`'s geometry, title and display methods (2026-11-25)

With recipes, 19 `pimos_window_editor` rows stopped at `pimos_window`
methods that `pmacs_window` should supply. `standin/pmacs_window.esp`
gains 17 of them, every one INTERPOLATED and labelled from
`subsystems/window.md` §2.1-2.3:

- PRINTED-INTERFACE: TM-125 p15-16 for `set_size/3`, `set_position/3`,
  `set_title/2`, `activate/1`, `show/1`, `hide/1` and `deactivate/1`;
  TM-115 p50 and p63-64 (the methods on p64: step 3g, source check §7) for `get_size/3` and `get_position/3`; TM-112 p16
  for `set_font/2`.
- USAGE only: `reshape/5`, `set_text_size/3`, `get_text_size/3`,
  `get_title/2`, `get_font/2`, `beep/1` and `flash/1`.

Model (OURS): each value is a slot that its setter writes and its getter
reads back. Nothing is displayed, so the display methods succeed and do
nothing. The initial values (0, an empty title) are OURS. The font
starts as 0, since no font object is stood in, and a `manipulator`
coordinate is stored like a number. Not stood in: the text protocol
(§2.4: `putl/2`, `putf/3`, `getl/2`), `get_interpreter/2`, and the
`has_been_reshaped/1` callback.

Effect, method by method:

- Plain `census --fep`: 1,403 becomes 1,420 methods. The 17 new rows

  which is not progress. So 790 / 154 / 442 becomes 807 / 154 / 442.
  `pimfep_message_log` `dump_log/1`, `sorted_log/1` and
  `sorted_log_string/1` stop later, past `activate/1`: at pmi ff, or at

- With recipes: 809 / 154 / 440, with the same 17 new successes. The

  (`clear/3` and `buffer_name/3` still stop at `get_interpreter/2`):

    `show/3`, `hide/3`, `beep/3`, `set_title/4`, `get_inside_size/3`,

  - 4 stop at the `has_been_reshaped/1` callback: `set_size/3`,

  - 1, `getl/3`, stops at `get_interpreter/2`.

Test (`test_espc.py` `test_step3f_window_protocol`): each setter's value

- `activate/1` failing: test_fep_census, test_step3d_window_and_timer,

### Step 3f: pmi b7 with A != 0 (2026-11-25)

The step 3e row of pmi b7 (`put_vector_n`) refused A != 0 for want of a
witness whose source is held. A search of the six source-held packages
(MEASURED 2026-11-25) found 12 uses with A != 0, all with a zero second
byte. One is lined up with its source:

- `kappa##kappaMDM_manager set_up/2` @e6 is `b7 01 00 09`. Nine `43`
  element cells follow (@e7-ef), then `a2 00 00` and a send of
  set_up/3 (@f1-f2).
- The source is KP_MDM.ESP:89, `:set_up(DBO,OBJuser,{MS_max,MSN,RS_max,
  RSN,RS_size,MH_max,MHN,IH_max,IHN})`. The nine-element vector is the
  second argument, so it goes to A1. The two slot writes just before
  (@e1-e5) are lines 87-88, `OBJ!default_record_extent_size :=` and
  `OBJ!default_index_extent_parameter :=`.
- KNOWN-PLAINTEXT. The row now accepts any A. A non-zero second byte
  still refuses.

Census, row by row: one FEP method stops later in both modes.
`pimos_fep_superviser error_mode_menu/2` (`b7 05 00 07`) now reaches
`simpos##standard_choice_window`. Totals are unchanged, and the
six-package census is unchanged (310 / 12 / 207 / 1).

Test (`test_kl0.py` `test_vector_puts`): b7 with A = 1 makes a
nine-element vector there; the second byte still refuses; the witness's
cells are pinned. Injected into `kl0.py` from a backup, restored and
cmp-checked. Observed:

- A != 0 refused again: test_vector_puts errors.
- A non-zero second byte accepted: test_vector_puts fails.

`test_espc.py` passes both times: its census pins count the moved row
as refused either way.

### Step 3f: the census accounting, recomputed (2026-11-25)

A review after the b7 commit found the step-by-step numbers above were
partly mis-attributed. Every figure here is recomputed, row by row, from
two final TSVs of HEAD (`census --fep` and `census --fep --recipes`)
and from the plain census of master 496173b8 run on the untouched tree
at the start of step 3f. Rows are keyed by (class, section, selector).
Outcome and cause are compared, and `_G` variable numbers are ignored.

**Master plain to HEAD plain** (1,403 to 1,420 methods; 789 / 154 / 443
/ 16 / 1 to 807 / 154 / 442 / 16 / 1):

- 17 new rows, the `pmacs_window` methods inherited by `pimos_window`.
  All succeed, which is not progress.
- 13 rows keep their outcome and cause but stop later.
- 4 rows move:
  - `remote_stream create_from_fep/4`: simpos to unbound receiver.
  - `remote_stream create_to_fep/3`: simpos to success. This rests on
    the no-op `library:assign/1` (INTERPOLATED).
  - `pimfep_message_log dump_log/1`: simpos to opcode, now at pmi ff.
  - `pimos_fep_superviser error_mode_menu/2`: operand to simpos, now at
    `standard_choice_window` (b7 A != 0).

So the plain census gains 1 real success. The other 17 are new rows.

**HEAD plain to HEAD recipes** (807 / 154 / 442 to 809 / 154 / 440;
slot constants 142 to 86):

- 56 rows leave `receiver a slot constant`:
  - 30 to simpos: `stream:see/2` (10 `goal/1`/`invoke/1` rows, 7
    `loop_main/1` rows), `pimos_window_process:see/2` (the window
    process's `loop_main/1`), `get_interpreter/2` (3 editor rows, 2
    window-process rows), `has_been_reshaped/1` (4 editor rows),
    `directory:retrieve/4` (3 rows).
  - 20 to `unbound argument` at `set_string_element`: 12 editor rows and
    8 `attention/1` rows.
  - 4 to success: `pimos_file goal/1` and `invoke/1`,
    `pimos_window_process initiate/1`, `pimos_window
    set_output_stream/2`.
  - 1 to failure: `pimos_file initiate/1`.
  - 1 to `receiver not an object`: `remote_pimfep_executor close/1`
    sends `close_socket` to `accept`, which now holds the class slot
    `socket`'s template constant, the string `'pimos_scsi_fep_control'`
    (one template constant replaced by another).
- 2 rows are still slot constants but stop later: editor `attention/1`
  and editor `get_font/3`. `get_font/3` reaches the font slot of the
  `pmacs_window` stand-in, whose initial 0 is OURS. That slot constant
  is made by the stand-in, not by held code.
- Out of success, 3:
  - `remote_pimfep_executor goal/1` and `invoke/1`, to
    `to_tcpip_address/3` (simpos). Their plain successes were on an
    executor that new/2, with its after-demon, could not have made.
  - `pimscsi_fep resign_core/1`, to failure.
- 2 more rows move:
  - executor `open/1`: failure to simpos.
  - `pimos_file resign_core/1`: failure to success.

**The two `resign_core/1` rows are order effects.** On a fresh machine,
each alone, both fail in both modes (MEASURED 2026-11-25). Their census
successes, `pimscsi_fep` plain and `pimos_file` with recipes, come from
state left by earlier rows in the same run.

**Net with recipes:** 4 real successes, 1 real failure, and 51 rows on
to a named gap (30 simpos, 20 unbound request, 1 non-object). None of
the 56 is progress toward a running front end by itself: it is the
removal of a census artifact.

**The `set_string_element` stops, traced.** In editor `get_title/3` the
unbound argument is the request Id (A0). It reaches
`fep_message_assembler assemble/3` through `reply_message/2`. With Id =
7 the call goes on to `pad_string/2` and refuses at blt 48
(`multiply_constant`) with R != A, an unwitnessed operand pattern. The
other rows were not traced one by one.

**Six-package census:** identical to master row by row (530 methods,
310 / 12 / 207 / 1).

**Invented slot names.** The `pmacs_window` slots `x`, `y`, `width`,
`height`, `columns`, `rows`, `title` and `font` are OURS. A slot read or
write in compiled code names its slot with an atom (`pmi-atom put rN,
activity` before `pimos_window create_pimos_window/3`'s write is the
pattern). The pimfep and pimscsi listings were searched for those eight
atoms (MEASURED 2026-11-25). Only one is found, `pmi-atom get r76,
title` at pimscsi `scsi_load_test` @cc, and it is a unify with an atom,
not a slot access. So no held code reads or writes an invented slot.

**Rows that are called real were checked alone** (MEASURED 2026-11-25:
each method on a fresh machine). With recipes, `pimos_file goal/1` and
`invoke/1`, `pimos_window_process initiate/1` and `pimos_window
set_output_stream/2` succeed alone, and `pimos_file initiate/1` fails
alone. Plain, `remote_stream create_to_fep/3` succeeds alone. Only the
two `resign_core/1` rows are order effects.

**The 20 unbound-argument rows by class.** The 12 editor rows are the
11 named in the `pmacs_window` section plus `deactivate/3`, which that
list missed. The 8 `attention/1` rows are one per class: `pimos_timer`,
`pimos_file`, `pimos_directory`, `pimos_window_process`,
`pimos_remote_login_replier`, `pimos_remote_login_server`,
`simpos_user_device` and `pimos_standard_io_window`. The milestone
sections' partial counts are superseded by these. Only `get_title/3`
was traced to the request Id. That the other rows at
`set_string_element` stop on the same unbound request is INFERENCE,
from the shared reply path.

Test (`test_espc.py` `test_step3f_census_recipes`): the executor's
`accept` is now pinned as the string `'pimos_scsi_fep_control'`, and
`time` as 10. Both are template constants (`time` is still a slot
constant). Made by `make_instance` instead, both are 0 (MEASURED), so
both assertions fail under the step 3f "executor made by
`make_instance`" injection.

### Step 3g: `stream:see/2`, `try_to_see/2`, `try_to_get/2` (2026-11-25)

**The search.** No printed or held source defines `see/2` on a stream.
Searched (2026-11-25):

- the page images of TM-100 PDF p13-15 (Fig. 3.6 prints `create/2`,
  `put/2`, `get/2`; the prose names no other method) and TR-056 PDF
  p12-13 (folio 10-11: "Two major operations on a stream", `:put` and
  `:get`; a stream "looks like a tap on a pool");
- TM-009 p5-6 (OCR: a stream's operations are put and get);
- the held ESP source: the 24 IFS SIMPOS/CESP archives, kappa2, the ESP
  manuals, and `pimos`, `psdpim`, `pimosman`, `pimosobj`, `vpim`,
  `pkl1` and the other IFS and `icot-other` archives (grep `:see(`,
  `try_to_see`, `see_with_key`);
- every predicate of the eleven held packages (`see/2` and
  `try_to_see/2` sends).

What the search found:

- `see/2` sends: two, both in pimfep, whose source is not held.
  - `as_pimos_device` `$or$1/3` @215-22b, the body of every device's
    `loop_main/1`: `:see(S, _)`, with a fresh variable (@21b) never read
    again; `inhibit_forced_event/1` (@21f); `:get(S, M)` on the same
    stream (@223); the local `refute/3` on M (@227).
  - `pimos_tcpip_socket` `do_getb/3` @3b0-3c3: `allow_forced_event/1`
    (@3b9), `:see(S, _)` (@3bd, result unused), `inhibit_forced_event/1`
    (@3bf), `:get(S, M)` (@3c3).
- `try_to_see/2` sends: two, source not held, and here the result is
  used.
  - pimfep `pimos_tcpip_socket abort_loop/1` @30e-324: `:try_to_see(S,
    M)`, `fep_message_assembler get_command/3` on M, `== 210`, cut, then
    `:get(S, _)`.
  - pimmcsp `csp_stream_interface` `$or$2/1` @281-28e: `:try_to_see(S,
    X)`, `string(X,_,_)`, cut, `:get(S, _)`.
- `try_to_get/2` on a `:create(#stream, S)` stream, HELD-SOURCE: fuzclust
  `src/cliant.grape`:212-215 (`try_get(Stream,Mes) :- :try_to_get(Stream,
  Mes),!; try_get(Stream,Mes) :- Mes=none,!;`, polled in a `repeat` loop
  at 203-206; the stream made at 358-360) and icotext
  `src/bunkatu_io.esp`:24. Also kappa2 `kernel/svm/SVMSG.ESP`:132 (a
  `channel_set`) and pimfep `pimfep_message_sender`
  `clear_from_fep_stream/5` @2c3-2c4.
- In other classes, `see` looks without taking. This is ANALOGY only.
  kwic-esp `doc/classSpec.doc` (ISO-2022-JP):
  - its taps' `see_with_key/3` and `see_key/2` are "the same as the
    simpos pool tap's";
  - its own `see/4` reads the current position, "the current position
    is not changed", beside a `get/4` that advances it.

  The `:see(B, Char)` sends to text buffers (cil `ds_fep.esp`:1842-1908)
  and kappa2's `:see(Buffer,Size,MDU)` are the same idea, in buffer
  classes.

**The stand-ins (INTERPOLATED, `standin/stream.esp`).** Each copies the
printed `get/2`. The post queue's oldest object is looked at, by a new
`post_queue see_object/2` (name OURS), instead of taken out.

- `see/2` waits as `get/2` does (hook, then suspend) and removes
  nothing. That it waits and removes nothing is INTERPOLATED from the
  `see/2` call sites: forced events are allowed around it, and the
  `get/2` after it takes the object. What its second argument receives
  (the oldest object) is INFERENCE, from `try_to_see/2`, whose result is
  read.
- `try_to_see/2` is the same, and fails at once on an empty stream.
  Failing is INFERENCE from the `try_to_` prefix.
- `try_to_get/2` is `get/2` that fails at once on an empty stream: from
  the fuzclust polling loop.
- Not modelled: `put/2` resumes one waiting process. A process waiting
  in `see/2` and another in `get/2` on one stream are not distinguished.

**`process:suspend/2` refuses (OURS, kl0 `SIMPOS_MODELS`).** kl0 runs one
process and no scheduler. A process that suspends here waits for a put
or resume that nothing can make. Run, the printed body (TM-100 Fig. 3.1)
fails for the census's process: `:self(#process, P)` makes it with
new/2, its status is nil, and nothing makes it ready. So the wait would
show as a clean failure. kl0 now refuses there, with cause `would
suspend`. Before this step no census row reached `suspend/2` (MEASURED:
a dispatch probe, plain and recipes). So the only earlier behaviour that
changes is the test of `get/2` on an empty stream (`test_stream_runs`).

Effect, row by row against HEAD before the step (MEASURED 2026-11-25):

- Plain `census --fep`: identical (807 / 154 / 442 / 16 / 1).
- With recipes: totals unchanged (809 / 154 / 440 / 16 / 1). The 17 rows
  that stopped at `simpos##stream:see/2` move from `simpos` to `would
  suspend`:
  - `goal/1`, `loop_main/1` and `invoke/1` of `pimos_timer`,
    `simpos_user_device`, `pimos_remote_login_server`,
    `pimos_remote_login_replier` and `pimos_standard_io_window` (15);
  - `loop_main/1` of `pimos_file` and `pimos_directory` (2).

  Each waits in `see/2` on its device's empty input stream. That is
  what a device with no request does. It is not progress, but the gap
  is now the missing request, not a missing method.
  `pimos_window_process:see/2` (a different receiver, the window process
  itself) is still refused.
- With one message on its input stream (`test_step3g_stream_see`), a
  timer's `loop_main/1` sees it, gets it (the stream is empty after),
  and its local `refute/3` stops at `fep_message_assembler
  disassemble/3`'s blt 6f (`list_to_vector`, unwitnessed).

Test (`test_espc.py` `test_step3g_stream_see`): the empty-stream cases;
`see/2` and `try_to_see/2` leave the oldest object; `try_to_get/2` takes
it; the timer loop. `test_step3f_census_recipes` pins the 17 `would
suspend` rows. Injected from a backup, restored by cp, cmp-checked.
Observed, with `test_kl0.py` passing each time:

- `see/2` taking the object out: only `test_step3g_stream_see` fails.
- `try_to_see/2` waiting: only `test_step3g_stream_see` errors.
- `try_to_get/2` leaving the object: only `test_step3g_stream_see`
  fails.
- kl0's suspend/2 model removed: `test_step3f_census_recipes`,
  `test_step3g_stream_see` and `test_stream_runs` fail.

### Step 3g: census requests, and blt 48 with R != A (2026-11-25)

**Where a request sits, read from held code.**

- *A device command.* The device's local `refute/3` (`as_pimos_device`
  @129-145) handles it:
  - `fep_message_assembler disassemble/3` (@8f-ac) takes message
    elements 0 and 2 (@96-9b) and the argument list, and makes the list
    `[Id, Code | Args]`;
  - blt 6f (`list_to_vector`, unwitnessed) makes it a vector;
  - `convert/3` gives the command's name;
  - `:refute(Device, Name, Vector)` calls the handler.

  So a handler's A0 is the request's Id and A1 its code. The other
  readers agree:
  - `get_device_id/3` reads element 0 (@3c-44);
  - `get_command/3` reads element 2 (@14-1c);
  - `close/3` replies `{A0, 1}` (@6e-77).

  That the vector's elements arrive in order is `fep_services.md`
  §6.1's INFERENCE. A window passes its commands on to its editor the
  same way: `pimos_window kl1_message/2` (@77-98) disassembles,
  converts, calls `get_editor/2`, and sends `:refute(Editor, Name,
  Vector)`.
- *An abort handler* takes its request's Id as A0. `simpos_user_device
  io/5` (@36-4c) catches `pimos_abort` with `abort_string_io` and `{Y0}`,
  where Y0 is its own A0. `pimos_timer on_after/4` (@63-7d) does the
  same with `abort_timer` and `{Y0, X8}`.
- *A creator's device id* is the first element of the create message.
  Each creator keeps it for `:set_device_id(Dev, Y)` and for its
  creation reply `{Y, 1}`:
  - `create_timer/9`: A3 (@1d, @41-44, @52-58);
  - `directory/10`: A3;
  - `create_with_buffer/10`: A3;
  - `create_string_io/10`: A3 (@19, @3b, @4f-51);
  - `pimos_file create/9`: A4. Its callers `read_open/10` and the other
    open handlers (@67-72) put the open mode (`read`) in A0, which
    shifts the message one place.
- *PIMOS gives device ids from 16*: `scsi_fep.kl1` `create/2` (22-23)
  seeds `id_manager/3`, which answers `normal(ID, Stream)` and goes on
  with ID+1 (131-141, 157-160).

**The values (OURS), kl0 `REQUEST_ID`, `request_args`.**

- Creators: the device id is 16, the first id PIMOS gives.
- Command handlers: an instance method of an heir of `as_pimos_device`
  or of `pimos_window_editor`, whose class has a recipe, with arity 3 or
  more, and whose name the held `fep_command_code_table convert/3` maps
  to a code. It gets A0 = 16 and A1 = that code. The other arguments
  stay unbound.
- Abort handlers (`abort_string_io/2`, `abort_timer/3`, `abort_getb/3`,
  `abort_accept/3`) get A0 = 16.
- Plain `census --fep` is unchanged.

**A step 3f recipe was wrong (CONTRADICTED by the cells).** The
`pimos_file` recipe put the output stream in A0 of `create/9`. A0 is
the open mode (`set_open_mode`, @4b-4e). The output stream is A1
(@37-3a), which stayed unbound. So step 3f's `pimos_file goal/1` and
`invoke/1` successes, and its `initiate/1` failure, ran with a stream as
the open mode. `DEVICE_CREATORS` now names each creator's output-stream
and device-id arguments, and gives `pimos_file` A0 = `read` (OURS: the
mode `read_open/10` passes).

**blt 48 with R != A: KNOWN-PLAINTEXT, row widened.** The witness is
`paragraph##scroll_bar_icon` `$or$0/3`, against `p_hwin.esp`:229-236:

    :code(Icon,X,Y,Code):- Height=Icon!height/4,
        (Y=<Height,!, Code=previous_page; Y=<(Height*2),!, Code=previous_one;
         Y=<(Height*3),!, Code=next_one; Code=next_page);

- `code/4` @14-16 puts Height in A0 (`27 03 00 00`, `49 00 04 00`) and
  executes `$or$0` with Y in A1.
- @282 is `48 00 02 03`: A3 := Height * 2, in a temporary.
- @283 is `67 03 01 00`: not_less_than(A3, A1), which is `Y =< Height*2`
  with the operands swapped (as `=<` compiles; step 3c). Then the cut,
  and the atom `previous_one` into A2.
- @288-289 are the same with 3 (`48 00 03 03`) for `next_one`.
- The source is release 3200 against the object's 3500 (Inputs, above).

Effect, row by row (MEASURED 2026-11-25):

    What they do is put the reply on the device's output stream. A

  - 10 `pimos_window_editor` replies reach a slot constant:

### Step 3g: pmi c6, ce, c3, c1, ff and the cd03/cd04 tables (2026-11-25)

**The jump-base reading, on predicates whose source is held.** Three
predicates were read cell by cell beside their source:

- `kappa##kappaRDM_manager` local `get_BFS_list_only/2` @6b7
  (kappa2 `kernel/rdm/RDMSOPE.ESP`:285-291: `([],[])`, a list clause,
  `(end,end)`);
- `paragraph##view_selector` local `make_com/3` @46d (`p_MAIN.esp`:479-
  689: twenty atom clauses, seventeen 3-vector clauses, two
  `make_com(Mes,...)` catch-alls);
- `paragraph##receiver` `send/3` @11c (`receiver.esp`:38-686: one atom
  clause `read_code`, vector clauses, the catch-all
  `:send(Obj,Term,Ans):- !,Ans="abnormal"` at :686).

What they settle (each target checked against the clause the source
puts there):

- **pmi c6 R; word W1; word W2** (`switch_on_term`, an INFERENCE
  binding to TM-444's indexing group). The base is the cell after the
  two words, c + 3. A_R unbound goes to base + hi16(W2), the try chain
  over every clause (RDMSOPE @6c0 `e4`; make_com @513, clause 1's
  `c2`). An atom falls through to base, a `cc` chain or a `cd00` table.
  A list goes to base + lo16(W2) (RDMSOPE @6c5, clause 2's `2b`; 9 held
  lo16 targets land on a `2a`/`2b`). A vector goes to base + W1: a `ce`
  (make_com @4bb), or an `ff` to fail where no clause takes a vector
  (RDMSOPE @6bf). MEASURED over the six packages: hi16 lands on
  e4/c4/e2/c2 in all 132 c6.
- **pmi ce R, K; word W** (`switch_on_vector`, OURS). A_R is a vector.
  Element 0 unbound goes to c + 2 + lo16(W), a retry chain over the
  vector clauses in source order (make_com @501: seventeen `c3`, kill
  first). Element 0 an atom: X_K := the key Name/N and fall through, to
  a `cc` chain testing register K or to the table cdKK. **K is a
  register, not an arity**: pimscsi's `ce 00 0b` is followed by
  `cc 0b ...`, and each of 20 cdNN tables directly follows a `ce 00 NN`
  (MEASURED over nine listings: the six source-held packages and
  pimfep, pimscsi, pimmcsp; two more follow a ce with an `e2` between).
  **N is the vector's length**, element 0 included: receiver's cd04
  table has the three keys init/4, init/2 and init/5 for `init(pe_view,
  PE,Wid)` (:669-675, a c5 c3 c3 c1 chain), `init(htree_view)` (:678) and
  `init(htree_view,{Id,Nid},Depth,Wid)` (:680). The key cell is tag 01
  with low byte N (ptfread prints it as a selector). kl0 holds it as a
  `Functor`, interned.
- **pmi c3** is the second encoding of e3 (`retry`, target = c + 2 +
  s16), and **pmi c1** of e1 (`trust`). make_com @501 `c3 00 017d`
  lands on @680, the kill clause's `33 00` just past its `c2`. MEASURED:
  864 c3, preceded by c5 or c3 in 817 and followed by c3 or c1 in 811.
- **pmi ff** (`jump`, OURS) goes to c + 2 + s16. It ends a cc chain
  when no constant matched. RDMSOPE @6bf goes to @6d4 `fe`, make_com
  @512 to @81e (the catch-all), receiver @121 to @b8b (the catch-all).
  Byte A is 00 in all 956 held uses.
- **The table's register** is the low byte of its op (cd00 after c7 R
  00 and c6 R 00; cdKK after ce K). `switch_reg` now reads it there and
  checks the switch before the table. Tables cd03 (make_com @4bd) and
  cd04 (receiver @126) get rows. cd02, cd05 and cd08 stay refused: their
  witnesses are not paired with source.

This also settles the step 3f bounded read of pimscsi `command_code/2`
@361 (Open, now removed). The selector entries were `cc` on register
2 after `ce 00 02`. The row's cell + 3 + s16 fits all three of them:
`sense` reaches @37a, `trace` @372 (a chain: trace has more than one
clause) and `read_trace` @388. The two words were W1 = 5 (@369, the ce)
and W2 = 0x150031 (hi16 @379, the `e4` chain; lo16 @395, `fe`).

**Not witnessed, so refused:**

- c6 on an integer, a string or an object (no held c6 `cc` chain tests
  an integer: MEASURED, 117 chains of atoms only);
- c6 with a non-zero second or third byte (5 held, kappa `assoc/3`,
  `set_part_value/5`, `get_node_ID/2`);
- ce on an element 0 that is neither unbound nor an atom. hi16(W) is
  the miss target in both witnesses, and what else takes it is
  UNSETTLED. pimscsi `check_argument/2` @9b sends it to an `ff`;
- ff with byte A != 0;
- a hash table not directly after c7, c6 or ce (two kappa ce have an
  `e2` between).

Effect, method by method:

- Six packages (`kl0.py census`): 310 / 12 / 207 / 1 becomes 311 / 12 /
  206 / 1. `pimmcsp##pe_registers register_type/3` succeeds; checked
  alone, its unbound first argument takes clause 1 (`nifr`).
- Plain `census --fep`: 807 / 154 / 442 becomes 808 / 163 / 432. In the

  - `pimos_remote_login_server device_abort/1` succeeds (both censuses);

  - 9 fail: pimscsi `cmt`'s and `scsi`'s `check_argument/2`, `low_if/2`,

    check_argument/2`. Not progress. The cause was traced for

  - 6 stop at `not_less_than` on an unbound argument (cmt and scsi
    `send_data/4`, `receive_data/4`, `request/4`).
  - `pimfep_message_log dump_log/1` and `/2` reach `vector_element` on an

  - `pimos_scsi_front_end exit/1` reaches `process:children/2`.
  - 9 more stop later at another opcode (blt e1, blt a8, blt 05, pmi
    34), and none stops at c6 any more (21 before).
  - None leaves success.

Tests (`test_kl0.py` `Step3gSwitches`):

### Step 3g: `process:invocation_stack/2` (2026-11-25)

`standin/process.esp` gains `:invocation_stack(Process, Stack)`, the
list object itself. It is INTERPOLATED in the same way as the slot
accessors, from:

- the printed slot `invocation_stack`, a list (TM-100 Fig. 3.1, PDF
  p8);
- the held source kappa-p.tar.Z `src/emu/kappaPITR.esp`:13-14,
  `:self(#process, Proc), :get_first(Proc!invocation_stack, Prog)`,
  whose `Prog!kappa_in_stream` shows that the first element is the
  calling program;
- the held compiled caller `pimos_tcpip_socket create_socket/9`
  @4d-59, the same sequence through a method.

That it answers the list object and not a copy is OURS.

Effect: tcpip still has no recipe. A fresh census process runs no
program, so `get_first/2` fails on its empty stack and the creator
fails. A recipe would have to push the front end's program, which the
census does not run. In both censuses one row moves:
`pimos_tcpip_socket create_socket/9` now stops at `process:status/3`
(inside activate/2, like the other creators), not at
`invocation_stack/2`. That is a census order effect: the census's one
process is shared, and an earlier row's `assign/1` left a program on
its stack. Alone, on a fresh machine, the creator fails at
`get_first/2`. Totals are unchanged. Test (`test_espc.py`
`test_step3g_invocation_stack`): on the empty stack the creator fails.
With an `as_program` pushed, `invocation_stack/2` answers the stack,
and the held creator runs to activate/2 with that program as the
device's `fep_object`, the process as `fep_process` and device id 16.

Injected into the stand-in from a backup, restored by cp, cmp-checked.

### Step 3h: the six pimscsi failures, traced (2026-11-25)

Step 3g left six new failures untraced: `low_if/2`, `send_request/5`
and `receive_request/5` of `cmt` and of `scsi`. Each was run alone on a
fresh machine with a trace (MEASURED 2026-11-25). All six are **held
behaviour**, not census artifacts.

- Each method lists its arguments for scsi_communicator
  `check_arguments/2` (@130-147). That checks each element with
  `check_argument/2` (@93-10d), and on a failure it sets `status :=
  software`, `detail := arguments` (@13f-146) and fails (`fe`, @147).
- All three list the atom `initiator` (scsi_low_if `low_if/2` @f1-f5).
  Its clause (@b7-bd) unifies `Obj!lun` with `initiator`.
- The receiver is new/2's instance, so `lun` is 0, its template
  constant. 0 is a target's lun: the `target` clause (@be-c4) is
  `integer(Obj!lun)`. scsi_common_if `create/4` writes an integer there
  (@46-49), `create/3` writes `initiator` (@22-25).
- An scsi left with lun 0 is a state held code makes. scsi_handler
  `initiate_device/7` makes its scsi with `:new` and writes no lun
  (@3cb-3d3: new/2, set_device/2, add_at/3). So does `shutdown_device/4`
  (@413-41c). So the six rows are initiator methods sent to a target.
  They fail as the held code says.

The step 3g README and test comment that put this down to "the census's
unbound first argument" were right only for `check_argument/2`.

**No recipe.** A recipe through `create/3` (unit 0, as scsi
`initialize/1` @2b-30 passes it) was tried and dropped. It only swaps
the stops: the target methods (`send_data/4`, `receive_data/4`,
`transfer/6`, scsi `receive_command/3`) then fail at `target`, and the
initiator methods stop at the unit check. Which mode the census should
pick would be OURS.

**The 6 `not_less_than`-on-unbound stops** (cmt and scsi `send_data/4`,
`receive_data/4`, `request/4`): the `unit` clause (@c5-ce) tests `0 =<
Unit =< 7` on the census's unbound unit. They are artifacts of unbound
probing and stay refused (fail loud). The held callers pass a run-time
value:

- pimfep `scsi_stream`'s local `send_data_to_handler/4` (@3aa-3b3) and
  `receive_data_from_handler/4` (@354-35d) pass their own arguments
  through (from `send_data/5` @244-25b and `refute_internal_command/4`).
- cmt's own `test_unit_ready/1`, `inquiry/1` and `request_sense/2` pass
  `Obj!cmt_unit` and `Obj!cmt_lun` (`$or$4/2` @119-125, `$or$5/2`
  @12e-13a, `$or$6/3` @143-14e). Those rows are in the census already.

No single held constant is the unit, so none is invented.

Test (`test_espc.py` `test_step3h_scsi_mode`): a pin of held behaviour.

- With `lun := initiator`, written by the held `set_lun/2`, they get

### Step 3h: blt e1, `integer/1` as a test-and-branch (2026-11-25)

The census's commonest missing opcode (7 recipe rows, 14 six-package
rows). It was listed as seen only in `$or$` predicates whose source is
not held. In fact 16 of its 156 uses in the source-held packages are in
paragraph, and a `$or$`'s source is the disjunction in its parent
clause.

**KNOWN-PLAINTEXT** (release 3200 source against the 3500 object):

- `what_manipulator` `$or$3/2` @53a `e1 00 00 01`, then `74 01 02
  select/2`. That is p_what.esp:87-89 `(Code==exit,!; integer(Code),!,
  :select(W,Code); Code==previous_page,!, ...`. The `==exit` test is
  @537-539. The else lands on @53d = @53a + 2 + 1, the `previous_page`
  test.
- `g_window` `$or$12/2` @704 `e1 00 00 03`. That is g_window.esp:147-149
  `( integer(N),!, :get_number_string(#symbolizer,N,P1); atom(N),!,
  :get_atom_string(#symbolizer,N,P1))`. The integer branch @705-708
  ends in an execute. The else lands on @709 = @704 + 2 + 3, the `atom`
  test (blt 00, not rowed).
- A != 0: `view_selector` `$or$11/5` @a29 `e1 01 00 03`. That is
  p_MAIN.esp:723 `(integer(N),N>Max,!,F1=1,N1=Max;F1=F,N1=N)`, with N in
  A1. The else lands on @a2e-a2f, `F1=F` and `N1=N`.

So e1 is blt 01 (`integer/1`, which fails) as a test-and-branch: go on
if A_A is an integer, else jump to cell + 2 + u8, as f0 does for `==`.
That 0xe0 | n is the branch form of blt n is a NAMING INFERENCE only.
blt e0 (151 held uses) beside blt 00 (`atom`, unrowed) fits it, but
neither is added. The second byte is 0 in all 208 held uses; a
non-zero one refuses.

Effect, row by row (MEASURED 2026-11-25):

- Six packages: totals unchanged (311 / 12 / 206 / 1). 14 rows stop
  later:
  - 10 `csp_pe_control` rows go on to a slot write (`Obj!start := on`,
    @1af; `mpc`, @1f3 onwards) on a receiver whose class has no slots.
    The class has no natures and no slots of its own, so the slots must
    come from a class that inherits it. That is a census artifact of
    instantiating it alone.
  - 4 `state_pe` rows go on to blt f4 (unrowed).
- Plain `census --fep`: 808 / 165 / 430 becomes 809 / 165 / 429. With
  recipes, 831 / 163 / 409 becomes 832 / 163 / 408. The same seven rows
  move:
  - `pimos_fep_initiation_table set_scsi_init_list/2` succeeds. Checked
    alone: the census's unbound list becomes four unbound elements.
    Each is not an integer, so each is stored as `nil` (@149-152).
    Unbound probing, not progress.
  - cmt `test_unit_ready/1`, `inquiry/1` and `request_sense/2` go on to
    a pool constant of kind `raw` (`$or$2/2` @e6, `pmi 29 01 ff 29`, the
    else of its `e1`), which kl0 does not model.
  - cmt and scsi `transfer/6` go on to blt e0.
  - `mbus_allocator allocate_without_check/4` goes on to a SIMPOS class
    not loaded.

Test (`test_kl0.py` `test_if_integer`). Each case runs the held
`$or$` predicate:
- `exit` succeeds;
- 5 reaches `select/2`, sent to the unbound W;
- `previous_page` reaches `previous_page/1`;
- an unbound Code does not reach `select/2`;
- in view_selector, `t` takes `F1=F, N1=N` and 3 reaches blt f4.

### Step 3h: a recipe for `pimos_tcpip_socket` (2026-11-25)

`create_socket/9` (@21-69) takes the calling program from the top of
its process's invocation stack: `:self(#process,P)`,
`:invocation_stack(P,L)`, `:get_first(L,Prog)` (@4d-59). It stores it
as the device's `fep_object`, with the process as `fep_process`. A0
goes to `set_output_stream/2` (@3d-40), A3 to `set_device_id/2`
(@49-4c).

**Which program is on top: the front end (INFERENCE).**
- The device creators are reached from `pimos_scsi_front_end`'s local
  `create_device/4` (@409; `fep_services.md` §6.1), through
  `refute_command/3` (@30b) and the local `loop` (@2bd).
- The front end's `goal/1` runs `loop` in its own process: `tag_catch/9`
  with the atom `loop` (@c5-c9). The supervisor gets a new process and
  `activate/2` (@a4-b9).
- A program's goal runs with the program pushed on its process's stack.
  TM-100 PDF p11: "On invocation of a program, the program is pushed
  onto the invocation stack of the process and the instance predicate
  :goal(Program) is called" (`as_program` `assign/1`, Fig. 3.3).
- Held code also reads the socket's `fep_object` again: `do_accept/3`
  (@419-424) passes it to the accepted socket's `set_socket/5`.

**The recipe** (kl0 `_tcpip_socket`, OURS):
1. push a front end with the printed `assign/1`;
2. run `create_socket/9` with a new stream in A0 and `REQUEST_ID` in A3,
   stopped before `activate/2`, as the other creators are;
3. pop the front end with the printed `resign_core/1`, so the census
   process's stack is left as it was found.

The front end is new/2's instance (OURS): its creator `create/6` is not
run.

Effect, row by row (MEASURED 2026-11-25). The plain census is unchanged
(it uses no recipes). With recipes, 833 / 163 / 407 becomes 836 / 164 /
403, and slot constants go from 96 to 93. Five rows move, each checked
alone on a fresh machine:

- `attention/1`, `bind/4` and `flush/3` succeed. Each puts its reply on
  the output stream, as the timer's do (`attention/1` puts `[16, 0, 3,
  0]`). They were slot constants or `set_string_element` on an unbound
  Id before. These are held reply paths given an OURS request, not a
  running front end.
- `abort_loop/1` fails: `try_to_see/2` (@30e) on the empty input
  stream fails, as stood in.
- `loop_main/1` waits in `see/2` (`would suspend`), as the other devices'
  do.

`abort_getb/3` and `abort_accept/3` still stop at `set_string_element`.
Their reply `{Id, X}` has an unbound X, which the census does not give.
`create_socket/9` itself (a class row) still stops at
`process:status/3`.

Test (`test_espc.py` `test_step3h_tcpip_recipe`):
- the socket's `fep_object` is a front end, its `fep_process` the census
  process, its device id 16 and its output stream a stream;
- the stack is empty again after the recipe;
- `attention/1` puts `[16, 0, 3, 0]`.

`test_step3f_census_recipes` pins the totals, the 93 slot constants, and
the 18 `would suspend` rows.

Injected from a backup, restored by cp, cmp-checked. Observed:
- No push: `test_step3h_tcpip_recipe` errors and
  `test_step3f_census_recipes` fails.
- No pop: only `test_step3h_tcpip_recipe` fails. No census row depends
  on the stack being emptied.

### Step 3i: the census made order-independent (2026-11-25)

Step 3h left `pimfep_message_log dump_log/1` and `/2` as "a census
order effect". The shared state is the machine's **class slots**. Until
step 3i every census row ran on one `Machine`, so a class slot written by
one row was read by every later row. The old code was run on a fresh
machine, writer row then reader row (MEASURED 2026-11-25):

- `set_log_table/2`, called with the census's unbound argument, sets
  `log_table` to that variable. `dump_log/1` and `/2` then stop at
  `vector_element on _G`. Alone, `log_table` is its template constant
  `none`.
- `log/2` makes the table, and `set_position/2` sets `position` to an
  unbound variable. `initialize/2` then stops at `index on _G`.
- `assign/1` rows push their program on the one census process, which
  is kept in `process`'s class slot `current`. Later `resign/1` and
  `resign_core/1` rows pop it and succeed. Alone, the stack is empty and
  they fail.

**The change (OURS).** `kl0.census_of` drops the class slots before
every row. Class objects then take their initial values again on first
access (TM-388 p.49), and each row gets a new census process. A row's
outcome is what it is alone on a fresh machine. The one-row body is now
`census_row/6`.

**Checked.** Every row of both FEP censuses was run alone on its own new
`fep_machine()`: 1,420 plain rows and 1,420 recipe rows. Each agrees with
the census (0 differences; the script is not kept).

**Effect, row by row (MEASURED 2026-11-25).**

- Six packages: identical, row by row (311 / 12 / 206 / 1).
- Plain FEP: 810 / 165 / 428 becomes **757 / 219 / 427** (exception 16,
  step limit 1). 70 rows move, all order effects:
  - 54 `resign/1` and `resign_core/1` rows go from success to failure:
    the row's own process has an empty program stack.
  - `pimos_socket_manager_table` and `pseudo_scsi` `locked/1` fail: no
    earlier `lock/1`.
  - `pimos_tcpip_socket create_socket/9` fails at `get_first/2` (@4d-59):
    its process's invocation stack is empty. Before, an earlier row's
    program was on top and it went on to `status/3`. The recipe pushes a
    front end first (step 3h).
  - `pseudo_scsi shutdown/1` goes from success to refused (built-in
    `class_object` of the integer 1900544). `initialize/2` and
    `pseudo_scsi_handler send_to_PE/2` change their detail.
  - `pimos_remote_login_replier` `io/4`, `io/5` and `do_io/6` go from
    failure to refused, at `class_object/2` (no primary in the held
    classes; the stand-in `as_program` is an ancestor).
  - `pimfep_message_log`: `initialize/2`, `dump_log/2`, `sorted_log/2`
    and `sorted_log_string/2` succeed, and `dump_log/1`, `sorted_log/1`
    and `sorted_log_string/1` stop at `pmacs_window:putl/2` (not stood
    in). The four successes are paths on an empty log (`log_table` is
    `none`); run after `initialize/2` the sorted ones reach
    `sorted_sequence:create/2`. Not progress.
- With recipes: 836 / 164 / 403 becomes **782 / 220 / 401**, the same
  order effects (55 resign rows; `io/4` was already refused). Slot
  constants go from 93 to 94: `pseudo_scsi_handler send_to_PE/2` used to
  meet an earlier row's unbound value.

None of the drop is lost progress. The successes removed were each
produced by a row that ran earlier.

Test (`test_espc.py` `test_step3i_census_order`): the census run
backwards (classes and each class's methods reversed) equals the forward
one row for row, plain and with recipes; and `dump_log/1` reaches
`pmacs_window:putl/2`. `test_fep_census` and
`test_step3f_census_recipes` are re-pinned.

Injected from a backup (the reset line removed), restored by cp,
cmp-checked. Observed: `test_step3i_census_order`, `test_fep_census` and
`test_step3f_census_recipes` fail; `test_kl0.py` passes.

### Step 3i: the boot process (2026-11-25)

The first step on the boot route (`../docs/simpos/boot_route.md`, B2).
TM-100 p6 and p13 and TR-056 p21 say SIMPOS starts in one process, the
boot process, which runs a boot program (`as_boot_program`) and whose
class overrides `:create` and `:activate`. The held class
`pimmcsp##pseudo_pe_process` (a `process` subclass) names it: its
`release/1` (@177-189) sends `:get_instance(#boot_process, B)` and
then `:request(B, release, Self)`.

**Stand-ins.**
- `standin/boot_process.esp` (INTERPOLATED): `nature process`, a class
  slot `instance`, `get_instance/2` (the running process, if it is the
  boot process) and `boot/2` (make it once, status `active`). The
  selector `boot/2` is OURS.
- `standin/as_boot_program.esp` (INTERPOLATED, TM-100 p13): `nature
  as_program`, no methods of its own.
- `standin/process.esp` `self/2`: the census's and the tests' first
  process is now made by `boot/2`, so it is the boot process.
- `kl0.py`: `boot_process`'s own `create/2`, `create/3` and
  `activate/2` are refused (`boot_process overrides :create and
  :activate ... the bodies are not printed`). A model keyed on a class
  now also serves its subclasses (`dispatch` walks the inheritance
  order, stopping at an ancestor that defines the method).

**`status/3` has a second held call site.** Step 3h searched source
text and found only TM-100's `:status(Process, dormant, on_activate)`.
The compiled `pseudo_pe_process release/1` sends `:status(Self,
dormant, on_release)` (@17a-17e). `status/3` stays refused: there is
still no definition, and the only held `status/3` methods are on
`pimscsi`'s `pseudo_scsi` and `scsi_communicator`, which are not
processes.

Effect, row by row (MEASURED 2026-11-25): six packages identical; plain

### Step 3j: the supervisor, route step B3 (2026-11-25)

`../docs/simpos/boot_route.md` B3: the boot process comes under the
supervisor, and a second process runs. Step 3i left four gates, none
with a printed or held body: `process:status/3`, `entry_of_invoke/2`,
class `context`, and the dispatcher. Step 3j stands each in, as
INTERPOLATED or (where marked) OURS. It adds three more that the printed
`activate/2` and the boot process's overrides need: `clear_masks/1`,
and `boot_process` `create/2` and `activate/2`.

**Bases gathered first.**
- Page images re-read on 2026-11-25:
  - TM-100 PDF p5-p13 (§2.1, Fig. 3.1 to 3.4, the boot process).
  - TR-056 PDF p18-p21 (folios 16-19). This gives the four states;
    "the dispatcher and the scheduler"; the limit of 63 processes;
    activation, which places an ordinary process "in the ready queue by
    the supervisor"; resume, which "calls the software scheduler"; and
    the boot process.
  - TR-055 PDF p5 (folio 2): 63 contexts, four stacks, "process
    dispatching must be controlled by software".
  - TR-285 PDF p11 (folio 8): resources are released on termination by
    one method.
- A sweep of the compiled cells of all eleven held packages.
  - Selectors swept: `status/3`, `entry_of_invoke/2`,
    `set_entry_point/2`, `clear_masks/1`, `set_status/2`,
    `get_process/3`, `alive/1`, `add_child/2`, `context/2`,

    `assign/1`, `resign/1`, `terminate_core/1`, `create_core/4`. Classes

    not kept.
  - New: two more `status/3` sends, in `pimmcsp##pseudo_pe_process`
    `$or$5/2` @3a9-3bc (reached from `terminate_core/1`). Step 3h's
    source-text search could not see them.
  - There is no `entry_of_invoke/2`, `set_entry_point/2` or

  - kappa2 `kernel/itr/ITRSES.ESP`:111-116, and

- Also read for this step: the four other documents in the brief. Each

  - TR-087 PDF p4 (§3.2). "A process executes a given program, which is
    an instance of a program class. The main goal of the program is
    defined as an instance predicate". Also, a stream's "retrieve
    operation is suspended until some object is put". These support the
    entry's `{Program, invoke}` reaching the program's goal, and a
    `get/2` that suspends. Nothing on status, contexts or the
    dispatcher.
  - TR-059 PDF p30 (folio 28, §5). A device handler is "an interrupt
    process", which "is dispatched differently from ordinary processes".
    The hardware dispatches it on an interrupt; a message to its channel
    "raises a trap to activate" it; finished, it "releases the processor
    so as to re-dispatch the other processes". Device handlers are
    "instantiated and activated during a system boot-up procedure". This
    concerns the file system's disk handlers, not the FEP device
    processes. The dispatcher here runs the FEP device processes as
    ordinary processes (OURS). Whether they were interrupt processes is
    not settled.
  - TM-388 PDF p18 (§1). The class slot and class method exist to limit
    or manage instance generation, for instance when "a limited number of
    resources" is defined. This supports `context`'s class-slot counter
    and its 63 limit as a pattern. It says nothing about SIMPOS
    contexts.
  - TM-115 (the window manual). Nothing on processes, status, contexts
    or resources; its "dispatch" is input dispatching to windows.

**The dispatcher (OURS).**
- Each process that is not running keeps its registers in
  `Machine.saved`: pc, argument registers, environment, continuation,
  choice points and trail.
- The running process gives up the processor when it is no longer
  ready:
  - after the printed `suspend/2` has made it suspended (a `yield` step
    after the printed body);
  - when its program has ended (an `exit` step under its first goal).
    A choice point at its base means a program that fails also ends the
    process.
- The next process is the first ready one in the ready queue, other
  than the one leaving. A process started for the first time begins at
  its context's entry, as `:invoke(Program)`.
- The running process is kept in the class slot `current`, which
  `self/2` reads.
- If no process is ready, the dispatcher refuses `would suspend` ("no
  process is ready").
- There is no preemption and no time slice, and priority is ignored.
- A process the supervisor does not know still refuses `would suspend`
  at once, as before. Such a process has a status that is neither
  `ready` nor `suspended`; the census's process (the boot process before
  its own `create` and `activate`) is one.
- The census drops `Machine.saved`, along with the class slots, before
  every row.

**Tests** (`test_espc.py`):
- `test_step3j_supervisor`. A probe boot program (OURS), shaped like
  the kappa2 handshake above, comes under the supervisor. It makes a
  stream, a child program and a process, activates the process and
  `:get`s. It checks:
  - in the child, `:self(#process, P)` is the child's process, its
    program is on top of that process's stack, and its parent is the
    boot process;
  - the boot process gets `created`, and is `ready` afterwards;
  - the child has died (`nil`, stack empty);
  - the contexts are numbered 0 and 1, and the child's is `active`
    with entry `{Program, invoke}`;
  - `status/3` sets on a match and fails on a mismatch, so a second
    `activate/2` fails;
  - the held `pseudo_pe_process release/1`, on a dormant PE process,
    passes `status/3` and stops at `boot_process:request/3`. With
    status `nil` it fails (step 3k: it now runs to its end, "Step 3k");
  - a boot program that waits on an empty stream, with nothing ready,
    is refused `would suspend`;
  - the 64th context is not made.
- `test_step3j_fep_device`. The held `pimos_timer create_timer/9` runs
  to its end under the supervisor:
  - it activates the device process, the device process runs its held
    `goal/1` and puts `normal`, and the creator replies `{16, 1}`;
  - the device process then waits in `see/2` on its input stream,
    suspended, with the device on top of its stack;
  - a request (OURS: close, code 60, a 32-bit string) is taken by the
    device process and stops at blt 6f in `fep_message_assembler
    disassemble/3` @a9 (step 3k: it now round-trips, "Step 3k").

  `b4_timer` is an OURS subclass of the held class. Its `create/2` also
  records the device, so the probe can reach the device's input stream.
- `test_step3j_as_resource`. Before `assign/1`, the held `pseudo_scsi
  close/1` takes its `not_assigned` path. After it, `close/1` takes the
  held path: status `closed`, then `resign/1`, which leaves the
  process's `resources` empty again.
- `test_step3i_boot`, re-pinned. Only `create/3` refuses as an
  override. A plain process's `activate/2` now stops at the ready
  queue, which is missing because the boot process has not come under
  the supervisor.

    `test_step3j_fep_device` shows the same creator running to its end

    `send_data/4`, `receive_data/4`, `aborted/1` and
    `receive_command/3`. The path stops at `class_object` of 1900544, a
    pimscsi pool term (the pimscsi pools are not relied on). This is
    not progress.

  Totals: **801 / 233 / 415** (exception 16, step limit 1). Slot
  constants go from 143 to 154. `process:status/3` is no longer a stop.
- With recipes: the same 46 rows and the same 16 changes. Totals are
  **826 / 234 / 389**, and slot constants go from 94 to 105. The 18
  `would suspend` rows stay; their detail now says the process is not
  under the supervisor.

No held row moved to success, and none is claimed as progress. The
progress is what the census cannot show: two processes running, and a
held creator completing its handshake with the device process it made
(`test_step3j_fep_device`).

**The dialogue reached** (`test_step3m_local_login`):

```
Enter FEP node name--------->   local
Enter your PIMOS user name-->   pimos
Enter your PIMOS password--->   (not echoed)
Your request is accepted.
Login procedure starts.
OK. Your initial program starts.<000a>
Good luck!<000a>

```

The server's answer to the held `io(1)`, NI, equals the output of
`ref/pimm/fep.py` `login_record(id=0, node='local', rn='pmpsi',
rp='pmpsi', un='pimos', up='')`, compared in the test by a read-only
import. It does not equal `fep.LOGIN`, whose RP is `''`. Both values
are OURS, and they differ in RP only (`boot_route.md` §6.3).

**Where it stops.** The requester has done its work and waits for a
key, as does every other process. The probe called the server's and the
replier's held `io/4` directly. The real path goes through
`fep_message_assembler`'s 620 frames, and that still stops at
`substring/7` from 16 to 32 bits (@122, unwitnessed). No clock is
modelled, so no timeout fires.

  - The editor's `get_title/3` moves from blt 36 to the same
    unwitnessed substring/7 width.
  - The other three `scsi_handler` rows and `packet free/1` move as in
    the plain census.
- Slot constants are unchanged (157 plain, 110 with recipes). The step's
  progress is in its two tests, not in the census.

### Step 3n: the held requester logs into PIMOS through the held front end (2026-11-25)

Step 3m's probe is replaced by PIMOS itself. PIMOS runs on
`ref/pimm/pimm.py` from the IPL's link checkpoint. The PSI side is the
held `pimos_scsi_front_end` on kl0. `fep_bridge.py` (OURS) is the
bridge between them. The boundary and the message table are in
`../docs/simpos/boot_route.md` §6.5, the transcript in §4.6, and every
inference in `../docs/simpos/source_check_2026-11-24.md` §13.

**The transcript in the requester's window** (all PIMOS's texts come
from `login.kl1` through the held replier):
`Enter FEP node name--------->` `local`, `Enter your PIMOS user
name-->` `pimos`, `Enter your PIMOS password--->`, `Your request is
accepted.`, `Login procedure starts.`, `OK. Your initial program
starts.<LF>`, `Good luck!<LF>`, then two beeps. PIMOS's shell window is
still `fep.py`'s. With `pwd` typed there it shows `Shell>> `, ` The value
of task: directory is ">sys>user>pmpsi" . <LF>` and `Shell>> `, the
pinned `lockstep_shell.APPS['pwd']`.

**What kl0 needed** (each labelled at its place):
- `substring/7` from 16 to 32 bits and back (INFERENCE: the high half
  first; U = 0, V = N).
- A 32-bit string element read signed, and `get_integer`'s immediate
  sign-extended (INFERENCE, `take_argument/5` @17b-17d).
- Rows blt 30 `subvector` and pmi 36 `get_vector_6`
  (KNOWN-PLAINTEXT), and blt 0d `object/3` (INFERENCE: a type test; its
  outputs are left unbound). `kl0_opcodes.tsv` has 156 rows.
- The idle interrupt (OURS): when every process waits, the bridge's
  packets are put on the front end's stream with the printed `put/2`.
- `exterminate/1` (`standin/process.esp`, INTERPOLATED). A process that
  kills itself gives up the processor (OURS).
- `world root_path/3` and `pathname remote_or_local/6` (kl0 models,
  INFERENCE).

**Stand-ins**:
- New: `abort_handler`, `tcpip_network_subsystem` (is_installed fails:
  no Ethernet, OURS), `monogyny_list_index` (one object per key,
  INFERENCE).
- Added to: `process` (`set_area_limit_execution/7`, `set_package/2`,
  `exterminate/1`), `stream` (`grant_stream/2`) and `world`
  (`default_logical_name/2`), each INTERPOLATED.
- Corrected: `string append/3` takes the first string's element size,
  and `list_index` keeps its entries in heap vectors. The front end's
  failure-driven loop had undone them.

**RP.** It stays `pmpsi`, the PSI user's registered password (OURS,
step 3l). The held requester sends it, and the held
`simpos_user_device` identifies with it. Both values, this one and
`fep.LOGIN`'s `''` (not changed), are still unsourced.

**Tests** (`test_fep_bridge.py`, 4 in the suite in 4 s, 1 opt-in):
- `Assembler`: the held assemble/3 equals `fep.pack_message` on five
  messages. Disassemble/3 decodes eight packets of PIMOS's.
- `Scripted`: PIMOS's messages of the pinned run fed to the PSI alone.
  The answers are `fep.py`'s, then the NI.
- `Tape`: the live run's 12 exchanges replayed. The window is checked,
  and the shell texts against `APPS['pwd']`.
- `test_live` (`FEP_BRIDGE_PKL=...`): the live run against the tape.
  Measured: 38 s, 15.5 GB resident.

Eleven injections were made against `test_fep_bridge.py`, each into
the file from a backup copy, copied back, and checked with cmp. The
observed failures are in the test's header. Nine are discriminated
there. Two change nothing on this path, as traced: blt 0d always
failing (it meets only the atom `nil` here) and `exterminate/1` not
yielding (each dying process calls it last). They are discriminated by
new unit tests:
- `test_kl0.py` `Step3nRows`: `get_vector_6`, `object/3` and
  `subvector` on held code, with four injections.
- `test_espc.py` `test_step3n_exterminate`: a process that kills itself
  runs nothing after.
- `test_espc.py` `test_step3n_monogyny_list_index`: `put_at/3`
  replaces.
Each has its observed failures in its header.

**Census, recomputed row by row** against 2eda555c (MEASURED
2026-11-25, `_G` numbering normalised):
- **Six packages:** 319 / 12 / 198 becomes **322 / 12 / 195**.
  paragraph `graph_status_element create/3`, `get/2` and `set/2` go from
  refused at pmi 36 to success. That is unbound probing past an opcode

  (`test_espc.py` `test_census`) goes from 98 / 86 / 40 / 3 to

  - blt 0d's 10 rows go on. 3 succeed and 1 fails. Traced: in these

- **With recipes:** 832 / 235 / 384 becomes **837 / 239 / 375**. 17

### Window M0, M3, M5: the Login PIMOS window drawn by a live run (2026-11-25)

The owner's goal: "keep going until you can connect them", that is, a
picture of the Login PIMOS window that comes from a live run of the
held code, not from a script that types the strings. The plan is
`../docs/simpos/window_environment_plan.md` (§4.1 architecture, §5
milestones); the look is `../docs/simpos/subsystems/window.md` §4.2-4.3.

**Who draws what.**

| layer | file | label | what it does on the route |
|---|---|---|---|
| held code | `pimfep##pimos_login_requester` (pimfep.ptf) | HELD | every send: `create/3` with `{size,{ch,40},{ch,7}}`, `:create(#font,"font:test_11.font",F)`, `set_font/2`, `set_title/2` (its `title_string/1`), `activate/1`, `clear/1`, `show/1`, `putl/2` of its pool prompts and of PIMOS's replies, `read/2`, `getl/2`, `inhibit_echo/1` |
| window stand-in | `standin/standard_io_window.esp` | OURS bodies, HELD selectors | keeps the step 3l/3m state (transcript, history, echo) and hands the drawing to its pane |
| window manager | `standin/window_pane.esp` (new) | OURS (class and body); the look cited per item in its head | rectangles, the 2-dot border, the label strip and rule, glyph positions, wrap, scroll, key#lf/cr, the block cursor of the selected window, erase |
| font | `standin/font.esp` | OURS body, HELD selector; file HELD | `create/3` retrieves the file and asks the device to decode it |
| device | `windev.py` (new), reached by `SIMPOS_MODELS` | OURS | `simpos##bitmap_display` `fill/6`, `glyph/6`, `copy/8`, `line/7`, `load_font/5`, `default_font/4`, `get_size/3`: each one winfb command on one frame per machine, logged |
| frame | `winfb.py` (unchanged) | OURS, frozen | the dots |
| file system | `simpos_fs.py` | placement HELD, directory `>sys>font` and alias `font` OURS | FONT.DIR's seven files under `font:` |

**Why the window manager is ESP.** The plan §4.1 recommends it: the held
classes subclass the window stand-ins and hang demons on their
primaries, so the window logic must be ESP in the inheritance order; and
on the PSI/UX the SIMPOS window manager stayed in SIMPOS and an outside
"window server" only drew (Giho 9106 p86, PROSE). So Python is kept below
the primitive line, and what a live run drew is exactly a winfb command
stream: the test replays the device's log through `winfb.run_stream` and
gets the same frame, which is also what `../bsv/WinFbBox.bsv`'s bench and
the hardware plan's UART consume. One cost, when it was written: espc
refused `X*Y` on two registers, so the pane keeps the cursor's dot
position beside its cell and multiplies by repeated addition. (espc
emits `X*Y` as blt 42 since 2026-11-25, section "espc's register cache
after an overwrite" below; the pane is unchanged.)

**The tests** (`test_espc.py`, class `Window`):

| test | what it pins |
|---|---|
| `test_win_m0_font` | the seven `>sys>font` files; test_11's bytes equal the archive's; `font:test_11.font` → id 0, cell 11×14; `test_11b` → id 1 (same cell, other glyphs: not drawable by the frozen command set); `test_10` → id 2, 10×11; `font:font_13.font` (no file) and `font:csp.icon` (not f822) fail `create/3`; the decoded 'A' is 48 dots |
| `test_win_pane` | a 10×3 pane alone: geometry, the empty frame's dots, the label cut to the strip, wrap, scroll, key#lf/cr, the 0xffff box for a missing code (status MISSING), the cursor erased by xor on deselect, clear, replay, erase to an empty frame |
| `test_win_m5_login_screen` | the live run at three stages (below): place and size, the cursor's cell, the label `Pimos remote login N` (N the process's context number), the text read back from the frame by glyph matching (`windev.read_cells`), nothing drawn outside the window, every command OK, the PBM sha256 and dot count, the stream's replay, the PNG round trip |

The pinned screens (the frame read back; `#` is the block cursor):

| stage | keys | text | sha256 (PBM), dots |
|---|---|---|---|
| name | `local` | `Enter FEP node name--------->` / `local` / `Enter your PIMOS user name-->` / `#` | `d134cbfa…`, 5,196 |
| password | `local`, `pimos`, then `sec` without RETURN | the above, `pimos`, `Enter your PIMOS password--->`, `#` | `380d5779…`, 6,265: the same frame as with no password keys, so nothing was echoed |
| login | `local`, `pimos`, `` (3m probe replies) | `Your request is accepted.` / `Login procedure starts.` / `OK. Your initial program starts.` / `` / `Good luck!` / `` / `#` | `c4e83dfd…`, 5,959 |

`python3 -I psi/winshot.py OUTDIR` writes the three PNGs and the bridged
one (1,280×1,024, 1 bit; 1.5-1.8 KB each) and checks them against the pins;
`../docs/simpos/screens/` holds the password screen and the bridged
run's final screen.

**Choices, labelled** (each also in its file):
- OURS: the window is centred on the 1,280×944 screen for the held
  `{position, manipulator}` (no mouse is modelled); the label strip is
  one cell plus 2 dots and drawn in the window's font; glyphs are drawn
  opaque (`copy`); wrap at the last column; an echoed RETURN is a
  newline; kill clears the rectangle (no save areas until M7; since
  M7 it restores what was under it);
  `default_font/4` answers test_11 for a window drawn before
  `set_font/2` (font_13 is not held).
- INFERENCE: key#lf in a string breaks the line and key#cr returns
  (window.md §2.3); output scrolls up one row past the last; the cell is
  the font's space character (TM-115 p16-17, PROSE); a missing font
  file fails `create/3`; the screen is 1,280×944 (TM-785, kl1graph).
- PRINTED-INTERFACE / SCREENSHOT behind the look: border 2 (TM-125 p15),
  margins 1 (TM-125 p16), white screen, black text, thin borders, the
  label strip with its rule, the solid block cursor (window.md §4.2-4.3).
- The 0x000a at the end of two PIMOS replies (3m's probe texts) is a
  line break, so each is followed by a blank line.

**A compiler fault found on the way.** In `window_pane create/7`,
`Pane!iw := Iw, Pane!ih := Ih, Pane!w := Iw + 6, Pane!h := Ih + Ch + 9`
compiled `Pane!h`'s sum from a register that `put r0, w` (the slot name
of the assignment before it) had just overwritten: espc kept `Ih` cached
in r0 after the slot-name put reused r0. kl0 refused (`add on w`), so it
was not silent here; the pane passed the sizes through a local instead.
The fault was in `espc.py`'s register cache; it is fixed (section
"espc's register cache after an overwrite", below), and `create/7` now
assigns the sizes directly (the local `sizes/3` is gone; the screens'
pins are unchanged).

**Injections, observed** (2026-11-25; each from a backup copy, restored
by copy, `cmp` clean; the full test_espc.py and test_kl0.py run each
time; test_kl0 OK in all):
- W1 (`window_pane` draws every glyph at the row's left edge: putl
  ignoring the cursor), W3 (`windev` keeps a code's low byte: a wrong
  glyph mapping), W5 (a 1-dot border, the plan's M5 injection), W9 (the
  cursor drawn by `set`, never erased): the three live stages and
  `test_win_pane` FAIL, nothing else.
- W2 (the title not drawn): the three live stages FAIL, nothing else.
- W4 (scroll one row early: a scroll off by one): the `login` stage and
  `test_win_pane` FAIL. Only the last stage reaches the last row.
- W6 (the echo drawn with echo inhibited: the password shown): the
  `password` stage FAILS, nothing else.
- W7 (the cell's width and height swapped): `test_win_m0_font`, the
  three stages and `test_win_pane` FAIL.
- W8 (no `font` alias): `test_win_m0_font` ERRORS; the three stages,
  `test_step3l_b8_prompt`, `test_step3m_keyboard` and
  `test_step3m_local_login` FAIL (the requester's `font:create/3` fails,
  so its `initiate/1` does).
- After the step 3n merge, `test_win_m5_bridged_screen` (below) was run
  against W1-W9 with `test_fep_bridge.py`: it FAILS in all but W6, whose
  password is not on the final screen; W8 also fails test_fep_bridge's
  Scripted and Tape tests.

**Census, recomputed row by row** against 9a1a5e52 (MEASURED
2026-11-25, `_G` numbering normalised; no row new or gone), then the
step 3n merge's pins re-run:
- **Six packages:** 319 / 12 / 198, unchanged, no row moved.
- **Plain FEP** and **with recipes:** outcome totals unchanged (806 /
  234 / 411 and 832 / 235 / 384 before the 3n merge; 810 / 238 / 403 and
  837 / 239 / 375 after it, master's own figures). Three rows move their

**On step 3n's bridged run** (`test_win_m5_bridged_screen`): the
requester logged into real PIMOS (`ref/pimm/pimm.py`) through the held
pimfep, the PIMOS side replayed from the committed tape

`../docs/simpos/screens/login_pimos_bridged.png` is that frame, and the

**Where it stops.** The window stops where the route stops: the

M6-M7.

### Window M4 and M6: PIMOS's console on the PSI, the status band and the system menu (2026-11-25)

**M4: the console, on real PIMOS.** `fep_bridge.py` now routes a device
PIMOS makes by 104 (create_window) to the PSI, as it routed 601's in step
3n. `BridgeFep.BRIDGED` gains `CREATE_WINDOW`, and the module doc
describes the boundary. One live run (PIMOS on `ref/pimm/pimm.py` from the
link checkpoint, 28 s, 8,626,640 PIMOS instructions, 26 exchanges)
recorded `fep_bridge_tapes/console_pwd.json`. A second run gave the same
exchanges and result byte for byte: it came after merging master, with
the script's extra click. PIMOS's own messages to its console, device 28:

    104 (pmpsi) -> 127 (200, 200, 47, 14) -> 130 "PIMOS CONSOLE"
    -> 132 "font:test_11.font" -> 143 -> 70 x3 -> 112 -> 111 "Shell>> "
    -> 112 x2 -> 110 -> [user: pwd] -> 111 " The value of task:
    directory is ">sys>user>pmpsi" . \n" -> 112 -> 111 "Shell>> "
    -> 112 x2 -> 110 (waits)

The PSI answers `{28, 1}` to each, except the 70s (abort: no reply, as in
fep.py) and the waiting 110. It answers the first 110 with `{28, 1,
"pwd"}`. fep.py answers none of them: its log is empty and nothing is
held.

What runs them on the PSI. HELD:
- `pimfep##pimos_window_process`: create_window/9, create_with_buffer,
  goal/1, window_loop, catch/8, command_loop, tag_catch/9, put/2;
- `pimfep##pimos_window`: kl1_message/2, which disassembles each message
  and refutes it on the editor, then replies;
- `pimfep##pimos_window_editor`: reshape, set_title, set_font/4, show,
  putb/4, flush, getl/3 and getl_from_buffer/3, and carriage_return/1's
  demons.

The new or rewritten stand-ins, with their heads giving each label:
- `pmacs_window` (rewritten): create/3 makes the editor, an
  `editor_text_buffer` and an `editor_command_interpreter`;
  get_interpreter/2, input_data/2, has_been_reshaped/1, flash/1 and
  putl/2 are stood in.
- `editor_text_buffer`: the buffer protocol the held editor sends
  (current_position, input_region, move_to, insert_string, search/5,
  see_position/4, end_of_buffer, delete, reset), with redisplay through
  `window_pane` put_codes/2.
- `buffer_region`: the region's start and finish.
- `editor_command_interpreter`: command_interpret/1 reads requests and
  keys from its input stream. RETURN is the editor's carriage_return/1;
  any other key is inserted (Pmacs's bindings are lost, gap G6; this
  minimal set is OURS).
- `pmacs_request`: put/2's `{kl1_message, {Msg}}`.

Small stand-in additions:
- `standard_editor` carriage_return/1 (INFERENCE: a line end at the point);
- `with_lock` lock_with_hook/1 and unlock_with_hook/1 (INTERPOLATED);
- `process` catch/8 (INTERPOLATED from pimos_window_process $or$1/3).

One opcode was added: **blt 69 `not_equal_string`**, KNOWN-PLAINTEXT on
kappa2 `KP_UPT.ESP`:124 and :131 (`69 02 00 00`, `69 01 00 00`), which
set_font/4 needs (157 rows).

A model hazard was found and handled in the stand-in. kl0 stores a vector
in a slot as it is (`resolve_value` copies lists only). put/2's
`{Message}` held a variable bound to the message, and the binding was
undone when put/2's process backtracked: kl1_message/2 was sent an
unbound message (OBSERVED). `pmacs_request` keeps the one argument in a
slot of its own; injection J9 puts the bug back.

**M6: the chrome.** All OURS in look and event form, with the sources in
the files' heads:
- `status_band` (new): the black band on the frame's rows 944-1023, with
  `USER : pmpsi` and `SIMPOS Version V7`. A white box names the selected
  window `<class>/<n>`. `pimos_window` shows as `pmacs_window/N`, the
  stand-in's class name (OURS).
- `menu_pane` (new): the SYSTEM MENU, opened by `{mouse, rr, X, Y}` with
  its top-left at the mouse. It shows two columns of the held login.com's
  7 + 3 items. `{move, X, Y}` boxes the item under the mouse with an
  exclusive_or rectangle, so it erases exactly. `{mouse, l, X, Y}` on an
  item closes the menu and starts the program; on a window, it selects it.
- The coordinator keeps the windows, the mouse (starting at 640, 472) and
  the open menu. A window opened with `{position, manipulator}` is
  centred on the mouse (`window_pane`). At the start position this is the
  old centred place, so step 3m's pins hold.

The bridge's user is now a taped script (`fep_bridge.py`: JSON events
`['mouse', B, X, Y]`, `['move', X, Y]`, `['key', C]`). It has two batches:
- the login: the menu at (640, 560), the item at (978, 605), the three
  answers;
- the shell: a click in the login window, then in the console, then
  `pwd` RETURN. It is given at the first quiet exchange after the
  console's 110.

`replay` uses a tape's own script. `login_pwd.json`, which has none, gets
step 3m's menu select.

**Tests** (`test_espc.py` `Window`, `test_fep_bridge.py` `Console`):
- `test_win_m4_console`:
  - the console's geometry (200, 200, 523 x 219 dots, 47 x 14 cells);

  - its text read back from the frame: `PIMOS CONSOLE`, `Shell>> pwd`,

### Window M6's rest and M7's save areas (2026-11-25)

**M7, save areas.** A pop-up saves the dots under it and restores them
when it closes; until now it cleared its rectangle.
- `windev.py` gains `simpos##bitmap_display` `save/7` (Id, X, Y, W, H,
  Base) and `restore/3` (Id, Op). Each is one command of winfb's frozen
  set, which `../bsv/WinFbBox.bsv` already runs in lockstep (its bench's
  `save_restore` stream), so a live run's stream still replays on both.
- `standin/save_memory.esp` (new, OURS) picks the slot (the lowest free of
  16) and the base (first fit in winfb's 65,536 words, ceil(w/16) words a
  row). It checks no limit: an area past the end, or a seventeenth, goes
  to the device, which refuses it (BADSAVE, a kl0 Refusal). Running out
  is loud, never a silent clear. An area is the list [Id, Base, Size]: a
  vector stored in a slot would lose its bindings on backtracking (the J9
  hazard, below).
- `menu_pane` and `window_pane` save before they draw and restore on
  erase. Basis: PROSE TM-225 p10 and TM-126 p5 (window.md §4.1), a hidden
  window's image kept in a per-window save area in bitmap memory. What a
  covered window writes while covered is lost on the restore: no output
  permission is modelled (OURS).
- The panes are not clipped, so the selected window's exclusive_or
  cursor is taken off before a new pane is drawn (coordinator
  `cursor_aside/1`, OURS). Otherwise it would be saved under the new
  window and later toggled over it (injection R4).
- Not done: restacking, the manipulator (HIDE/SHOW, MOVE, RESHAPE), and
  output permission.

**M6, the date.** The band's second row ends with `20-May-93 Thursday
00:00:00`, right-aligned 8 dots from the frame's edge.
- The form `DD-Mon-YY Weekday hh:mm:ss` is SCREENSHOT: TM-153 PDF p28
  (row 2 right, `25-Jan-86 Saturday 13:01:36`), TM-115 p25 and FSTJ
  26(1) p99. The other months' and days' spellings are INFERENCE.
- The source is the PSI's `date_time`. Its class slots now start at day
  33742, the same OURS value as `ref/pimm/fep.py` DATE (PIMOS's banner
  date, days from 01-Jan-1901), and time 0. The real FEP was the PSI, so
  PIMOS's date and the PSI's are one clock. No clock runs on kl0, so the
  date is the same in every run and in every tape replay.
- `date_time` gains `create/2`, `set_count/3` and `get_value/8`,
  INTERPOLATED from the held sends in cil `src/ms_ist.esp:735-742`
  (HELD-SOURCE). The Gregorian calendar is OURS; the eighth argument (the
  milliseconds) is INFERENCE. espc has no immediate over 255 and no
  register divisor, so the constants are facts and the quotients are
  counted by subtraction.
- Every frame with the band has 941 dots fewer than before. The tests
  check that with the date's rectangle set black again each frame equals
  its pin from before the date (`assert_date`; the `M6_*` pins). The M4
  tape's recorded screen is that earlier frame.

**M6, SWAPPED and spool: not drawn.** The screenshots show them on row 1
right: TM-153 p28 (`SWAPPED` an inverse box, `spool` boxed), FSTJ p99
(both inverse) and Giho 8609 p16. No source read says what turns either
on or off (window.md §4.2, shell.md §4.4, notes/ipsj.md, notes/tm115.md,
the plan §3; searched 2026-11-25). With no state to draw them from, and
no swapping or spooler modelled, they stay out (`status_band.esp`'s
head).

**M6, create and kill from the system menu** (`coordinator.esp`'s head).
- Choosing an item whose program already has a window opens a pop-up at
  the mouse, titled with the item's label, with `create` and `kill`.
  SCREENSHOT simpos.wmv@49-60 (titled `file_manipulator`, `create`,
  `k…`) and prolog.wmv@14-15 (`WHICH ?`, `create`, `kill`); the sequence
  is INFERENCE (subsystems/notes/video.md §3).
- That the pop-up comes only when the program already has a window is
  INFERENCE, from `WHICH ?` and the video's `file_manipulator_2`. So the
  first choice starts the program at once, as before, and the M4 tape's
  path and pins are unchanged.
- `create`: the next left click is one corner. Each `{move}` redraws the
  outline from that corner to the mouse, a 1-dot exclusive_or rectangle.
  The next left click is the other corner: the outline is erased and the
  program started. Its window, made with `{position, manipulator}`, takes
  the outline's top-left (`window_pane` `placement/3`). Two corners as
  TM-125 p8's RESHAPE (PRINTED-INTERFACE). The held requester fixes its
  own size, so the swept size is not used (OURS).
- `kill`: the newest process started from that item that has a window is
  exterminated, and its windows killed. Each restores what it covered.
- The right button cancels a sweep.
- `menu_pane`: a title wider than the columns widens the last column
  (OURS; the pop-up's title is wider than `create`).

**Open, found by probes (not in a test); both FIXED in the next section
(window M7), and now pinned:**
- After `create`, keys go first to the OLDER requester: it already waits
  in get/2 on the coordinator's one keyboard stream, so the first key of
  `local` reached window 1 and the rest window 2. The created window is

- Killing a window that lies under a newer one restores its saved dots

now raises `CompileRefusal('stand-in', ...)` naming the predicate and the
