### Window M7: the manipulator and keyboard focus (2026-11-25)

**Keyboard focus.** Each `standard_io_window` reads its own input stream
(the `input` slot, unused since step 3m), and the coordinator puts a key
on the selected window's stream (`typed/2`). Basis:
- the selected window has the keyboard (PROSE TM-225 p11, TM-115 p111;
  window.md §2.7, §4.1);
- a window is selected when it is shown (TM-225 p10-11) or by a left
  click on it (PRINTED TR-046 p28, `mouse#l` = visit; PROSE TM-115 p111);
- a selection also brings the window to the top (INFERENCE from TM-225
  p11: the selected window is the top of the stack);
- `select/1` "make it the keyboard (input) window" (PROSE Giho 8609 p19,
  TM-125 p22; window.md §2.1) and TM-126 p7 (the selected window's cursor
  blinks; clicks go to the fully visible window under the mouse).
  The held code was searched for how a program picks the keyboard window
  (`docs/simpos/subsystems/notes/ptf.md`, 2026-11-25). pimfep sends no
  `select/1`. `has_been_selected/1` is a `select_notifier` callback,
  defined by kappa `pbwin` (an after-demon) and paragraph
  `profiler_view_window`. So selection by the user, as modelled, is the
  only path the held FEP route needs. The stand-ins do not send
  `has_been_selected/1` (open).

Until M7 every requester read the coordinator's one keyboard stream, so
after `create` the first key went to the older requester, which was
already waiting in get/2. Keys typed while no window is selected go,
oldest first, to the next window selected (coordinator `type_ahead/2`,
OURS). The step 3m dialogue and the M5 screens need this: they queue all
their keys before the requester shows its window. `pmacs_window` already
had a stream of its own (its interpreter's input).

**The stack** (`coordinator.esp` "THE STACK", OURS). The coordinator's
`windows` list is now the stacking order, top first. Every restack goes
through two new pane methods (`window_pane.esp`):
- `lift/1`: the pane's own dots saved, then what was under it drawn back;
- `lay/1`: what is under it saved, then its own dots drawn back.

They are called top-down and bottom-up, so a pane is always the top one
on the screen when its dots are saved. This is the mechanism of PROSE
TM-225 p10 and TM-126 p5 (window.md §4.1 "Implementation"): a hidden
window's image is kept in a save area, and restacking copies between the
save areas and the frame. A restack that would change no dot only
reorders the list and sends nothing: this is the case when the window
overlaps none of the windows it passes. So the M4 tape's frames and the
`saves()` logs of the M6/M7 tests are unchanged.

What uses the stack:
- kill: `take_off/2` lifts the windows above, erases the pane and lays
  them back;
- a selection: raises the window;
- `pmacs_window` `drawn_again` (set_font/2, has_been_reshaped/1): peels
  the windows above before it draws the new pane;
- `cursor_aside/1`: leaves alone a selected window that is lifted, whose
  cursor is in its saved dots.

**MOVE** (`window_pane.esp` `move_to/3`, OURS order). The device's
restore draws an area back where it was saved (winfb is frozen, and
`bsv/WinFbBox.bsv` runs it). So the dots go across by a copy in the frame:
1. the pane's dots are saved;
2. the under-area of the old place is drawn back, and kept;
3. what is under the new place is saved;
4. the pane's dots are drawn at the old place and copied to the new one;
5. the moved dots are saved;
6. the old under-area is drawn back again, and the moved dots over it.

`save_memory` gains `put_back/2`, a restore that keeps the area.

**The manipulator** (`coordinator.esp` "THE MANIPULATOR").
- The middle button over a window opens a pop-up titled `WINDOW
  MANIPULATOR` at the mouse, for that window. The items are SHOW, HIDE,
  RESHAPE and MOVE, verbatim from TM-125 p8 (PRINTED-INTERFACE, window.md
  §4.4). The other eight items are not offered.
- That the middle button opens it is OURS. TM-125 puts the manipulator
  on the system menu, but the held login.com has no such item, and adding
  one would change every menu pin. The middle button moves a pimfep
  window (USAGE, window.md §5.3).
- SHOW raises and selects the window.
- HIDE moves it to the bottom of the window list (TM-125 p8). A selected
  window loses its cursor while it is still on top, and the new top
  window is then selected (INFERENCE, TM-225 p11).
- MOVE: an outline of the window's size follows the mouse (exclusive_or,
  as create's), and a left click places its top-left. The window is
  raised, selected and moved with its dots. Then `reshape/5` and the
  callback `has_been_reshaped/1` are sent (window.md §2.1, §2.12: the
  reshape notifier calls it after the geometry is set).
- RESHAPE: two corner clicks with the rubber band (TM-125 p8 "point at
  the top-left and bottom-right corners with the mouse"), then
  `reshape/5` and `has_been_reshaped/1`.

`standard_io_window` gains `reshape/5` (USAGE selector, window.md §2.1)
and `has_been_reshaped/1` (OURS bodies). When the kept rectangle is not
the pane's, the pane is made again there: as many cells of the font as
fit (at least 1 x 1), the title, the transcript's lines oldest first, and
the cursor if selected. The echoed codes are not in the transcript, so a
typed line is lost on a RESHAPE (OURS). On `pmacs_window`, the held-route
stand-ins `reshape/5` and `has_been_reshaped/1` redraw the buffer at the
new place. Its text size is not changed (open).

**espc fails loud on a swallowed head** (`espc.compile_text`:
`source_refusals`, `swallowed_heads`). A comment line could eat a
clause's head, and the body then compiled with no complaint:
- in the method part, it read as a local clause outside the local part,
  which the parser dropped into `unparsed`, reported nowhere;
- in the local part, it read as a clause headed by `','/2`, or, for a body
  of one goal, as a fact.

`compile_text` now refuses (CompileRefusal `source`) on three things:
- any item the parser dropped;
- a clause headed by a control construct (`BODY_ONLY_HEADS`);
- a comment line that ends as a clause head does (`:-`, `:- !,`) and is
  followed by an indented line of code (`SWALLOWED_HEAD`, a heuristic,
  OURS).

The held programs go through `check()`, which still records such items as
`unparsed source`. Every stand-in and test program compiles unchanged.

**Tests** (`test_espc.py` `Window`):

| test | what it pins |
|---|---|
| `test_win_m7_keyboard_focus` | after `create`, `local` RETURN reaches the new requester only (each window's history; its text read back from the frame); a left click on the older window's uncovered part raises and selects it, and the next line is its own |
| `test_win_m7_manip` | the manipulator's place, title and items; HIDE equals a frame built from `two` and `before`; HIDE then SHOW equals `two` exactly; a kill under another window equals a run that never made it, with the same line typed; MOVE of the top window equals a window made there; MOVE of the covered window equals a composed frame; RESHAPE's outline (2w + 2(h - 2) dots), the new pane (35 x 12 cells), its title, transcript and cursor, and outside it the frame with the window gone. After each: save memory holds only the windows' own areas, every command is OK, and the stream replays to the frame |
| `test_win_m7_console_move` | MOVE of PIMOS's console (HELD `pimos_window` on the `pmacs_window` stand-in): `has_been_reshaped/1` makes a new pane, the window's place is (100, 100), and the frame equals the replay's with the console's dots moved; RESHAPE moves it with its size kept |
| `test_espc_swallowed_head` | four swallowed-head forms refused (`source`); the same clause with its head on its own line compiles |

None of the earlier pins moved. The new restacks send nothing to the
device when no dot would change, so the `saves()` logs of
`test_win_m7_save_areas` and every earlier frame are as before. Screens
(`psi/winshot.py`, checked by `assert_shots`):
`../docs/simpos/screens/window_manipulator.png`, `manipulator_hide.png`,
`manipulator_reshape.png`.

clean; `test_espc.py` Window (E2, E3: all of `test_espc.py`),

each; the lists are in the tests' heads.

| F1 | the same with M7's type-ahead kept (it puts each key back on the stream it takes it from) | M4, bridged and login screens, M6 selection, console move, focus, manip; bridge replay and login service |
| F2 | type-ahead a no-op | M4, bridged and login screens, M6 selection, console move; bridge replay and login service |
| F3 | a selection does not raise | focus, manip |
| M1 | lift/1 skips the restore (the plan's injection) | focus, manip |

**Output to a covered window: permission `out`.** Per-window input made
this reachable: a covered requester reads its queued keys, echoes them
and prompts. A probe drew window 2's prompt over window 1's label. Each
output method of `window_pane` (set_title, put_string, put_code,
put_codes, new_line, clear) now asks the coordinator to uncover the pane
(`uncover/3`: the windows above are lifted if one overlaps it), draws, and
lays them back (`unpeel/2`). The covered part goes into the save areas
above, and only the shown part appears. This is TM-225 p10-11's ② and
TM-125 p6's OUTPUT, the held `permission(out)` (63 USAGE sites, window.md
§4.1). Giving every window this permission is OURS; TM-225's default is
① wait.

**The console** (`test_win_m7_console_move`). After the M4 replay, the
mouse events go straight to the coordinator's `event/2` (OURS harness:
the tape's script is fixed). MOVE of `pimfep##pimos_window` sends
`reshape/5` and `has_been_reshaped/1`. The stand-in redraws the window at
its new place with a new pane, and the frame equals the replay's with the
console's dots moved. RESHAPE moves it and keeps its size.

**Open** (all five done the next day, in the next section):
- the other output permissions (wait, show, notify) and a per-window
  choice of permission;
- a pop-up menu over a covered window that writes: the lifted windows'
  areas would be drawn back under the menu, not over it. No flow does
  this;
- the manipulator's other eight TM-125 p8 items;
- a `pmacs_window` RESHAPE that changes its text size (it moves only);
- `has_been_selected/1` is not sent.

### Window M7's rest: output permission, the other items, has_been_selected/1, Pmacs RESHAPE (2026-11-26)

**Output permission** (`coordinator.esp` "OUTPUT PERMISSION"). A window
now has one of the four permissions of TM-125 p5-6 and TM-225 p10-11
(PROSE; the ATTRIBUTE dialogue lists `out / wait / show / notify`,
SCREENSHOT TM-125 p7 Fig. 4). It is set by the held create option
`permission(P)` (read by `standard_io_window` only: `pmacs_window` reads
no options, and no held Pmacs caller gives one, OURS), by
`set_permission/2` (both stand-ins; USAGE, window.md §2.2, §4.1), or by
the manipulator's PERMISSION item. Each output method of
`window_pane` asks `uncover/3` first. While the window is covered (a
window above overlaps it):
- `out` (②): writes at once; the windows above are lifted (M7's case);
- `wait` (①): the writer's process waits on a stream of its own, held
  in the coordinator's `waiters`, until the window is uncovered or its
  permission becomes `out` or `show`. Every event, selection and kill
  wakes the writers that may now write (`wake/1`);
- `show` (③): the window is raised and selected, then writes. This
  changes the selected window (TM-125 p6), so the next key goes to it;
- `notify` (④): the writer waits, and a temporary window opens at the
  mouse (TM-125 p6-7: the system's "NOTIFY WINDOW"; a temporary window
  appears at the mouse cursor). It offers `wait` and `show` (TM-225's
  "the user chooses ① or ③ by mouse"). Its title is the window's title
  (OURS). Only one pop-up is open at a time: while another is open, or
  a sweep is under way, it waits for the next quiet moment (OURS).

The default stays `out` (OURS, as in M7). TM-225 makes ① wait the
default, but the held FEP route never sets a permission, and `out` keeps
every earlier pin. The coordinator's own process never waits: its
redraws (`has_been_reshaped/1`, a selection's cursor) would deadlock
(OURS). A killed window's waiters are dropped (`remove_window/2`, OURS):
its writer stays suspended, since its output has no pane to go to, and
no notify window is offered for it, so `show` can never select a dead
window.

**A pop-up over a writing window** (README "Open" until now). `uncover/3`
lifts an open pop-up first, when it overlaps the pane or a window being
lifted (`menu_pane` `lift/1`, `lay/1`), and lays it back last. So output
under a menu goes into the menu's save area: a temporary window "is never
hidden by an ordinary window" and saves what it covers (PROSE TM-125
p6-7). A sweep's exclusive_or outline is taken off around the write and
drawn again after it, so no text lands in it (OURS).

**The manipulator's items**, in TM-125 p8's order (PRINTED-INTERFACE):
SHOW, HIDE, RESHAPE, MOVE, EXPAND, MENU, PERMISSION, EXIT.
- EXPAND "expand the window size as large as possible but not hide
  another fully shown window". The obstacles are the fully shown
  windows it does not overlap now. The edges go out left, right, up,
  down, each to the nearest obstacle or the screen's edge (OURS order).
  Then it is raised, selected and reshaped as by RESHAPE.
- MENU "select the window using the submenu of windows list": a pop-up
  titled `WINDOWS` (OURS), each window by its title, top first. The
  chosen window is selected (and so raised).
- PERMISSION "reset the window output permission by sub-menu": `out`,
  `wait`, `show`, `notify`.
- EXIT "exit from window manipulator": closes it.

Not offered, each for want of what it acts on:
- ATTRIBUTE: TM-125 p7 Fig. 4's choice window, with typed values. Choice
  windows are milestone M9 (offered since: "Window M9" below, with
  PERMISSION only, and the manipulator has nine items).
- HARDCOPY: no printer is modelled (window.md §2.2 `hardcopy/2
  (W,laser_printer)`, USAGE).
- SUPERIOR and INFERIOR: they move the "editing base" along the window
  hierarchy. Every window here is the screen's inferior, with none of
  its own.

**`has_been_selected/1`.** `select_window/2` sends it after a window
becomes the selected one, and not again while it stays selected. It is
the select notifier's callback (window.md §2.1, §2.12): kappa `pbwin`'s
after-demon on it proves a SIMPOS primary, and paragraph
`profiler_view_window` defines its own. The stand-ins' primary does
nothing (OURS). A send with no method refuses in kl0, so both window
stand-ins define it.

**Pmacs RESHAPE changes the text size.** `pmacs_window` `reshape/5` with
a size in dots sets the text size to the cells of the window's font
that fit, at least 1 x 1. `has_been_reshaped/1` then draws the window
again at that size, its buffer wrapped to the new width. This is an
INFERENCE: the held `pimos_window_editor` `set_inside_size/5` sets the
text size (@26e) before `has_been_reshaped/1` (@271). PIMOS's own 127
(reshape/7: `set_position/3` and `set_text_size/3`, `$or$1/5` @479-48e)
does not pass through it. `manipulator` in place of the size (the held
`reshape_by_mouse/1`) leaves the text size alone. MOVE sends the pane's
own size, which gives back the same cells.

**Tests** (`test_espc.py` `Window`):

| test | what it pins |
|---|---|
| `test_win_m7_permissions` | the PERMISSION submenu's place and items; each permission set on window 2; with window 2 echoing while a click raises window 1: `wait` gives the frame without the echo, the writer held back, and SHOW then gives the frame of the keys typed on top; `show` gives that frame at once, window 2 selected, the next line its own; `notify` opens the pop-up (place, title, items, pinned screen), `show` gives the typed frame, `wait` the frame without the echo |
| `test_win_m7_popup_over_writer` | the system menu opened in the same batch as the covered echo: the frame equals the menu opened after the echo, and after closing it equals the run without the menu; MOVE's outline over the writing window: the frame equals the outline drawn after the echo |

### Window M8: graphics (2026-11-26)

`standard_io_window` gains the drawing methods of window.md §2.5, drawn
by new `window_pane` methods (`draw_line_in/7`, `fill_in/6`,
`frame_in/7`, `string_in/6`, `transfer_in/8`). The selectors and argument
forms are window.md's, each cited there; the bodies are OURS:
- `draw_line/5-8` (TM-125 p17, TM-785 p40; `/6` is a VARIANT, an integer
  taken as the width, an atom as the op);
- `draw_rectangle/5`, `/7`, `/8` (the outline, its width inward; style
  `filled` fills, IPSJ 36239 p5);
- `draw_filled_rectangle/6`, `/8` (pattern `fix` only);
- `draw_string/4-6` (`/5` is a VARIANT: an op name is taken as the op,
  anything else as the font);
- `transfer/8`;
- `execute_graphics_command/2`, a list of `{Name, {Args}}` sent to the
  window's own methods (USAGE paragraph p_icon.esp; the five-element
  `draw_string` and seven-element `draw_filled_rectangle` of g_window.esp
  :141, :169);
- `get_mouse_position/3`;
- the markers (window.md §2.6, PRINTED-INTERFACE TM-125 p17):
  `create_marker/2` (and `/7`, the CESP substitute's form, an analogy),
  `draw_marker/2`, `erase_marker/2`, `move_marker/4`, `set_marker_type/3`
  (`box`, `line`; `reverse` from the CESP substitute), `set_marker_size/4`,
  `get_marker_list/2`, on a new stand-in class `marker` (`marker.esp`).
  The look is OURS: drawn with exclusive_or (TM-125 p17's default op), a
  box is the 1-dot outline, a line the diagonal from the top-left, reverse
  inverts the area; moving, retyping or resizing a drawn marker takes it
  off and draws it again. `get_marker_position/4` is UNSPECIFIED (window.md
  §2.6) and not stood in.

Defaults are TM-125 p17's: exclusive_or, 1 dot, solid. Coordinates are
dots from the inside's top-left. Every method goes through `uncover/3`,
so drawing obeys the window's output permission, and the block cursor is
taken off around it. Only the frozen winfb primitives are used (`fill`,
`line` with a width, `glyph`, `copy`); none was added.

Clipping (OURS):
- a fill is cut to the inside, and so is a horizontal or vertical line or
  a rectangle;
- a slanted line is drawn only when it lies wholly inside, and the check
  comes before `uncover/3`, so a refusal on a covered window leaves no
  window lifted;
- a glyph is drawn only when its cell lies wholly inside;
- a transfer's two areas must lie inside.

Refused loudly (a send of `graphics_refused/2`, which no class has):
- a slanted line leaving the inside;
- a line style other than solid;
- a fill pattern other than `fix` (until M8's rest: now an unknown
  pattern only);
- an unknown command in a batch;
- drawing on a window not on the screen.

**M8 was partial at this merge** (20efface). Circles, arcs, ellipses,
polygons, patterns, `copy/9`, `paint/5` and images followed in "Window
M8's rest" below.

**Test** `test_win_m8_graphics`. A 40 x 20 window is made at (100, 100)
after the Login PIMOS run. Each drawing is compared with a frame built
from winfb commands in Python:
- the HELD `paragraph##type_switch_icon` local `draw_box/5`
  (p_icon.esp:177-186, run from the compiled .ptf), over a black block
  (its width-2 `reset` lines carve white) and on white (105 dots);
- a slanted `draw_line/5` gives 100 dots, and drawn twice it erases (the
  plan's pin);
- `draw_string/4` is read back as text;
- a fill is cut to the inside;
- `transfer/8` copies;
- `get_mouse_position/3` is pinned;
- four loud refusals.

**Test** `test_win_m8_forms`: the other argument forms, against
winfb-built frames and dot counts: `draw_rectangle/5` (twice erases),
`/8` with Line 2 and `set`, `/7` `filled`; both readings of `draw_line/6`
(an integer width, an atom op) and `draw_line/7`;
`draw_filled_rectangle/8` with `fix`; both readings of `draw_string/5` and

`paragraph##g_window` local `draw_list/8` (g_window.esp:130-158, the mono

### Window M8's rest: patterns, images, shapes, copy/9 (2026-11-26)

M8 is done, except `paint/5` (a STOP, below), `save_image/6`, off-screen
windows and `get_marker_position/4`. Only the frozen winfb primitives are
used: patterns and images are drawn as winfb's styled `line` (its 16-bit
style operand, MSB first), reached by a new kl0 model
`simpos##bitmap_display` `line/8` (`windev.py`). No primitive was added,
so the hardware contract is unchanged.

**Fill patterns** (`draw_filled_rectangle/8`, the filled shapes):
- the named patterns `light_paint`, `dotted`, `mesh`, `small_mesh`,
  `left_hatch`, `right_hatch` (window.md §2.5), 4 rows of one word each
  (`window_pane.esp` `named/3`). `light_paint` is HELD-DERIVED: the held
  paragraph images were saved from a window filled `fix, set` and then
  `light_paint, reset`, and `profiler_mono_selector_image` has 16#"7777"
  on every even row and 16#"DDDD" on every odd one (measured in
  `test_win_m8_images`). Since 2026-11-26 the saved words are read as
  1 = white, so `light_paint` IS 16#"7777" / 16#"DDDD", anchored at the
  inside's top-left (see "The PSI's line geometry" below; it was the
  complement, 8888 / 2222, before). The other names' looks are OURS;
- a held pattern string (256 words, made by paragraph `bar_v.esp`
  `set_pattern/2`, which writes each word four times) read as a 64 x 64
  tile, 4 words a row (INFERENCE). The tile is anchored at the inside's
  top-left (OURS). This needed kl0's **blt 44 `divide_with_remainder`**
  (`kl0_opcodes.tsv`, KNOWN-PLAINTEXT: cil `cil_kl0.esp:119-120`; witness
  `paragraph##bar_v` `set_pattern/2` @211-214). Its four census rows move
  in `test_kl0.py` (each to the next refusal, accounted there).

**Images**: `restore_image/6` draws a `double_bytes` string, ceil(W/16)
words a row, MSB leftmost, opaque (OURS; since 2026-11-26 a 1 is white:
the area set, then the 1 dots reset, see "The PSI's line geometry"). `get_label_height/2` answers the label strip's height. A new
stand-in class `bitmap` answers `get_plane_count/2` = 1, so the held
image code takes its monochrome path. The held paragraph
`type_switch_icon` `draw/6` runs from the .ptf: three held `draw_box`
frames, then the icon through the held `restore/7` -> `adjust_pos` ->
`restore_image/6`. The icon's place is the held `adjust_pos`'s (its Y is
Y0 + the label height - 24), with OUR label height of 16
(`get_label_height/2`, OURS: no source gives the strip's height).

**Shapes**: `draw_circle/7` (PRINTED WINCS p3), `draw_ellipse/8`,
`draw_arc/9`, `draw_elliptical_arc/10`, `draw_fan/9`,
`draw_filled_circle/5` (USAGE gog) and `/7`, `draw_filled_ellipse/7`,
`draw_filled_fan/9`, `draw_graph/2`, `/9`, `draw_polygon/2`, `/9`
(TM-785 p40's vectors; the arity INFERENCE from their length), each also
through `execute_graphics_command/2`. One printed vector is refused
loudly: TM-785 p40's `draw_filled_ellipse` `{200, 0, 100, 1, dotted, set}`
has six elements, the filled circle's layout with its `1`, so it reads
either as an ellipse 1 dot high or as no ellipse (OURS: ambiguous). The dots are OURS
(`window_pane.esp` `shape_in/9`): a disc is x*x + y*y =< R*R + R, an
ellipse scales the disc's half-width, an outline is the shape less the
shape W dots smaller, and a sector is tested by cross products with a
rounded sine table (angles in whole degrees, anticlockwise from the
right, per TM-785 p13-14's prose). Each row is drawn as spans that do not
overlap, so exclusive_or draws each dot once. A fan is the arc and its two
radii; a polygon is closed, a graph is not (OURS). Refused loudly: a style
other than solid, a width under 1, an integer op (TM-785's colour
numbers: the screen has none), a negative angle, an unknown pattern.

**copy/9** `(W, Src, SX, SY, DX, DY, Width, Height, Op)` (USAGE icotext
`util_scroll.esp:211`; the direction is INFERENCE) copies between two shown
windows with the frozen screen copy, save and restore
(`window_pane.esp` `copy_in/9`; a new `save_memory` `restore/3` draws a
saved area through an op). Neither window's other dots change, whatever
covers either (OURS). Refused loudly: the held matrix form
`(W, DX, DY, Src, SX, SY, W, H, {Matrix})` (a scaled copy), a source not
shown, an area outside an inside, an integer op. The held caller itself
cannot run: its source is a 2000 x 2000 `hide_window` that is never shown.
That is an off-screen bitmap, which the frozen device lacks (save memory
holds 65,536 words; that image needs 250,000).

**paint/5 is a STOP.** `(W, X, Y, Pattern, Op)` (USAGE kappa2 `pdim.esp:606`)
is a seed fill (INFERENCE). Finding the region's edge needs the frame's
dots, and no frozen primitive gives a dot's value to the program (fill,
line, glyph and copy write; save and restore move dots unseen). It is
refused loudly rather than given a new primitive.

**Held caller that does not run**: kappa `make_copyright/2`
(`MOPMANAGER.ESP:154`, `draw_circle(Window,16,22,10,1,solid,set)`) draws
before its window is activated and uses `#font_13`. Its arguments are
drawn on a shown window in `test_win_m8_shapes`.

### Window M8's stops resolved (2026-11-26)

The owner's "do it" (2026-11-26) on `../docs/simpos/m8_stops.md`: its
recommendations, in its §7 order, one commit a step.

**Step 1: the held `draw_filled_ellipse` forms and `rough_dotted`.**
- `/8` `(W, CX, CY, RX, RY, _, Pattern, Op)` (USAGE icotext
  `imi_draw_win.esp:250-282`, `util_scroll.esp` forwarders) and `/6`
  `(W, CX, CY, RX, RY, Op)`, pattern fix (USAGE gog `CGS_DEMO.ESP:416,
  :424`). `/7` is kept, relabelled INFERENCE (only TM-785 p40's defective
  vector). `execute_graphics_command/2`'s seven-element vector is `/8`;
  the printed six-element vector stays refused, now because no held
  method has that arity (m8_stops.md §6).
- `rough_dotted` (kappa2 `pdim.esp:1094` and seven more held programs):
  density INFERENCE (sparser than `dotted`: examds `sl_draw.win`'s
  thresholds, paragraph `rmon.esp`'s order), dots OURS (`8888, 0, 2222,
  0`). No held image shows it.
- Neither held caller compiles under espc (icotext: 52 refused predicates;
  gog `CGS_DEMO.ESP`: its macro bank `moji` is not held), so
  `test_win_m8_stops_ellipse` replays their calls, transcribed.
- Injections, OBSERVED (Window, `test_fep_bridge.py`, `test_kl0.py`):
  S1 `/8` drawn with fix: stops_ellipse only; S2 `/6` drawn exclusive_or:
  first NOTHING (drawn on white), then, the /6 drawn over a half-black
  block, stops_ellipse only; S3 rough_dotted's rows swapped:
  stops_ellipse and patterns.

**Step 2: reading dots back (the 2026-11-26 extension of the winfb
contract, OURS, owner-approved).**
- `winfb.py` prim `a`, `read x y w h`. The rectangle is cut as a fill is
  cut and answered as data words: ceil(w'/16) a row, the pad dots 0, then
  OK. Prims 8, 9 and b-f stay BADOP. The format is in
  `../docs/simpos/window_hardware_plan.md` §2.4.
  - The 11 older streams are byte-identical to the frozen reference.
  - Three new gen streams (`reads`, `rand_r_1`, `rand_r_2`) and
    `bsv/win_streams/reads_stall.txt` (under `+stall`).
  - `bsv/WinFbBox.bsv` reads in lockstep with it: a blit from the frame
    whose sink is the response queue (rule `sCollectRead`), its status
    after the words.
  - The bench writes `win_read.txt`, and `run_win_tb.sh` compares it too.
    Its header records injections R1-R3.
- `windev.py` `read/7` writes the words into the caller's string in one
  `set_codes`. On top of it (`standin/window_pane.esp` `read_in/6`,
  `point_in/4`, `paint_in/5`; `standard_io_window.esp`):
  - `save_image/6`, `get_point_value/4` and `set_point_value/4`;
  - `paint/5`, a seed fill. The region is the dots 4-connected to the
    seed with the seed's value: INFERENCE and OURS. It is found by a
    scanline search on rows read once each, then drawn span by span, so a
    pattern's 0 dots cannot feed the search.
  - Reads go through `uncover/3` and take the cursor off, as drawing does.
- `test_win_m8_stops_read` covers:
  - `save_image/6` of a 40-wide block, open and covered;
  - point values;
  - gog `DISPPBMS.esp:116-118`'s painted triangle and cil
    `es_gin.esp:1752-1762`'s `draw_triangle`, both transcribed (neither
    class compiles under espc);
  - kappa2's patterned `paint(W, 1, 1, Pattern, set)` form inside an
    outline;
  - the refusals.
  Its header records the injections.
- MEASURED cost: paint tests dot by dot. A 78 x 48 region (3,744 dots)
  takes about 1 s. A 120,000-dot region passes kl0's 20,000,000-step
  limit. kappa2's icons are 61 x 64 (`pdim.esp:573-574`), within the
  measured size. Open item: test whole words for large regions.
- Not yet run: the held callers paragraph `make/3` (`p_imag.esp` go1-go6),
  gog and cil. paragraph draws into windows that are never shown, which
  needs step 3 (drawing targets); its saved `profiler_icon_image1..6` are
  then the independent oracle of `save_image`'s layout. Until then the
  test's expected words repeat the implementation's own row and bit order.
- Open item for the host link (`bsv/WinLink.bsv`, the hardware agent's
  file, not changed here). The TX frames data as 64-word records
  (`wLeft <= 64`), and `sendData` takes 64 answers without looking at
  `isData`. So a read whose word count is not a multiple of 64 would have
  its status, and the answers after it, sent as data words. This is from
  reading the rule; it has not been run.

**Step 3a: drawing targets in the winfb contract (the same extension,
OURS, owner-approved).** Python and RTL only; no stand-in uses them yet.
- `winfb.py` prim `b`, `target which kind base pitch w h`, sets the
  destination (which 0) or the source (1) surface: the frame (kind 0) or
  an area of the 65,536-word bitmap memory (kind 1). BADSURF (6) refuses
  an area that memory cannot hold, and a copy between two different areas
  that share words. fill, line and glyph draw on the destination; copy
  and read take dots from the source. save, restore and dump stay on the
  frame. Bitmap memory is 0 at reset. The rules are in
  `../docs/simpos/window_hardware_plan.md` §2.4.
  - The 11 frozen streams and `reads`, `rand_r_1`, `rand_r_2` give the
    same output as before.
  - New gen streams: `targets`, `rand_x2_1..3`. New directed stream:
    `bsv/win_streams/targets_stall.txt`.
- `bsv/WinFbBox.bsv` runs them in lockstep. It has the surface registers,
  a setup cycle for base + row x pitch (`blitSetup`, `lSetup`), and a
  line engine stepped by the pitch, with no per-dot multiply. It clears
  the bitmap RAM at reset beside the frame. Injections T1-T4 are in
  `run_win_tb.sh`'s header; synthesis is in the plan's §5.1.
- Next is step 3b: the stand-in's hidden windows draw into areas. That
  makes paragraph's `p_imag.esp` runnable.

**Step 3b: windows drawn before they are shown, or never shown (OURS
bodies).**
- A window drawn on before `activate/1` gets a surface pane
  (`standin/window_pane.esp` `create_surface/5`).
  - Its inside is an area of bitmap memory, `ceil(Iw/16)` words a row,
    taken from `save_memory.esp`'s first-fit list (new `allocate/3` and
    `free/2`; a surface's id is `-1 - Base`, never a device slot).
  - The area is cleared on allocation (OURS: TM-115 p44 says 1.0's areas
    are not).
  - The inside is the window's `size(Wd, Ht)` in dots (paragraph's form),
    or its size in cells.
- `coordinator.esp` `uncover/3` aims the device at the area (`windev.py`
  `surface/5`), and `unpeel/2` aims it back at the frame.
  - Every shown pane's `uncover/3` aims at the frame first. So a drawing
    refused inside a surface pane's uncover cannot leave a shown window
    drawing into the area.
  - `windev` sends target records only when the surface changes, so runs
    that draw only on the frame stream what they streamed before.
- `activate/1` copies the surface into the new pane's inside (`windev`
  `show/7`) and frees the area. `kill/1` of a window never shown frees it.
  A `size(Wd, Ht)` window, when shown, gets as many cells as fit.
- Refused loudly:
  - copy/9 to or from a window never shown (`surface_copy`);
  - a surface bitmap memory cannot hold (`bitmap_memory`). icotext's
    2000 x 2000 canvas needs 250,000 words; the memory is 65,536 words of
    block RAM until H8 moves it to SDRAM.
- `test_win_m8_stops_surface` covers:
  - the HELD paragraph `make/3` (p_imag.esp go1-go6), run through its
    public `make_image_class_file/3`, with a harness model in place of its
    file-writing `:write/4`. Each image is made into a 500 x 400 window
    that is never shown. The frame is unchanged and no area is left.
  - kappa2's paint before activate;
  - the refused-uncover case;
  - icotext's canvas;
  - the stream replaying to the same frame and bitmap memory.
- The paragraph images now equal the held `profiler_icon_image1..6`
  word for word (0 differing dots in each). Until 2026-11-26 every word
  differed (pinned then as `PARAGRAPH_GAP`); the rules that closed it are
  in "The PSI's line geometry" below.

**Step 4: copy/9's matrix form, whole scales.** `(W, DX, DY, Src, SX,
SY, Width, Height, {A, B, C, D, E, F})` (USAGE icotext
`util_scroll.esp:218-224`). The matrix is read as a row-major 2 x 3
affine map (INFERENCE, m8_stops.md §5).
- Stood in (`window_pane.esp` `magnify_in/10`): whole scales A, E >= 1,
  B = C = D = F = 0. Each source dot becomes A x E dots (nearest dot,
  OURS), drawn opaque. It runs above the device line: a read of the
  source (step 2), then rows drawn as images. No RTL was needed.
- Refused loudly: rotation and shear, an offset, scales that are not
  whole (the held `Min/Max`, the commented-out 0.8 and 1.5), a
  destination leaving the inside.
- `magnify` and `expand/9` are not stood in: no source fixes their
  arguments (euod's layout is INFERENCE only).
- icotext's own call still refuses, because its source is the 2000 x 2000
  canvas (`bitmap_memory`, H8).
- `test_win_m8_stops_matrix` covers {2,0,0,0,2,0} and {3,0,0,0,1,0}
  against a dot-by-dot reference, and the refusals. Its header records
  the injections.

### The PSI's line geometry, from the held paragraph images (2026-11-26)

The HELD paragraph `p_imag.esp` `go1`-`go6` draw icons into a 500 x 400
`standard_io_window` (label, margin and border off, never shown) and save
them with `save_image/6`; the results are the HELD classes
`profiler_icon_image1..6`. Those are the first independent oracle for the
stand-in's drawing. The rules below were fitted to them (a scratch
simulator of go3-go6, then the stand-in itself), each against the dots
that decide it, and checked on a second held image,
`profiler_mono_selector_image` (`p_v_imag.esp` `draw/2`, a titled
window saved at (0, 0)). All are INFERENCE.

| rule | before | now | the dots that decide it |
|---|---|---|---|
| saved words' sense | 1 = black | **1 = white**: `read_in/6` inverts, `image_in/6` draws 1 white | every image: `set` lines, `set` strings and `reset` fills are known; all seven images hold the `set` dots 0, the `reset` dots 1 (selector: `box/6`'s `fix,reset` interiors 1, its `set` text 0) |
| `light_paint` | 8888 / 2222 | **7777 / DDDD**, anchored at the inside | follows from the sense: the backgrounds (`fix,set` then `light_paint,reset`) read 7777 / DDDD in both windows, at the inside's phase once the image origin is taken off |
| image origin | the inside | **(3, 8) above-left of a never-shown pane's inside** (`save_image_in/6`) | go1-go6 save at (X+4, Y+8) the dots drawn at (X+1, Y): images 5 and 6 fit only with draw +(3, 8) (e.g. image 6's box tops, rows 50-51 and 77-78 of the inside, are rows 0-1 and 27-28 of the image saved at Y+8 = 58) (the next best offset, (4, 8), leaves 64 of their dots wrong). The selector holds its title strip in rows 0-23 and its inside from row 24, and `adjust_pos/3` adds `get_label_height - 24`, so the image methods address the outer rectangle on the PSI; why a flags-off window's inside sits at (3, 8) is unknown. A shown pane keeps the inside as origin (OURS) |
| end points | both | both | images 3-6: leaving the last point off gives 36 wrong dots; e.g. image 3's `draw_up1` line (57, 54)-(51, 63), `set`: its last dot (51, 63) is 0 in the held image (inside coordinates) |
| ties | away from the first end point (winfb's Bresenham) | **toward the first end point** | image 4's eight slope-1/2 x-major lines (`draw_left1`/`draw_right1` with W 12, H1 6: tips at (82..83, 59..60) and (121..122, 59..60), all four directions): away 60 wrong dots, "always up" 36, "always down" 24, "from the left end" 30, "from the top end" 24, toward 0. Held-out check: the selector's y-major `draw_up_tra`/`draw_down_tra` (dx 9 or 7, dy 12): toward 0 wrong dots on the triangles, away 12 |
| width | x-major down, y-major right (the device's) | **x-major UP, y-major right** | images 4-6, selector: every 2-wide line is axis-aligned. A box's `{X,Y+1,X+W-1,Y+1,2}` covers rows Y and Y+1, its `{X,Y,X,Y+H-1,2}` columns X and X+1 (images 3-6: x-major down gives 107 wrong dots, y-major left 72; e.g. image 5's top line, `reset`, makes (52, 50) 1 where `light_paint` leaves a 0, so row Y is covered; image 4's (74, 50) stays the `set` 0 of box 1's right column although box 2's 2-wide `reset` column starts at x 75, so that column goes right). Every wide line is drawn left to right or top to bottom, so "left of the direction drawn" fits as well; a wide line drawn the other way, and a wide slanted line, are OURS |
| glyph | its cell at Y | **its cell `bias` rows below Y** (the f822 record's word 6, 3 for test_11; new device query `bias/4`) | images 1, 2: `draw_label/5`'s `r`, `s` and `&` lie 3 rows below; the selector's `WhatxWhen` (`box/6`, Y+1) starts at row Y+4 |
| `exclusive_or` between areas (`transfer/8`) | the device's | **the PSI's dots' xor (1 = white)**, so on the frame the destination is inverted after the device's xor | images 1, 2: `draw_label/5`'s mono path (copy, `light_paint` reset, `exclusive_or`) gives the held glyph knocked out of the background only so; with the device's xor the glyph's cell comes out as the pattern's complement. `set`/`reset`/`copy` between areas: `copy` checked (same path), the others OURS |

How it is done without changing the device (so `winfb` and
`bsv/WinFbBox.bsv` stay as they are, and their command streams agree):
`window_pane.esp` `line_in/7` sends a slanted line from (X2, Y2) to
(X1, Y1) (the device's Bresenham run backwards rounds ties toward the
caller's first point; checked equal for every line within 15 dots), and
moves an x-major line up by Width - 1; the horizontal fill moves up the
same way, and `drawable/6` checks the moved box. `get_point_value/4` and
`set_point_value/4` take the saved words' sense (1 = white).

The glyph bias applies to `draw_string/4-6` (`draw_string_in/6`); the
stand-ins' own cell text (`string_in/6`, the choice window) stays in its
cells (OURS layout). The held `g_window` `draw_list_b/8` corroborates it:
it draws each number at Y and its bar at Y+3, so with the bias the
glyph's cell top meets the bar's.

`test_win_m8_stops_surface` asserts the six images equal the held ones
(0 differing dots each). Discriminating injection L1, OBSERVED: the
tie-break flipped (the slanted line sent from (X1, Y1)) fails that test
at image 4 with 60 differing dots, `test_win_m8_shapes` (its reference
sends lines reversed) and `test_win_m8_rest_screen` (the pin), nothing
else in Window.

Pins moved (2026-11-26): `GRAPHICS_PIN` (`window_graphics.png`: the held
box's 2-wide lines one row up, the string 3 rows down; same dot count
107,429) and `GRAPHICS_REST_PIN` (`window_graphics_rest.png`: 110,989 to
113,666 dots, `light_paint` now covers 3 dots in 4 and the restored held
icon image is drawn 1 = white). `test_boot_route.py`'s `SCREEN_SHA` did
not move (rerun with `--png`). Test references updated to the rules:
`put_line`, `image_ref`, `NAMED_PATTERNS`, the graphics and forms boxes,
the text reads 3 rows down, `save_image` words and point values 1 = white.

### Window M7's rest, pinned: the coordinator's own writes (2026-11-26)

Injection P13 (the coordinator's own process held back like any writer)
failed nothing at merge 20efface: no run on the route made the
coordinator write into a covered window whose permission is not `out`.
`test_win_m7_coordinator_writes` now makes one. A test class (OURS
harness, as `test_win_m7_has_been_selected`; kappa `pbwin.esp:72` is the
held after-demon on `has_been_selected/1`) writes a line into its partner
from that demon. The partner has `permission(wait)` and is covered. The
probe is shown while `simpos##process`'s current process is the
coordinator's (the direct send stands for the coordinator process's own
`event/2`). The line goes in at once, the cover's dots are unchanged, and
there is no waiter. From another process the same write waits (a waiter
of kind `wait`). P13 now ERRORS that test (would suspend), nothing else.

### Window M9: choice windows, and the manipulator's ATTRIBUTE (2026-11-26)

A new stand-in `standard_choice_window` (window.md §2.10, §3.4) serves the
HELD pimfep superviser's error mode menu. Its local
`create_error_mode_menu/1` @48a creates the window with a `choice_list`
of three label lines and a `choose` field (`error_mode`, on/off) and
`label_flag(off)`. Then `refute_sub_command/2` @36c-3b0 sends
`set_item_value/3`, `get_size/3`, `set_position/3`, `activate/1`,
`read/2` (it expects `[{error_mode, V}]`) and `deactivate/1`.

The fields:
- label lines (7 elements);
- text fields: shown with their default and read back as it. THE TEXT IS
  NOT EDITED (OURS: no held caller on the route edits one);
- `choose`: one of N, the current value in reverse video (SCREENSHOT
  TM-125 p7 Fig. 4); a click on a Text makes it current;
- `boolean`: as `choose`, with "yes" and "no" (OURS words).

The bottom row holds `do_it` and `abort` (Fig. 4). The window adds them
itself (INFERENCE: the held list has none, and the `..._with_no_execute`
classes are the ones without do_it). `read/2` waits on the window's own
stream: do_it gives `[{Key, Value}, ...]` for the keyed fields in item
order, abort gives `abort`.

The look is OURS: a `window_pane` of one row a field plus the button row,
one cell wider than the widest row. The coordinator keeps the shown
choice windows (`add_choice/2`, `remove_choice/2`), and a left click on one
goes to its `choose/3`, not to a selection. Deactivating draws back what
was under the sheet, and the selected window's cursor, which
`cursor_aside/1` took off when the sheet was drawn. A choice window is
not a window the user arranges (OURS): a middle click on one opens no
manipulator, and MENU's submenu leaves it out. It has a `permission` of
`out` and no title, the slots the coordinator reads of every window.

**ATTRIBUTE** (TM-125 p8, between PERMISSION and EXIT) opens a choice
window at the mouse, owned by the coordinator. It is made with
`create/4`'s owner, and its ending arrives as `choice_done/3`, since the
coordinator's process may not wait in `read/2`. Of Fig. 4's fields, only
PERMISSION is offered (OURS): it is the one attribute the stand-ins keep
that the sheet may set. do_it sets it (`set_permission/2`) and wakes the
writers. The manipulator now has nine items, so EXIT is item 8, and the
manipulator screens are re-rendered (their pins moved, each listed in
`test_espc.py`).

**Census.** pimfep superviser `error_mode_menu/2` goes from refused to
success in both census tests. Before, its create/3 met a missing class.
The row count is unchanged.

**Tests**:
- `test_win_m9_choice`: the held menu (made by the .ptf) is drawn and
  read back as text, a click on `off` moves the reversal, and
  do_it/`read/2` gives `[{error_mode, off}]`. Deactivate restores the
  frame. Abort gives `abort`. An unknown key is refused.
- `test_win_m9_attribute`: ATTRIBUTE on window 2 of the manipulator run,
  PERMISSION `wait` clicked, then do_it: window 2's permission becomes
  `wait` and the sheet is gone; with abort, it is unchanged. The sheet's
  owner is the coordinator's own process (so manager/1's bypass is live
  on this route); a middle click on it opens nothing; MENU lists the two
  requester windows and not it.

- `test_win_m9_choice_screen`: the menu's screen pinned; the committed
  PNGs decode to their pins.

Screens: `../docs/simpos/screens/choice_window.png` (stage `choice`),
`manipulator_attribute.png` (stage `attribute`); `window_manipulator.png`
re-rendered (nine items).

**Injections, OBSERVED** (2026-11-26; as above, each rerun at the final
code; M9 attribute is `test_win_m9_attribute`, whose sheet is a choice
window too):

| | injection | fails |
|---|---|---|
| M1 | a click on an option does not change the value | M9 choice, M9 attribute |
| M2 | a click on a choice window goes to a selection | M9 choice (ERROR), M9 attribute |
| M3 | deactivate does not give the cursor back | M9 choice, M9 attribute |
| M4 | read/2 returns the defaults (the plan's injection; first run NOTHING failed, the one edit ended on the held default; an edit to `on` added) | M9 choice, M9 attribute |
| A1 | ATTRIBUTE's do_it sets nothing | M9 attribute only |
| E1 | a middle click on a choice window opens the manipulator on it | M9 attribute only |
| E2 | MENU's submenu lists the choice windows | M9 attribute only |
| P13 | (above) | coordinator writes only (ERROR) |

### Window M10: Pmacs proper (2026-11-26)

Three held programs now run whole on the Pmacs stand-ins, each started
from an item added to the system menu (OURS harness classes, in
`test_espc.py` `Window.M10_HARNESS`):
- disc `exshow.esp` (IFS source): `exec_show_tmp go/3` makes a
  `pmacs_window` (title, `size(1100,700)`, `position(0,0)`), creates the
  pooled buffer "boot" (`create/4`, or `get_buffer_instance/3` and
  `reset/1` when the name is taken), `read_file/4`s `me:boot.init`,
  `set_input_buffer/2`, `activate/1`, `set_font/2`, `flash/1`,
  `command_interpret/1`, then `kill/1`. Its class slot `font` is
  initialised from `font:kanji_28.font`, which is not held: the run gives
  that name a directory entry for test_11 (OURS substitute, in the run
  only). Without it the class is refused at that slot.
- cal-esp `cal2.12/cal_pmacs.esp`: `cal_pmacs_window` (a `pmacs_window`
  whose `editor` slot holds `#cal_pmacs_editor`) and `cal_pmacs run/4`
  (load, `set_input_buffer/2`, `goto_beginning/1`,
  `set_input_position/1`, `activate/1`, `command_interpret/1`,
  `deactivate/1`, `current_buffer/2`, save). The held editor's own
  `:command(Editor,pf#11,cal_menu)` is found before the stand-in
  defaults; pf#11 runs the held `cal_menu/1`, refused at the class it
  creates, `cal_op_menu` (cal's, not loaded).
- papyrus `demo/EDITOR/my_select.esp.1`: `my_buffer_selecter`, a
  `pmacs_standard_editor` heir with after-demons on `select_buffer/1,2`
  and `list_buffers/1`. Its two other natures (papyrus classes, not
  loaded) are empty classes here. C-x b runs the stand-in primaries (the
  window visits the next pooled buffer), then the held demon, refused at
  its first send, `show_mode_menu/2`.

**The key macros** (`espc.py` key_constant; gap G2). `mouse#B` is the
atom named `mouse#B` (HELD: the names are in the .ptf symbol tables).
`key#N`, `control#"c"` and `pf#N` are integers (INFERENCE: no such atom
is held). Their values are OURS (ASCII where there is one, C-x = 24,
pf#N = 16#8100 + N), except key#lf = 10. The held cells witness two of
them: kappa2 KMACS2.ESP `command/3` and DUMP.ESP `dumpLine/6.11#1`
compile exact only with mouse#r the atom and key#lf 10 (each injection
loses exactly that row). With the macros, held code that was refused
compiles: kappa2 12,871 -> 12,963 compared, 1,514 -> 1,518 exact, none
lost; paragraph 1,280 -> 1,286 compared, none exact. Paragraph's
`as_view_select_window read/2` nests five deep, and two of its `$or$`s
pair with another arity: espc FLATTENED `(A ; B) ; C` into one `$or$`
where the held compiler keeps the inner one its own predicate, a
behavioural difference under cuts (fixed 2026-11-26; see "Nested
disjunctions and the cut in a `$or$`"). dedit.esp compiles whole since
`->` does (its `exit` was refused before; `test_win_m10_dedit_compiles`);
cal_pmacs.esp, my_select.esp.1, exshow.esp compile whole.

**The stand-ins** (selectors HELD where held code sends them, bodies OURS):
- `editor_text_buffer`: the buffer pool, a class slot (PROSE IPSJ
  114007: buffers "registered in a buffer pool and reachable by name");
  `create/4` fails on a taken name (exshow relies on it); a buffer's
  number is its order of creation from 1. Navigation (`move_to_first/1`,
  `next_line/2` and the rest), `load_file/2`, `contents/2`, `saved/2`.
  The OURS `create/3` of window M4 is renamed `new_buffer/3` (the held
  `create/3` is `(Class, ^Buffer, Size)`).
- `standard_editor`: the default binding tables, `:command(Editor, Key,
  Method)` and `:control_x(..)` (the form HELD: pimos_window_editor
  command/3, dedit control_c/3, papyrus my_save), and the commands. OURS,
  gap G6: C-f/b/n/p/a/e/d/k, DEL and BS, the arrows, RETURN, C-x C-s
  (save), C-x C-z and C-x C-c (exit), C-x C-b (list buffers), C-x b (the
  next pooled buffer: no prompt is stood in), mouse#r (the EDITOR MENU).
  A save writes a NEW VERSION of the file (kl0 `binary_file
  new_version/3`; INFERENCE from TR-524 p9: versions are assigned by the
  file system, old ones kept), and echoes `Written! <path>`.
- `editor_command_interpreter`: each input is looked up in the editor's
  tables (first answer, so a held subclass's clauses come first), the
  method sent with `:refute(Editor, M, {})`; a printing character is
  inserted; anything else is `undefined_key/2` (a beep, `Undefined key`).
  `exit` (the held table binds it) ends the loop after the editor's
  `exit/1`. After each input the window is redisplayed.
- `pmacs_display` (OURS class, not a SIMPOS name): the screen of a window
  that visits a buffer (`set_input_buffer/2`): the text rows, the mode
  line `PMACS(fundamental)(C,R) *-N* Name Path --Top--` reversed, the
  echo line (SCREENSHOT IPSJ 36239 p5-6, IPSJ 114012 Fig. 2; the fields
  per window.md §2.11; layout OURS). Only the rows that changed are
  drawn. The first shown line moves only to keep the point's line on
  the screen; the place field is `--Top--`, `--Bot--` or `--N%--` (N the
  first shown line's share of the lines, OURS) (`test_win_m10_scroll`).
  Implemented but NOT exercised by any test: `status_window(off)` (no
  mode line), a point past the last column (no cursor), and exshow's
  fallback when the buffer name is taken (`get_buffer_instance/3`,
  `reset/1`). It draws with two new `window_pane` methods, `draw_row/4` and
  `set_cursor/3`, over the existing bitmap_display primitives. **No
  device primitive was added**: the frame interface the viewer will tee
  is unchanged. A window that never visits a buffer (PIMOS's console)
  keeps M4's plain text screen.
- `pmacs_window`: the `editor` slot names a subclass's editor class
  (HELD cal_pmacs_window), the held create options (title, position,
  size in dots or cells, `status_window(off)`), the echo line, the right
  button, menu values, `kill/1` and `deactivate/1` off the screen,
  `set_input_position/1` (meaning UNKNOWN: nothing).
- the coordinator: the right button goes to the window under the mouse
  (`right_click/3`); `editor_menu/3` opens the EDITOR MENU there, its
  items verbatim from IPSJ 36239 p5, one column (OURS); a chosen item's
  value goes to the window's input. Only ABORT, List Buffers and Save
  All Files are stood in; the other five refuse loudly.
- `pmacs_standard_editor`: a `standard_editor` heir (INFERENCE).

**A bug found on the way**: `pmacs_window input_data/2` first wrapped
its datum as the term `{data, X}`. The held `pimos_window_process` loop
backtracks after putting it, and the binding of the term's elements was
undone (the README "Open" item on vectors in slots). The console tape's
replay met `{_, _}` and diverged at exchange 11. The datum is now a heap
vector (`new_heap_vector/2`, as list_index.esp's entries); injection X1
puts the term back.

**Census.** 1,485 -> 1,521 rows: 35 new rows inherited by
pimos_window and pimos_window_editor, then `set_input_position/1`
(success), 36 in all; no held row changes outcome; the
held `select_buffer/4` moves its stop past `get_buffer_instance/3`
(row by row in `test_fep_census`, `test_step3f_census_recipes`).

**Tests**: `test_win_m10_exshow` (the screen, an edit saved as version
2 with version 1 kept, exit kills the window, the class refused without
the substitute font), `test_win_m10_keys` (the bindings against an
Emacs's result; undefined keys), `test_win_m10_menu` (the eight items,

`test_win_m10_cal`, `test_win_m10_select`, `test_win_m10_scroll` (the

### Step 3o: the debugger's listener, TM-134's dialogue typed into it (2026-11-26)

The boot route's B8 (`../docs/simpos/boot_route.md` §4.9) had one top
level, the system menu. SIMPOS has a second (§1 of that page): the
debugger, "for debugging but also an ESP listener" (`shell.md` §5.4),
and the dialogue that starts a program in a new process is typed there
(TM-134 PDF p71, folio 67). The step's recorded next item, keyboard
input past the requester's prompt, was already done by step 3m (and
per-window focus by window M7); the menu path's create, create
(#process), activate was already `coordinator.esp` `start/2` (step 3l).
What step 3o adds is the listener. Every inference is in
`../docs/simpos/source_check_2026-11-24.md` §14. Tests:
`test_boot_route.py` (a new file; in `../run_tests.sh`).

| What runs | Label | Test |
|---|---|---|
| The held login.com's first item, `{"debugger", simpos##debugger}`, chosen with the mouse on the SYSTEM MENU, starts `standin/debugger.esp` in a new process (the coordinator's `start/2`, step 3l) | item HELD; the class lost, the stand-in OURS | `test_step3o_dialogue` |

### Window M11: the live view (2026-11-26)

Window plan M11's first half (`../docs/simpos/window_environment_plan.md`
M11). The stand-in SIMPOS draws to a real window on the Mac, and the
Mac's keyboard and mouse go back in as PSI events. The plan's "browser
canvas" was replaced by the SDL2 viewer `../tools/winview`
(`../docs/simpos/winview_protocol.md`), which the window hardware's H7s
had made. Everything here is OURS unless a line says otherwise.

**For the owner: try it.** From `pim/`, once:

    brew install sdl2 pkg-config        # if pkg-config does not find sdl2
    tools/winview/build.sh              # -> bsv/build-win/winview/winview

then the one command:

    python3 -I tools/simpos_live.py live

It boots the stand-in SIMPOS in kl0 (no PIMOS), opens the SYSTEM MENU,
chooses `debugger`, and shows the screen in a 1280 x 1024 window. The
window is shrunk to fit a smaller display, and it can be resized.
Close the window to stop. Things to try:
- **Type at the debugger.** `:create(#process,P).` then RETURN answers
  `> Success !!`. Backspace and Delete rub out. The PSI's dialogue
  (TM-134 p71) is:

      :create(#pimfep##pimos_login_requester,Req).
      :create(#process,Proc).
      :activate(Proc,Req).

  The login requester's window then takes the keyboard. Answer `local`,
  a user, and a password (not echoed).
- **The SYSTEM MENU**: the right button twice (mouse#rr, the PSI's) on
  the background; move onto an item and click it. `debugger` and
  `Login PIMOS` work. The other items' classes are not stood in. A
  refusal is shown in the title bar, and the click is taken back: the
  session runs again without it, so you can go on.
- **Clicks are the PSI's.** Two clicks of one button within 400 ms
  are a double (mouse#ll, mm, rr), and the coordinator ignores ll and
  mm. So leave a moment between two left clicks. The classification is
  done in the launcher (OURS), not in the handler process as plan §3.2
  has it.
- **The window manipulator**: the middle button on a window, or
  Option + the left button (OURS, for a trackpad). It has SHOW, HIDE,
  RESHAPE (click two corners), MOVE (click the new place) and the rest
  of TM-125 p8's items. A left click on a window selects it, and the
  keyboard goes to the selected window.
- **The Pmacs editor**: `python3 -I tools/simpos_live.py live
  --program exshow` (the start is then Login PIMOS) runs the held `disc/exshow.esp` on
  `me:boot.init`, by the M10 harness (an extra system-menu item `M10`,
  started at once). Click in its window, then type. The stand-in's keys
  are C-f C-b C-n C-p C-a C-e, C-d, C-k, Backspace or Delete, RETURN,
  the arrows, C-x C-s to save a new version, and C-x C-z to exit. The
  right button gives the EDITOR MENU. After C-x C-z the session goes
  on (checked headless). `--program cal` and `--program select` run
  the held cal and papyrus programs the same way. Headless, each started
  and took typing; neither was examined further.
- **Graphics**: not attempted live. The window M8 tests send the draw
  calls to a window from Python (`test_espc.py` `graphics_window`);
  there is no program to start from a menu.
- `--record F.events` writes the session's events. `python3 -I
  tools/simpos_live.py replay F.events OUT.pbm` runs them again
  headless and gives the same frame.
- `--start none` shows the bare screen; `--scale 2` doubles the dots.

**How it works** (`../tools/simpos_live.py`, its header):

| Part | What it does | Label |
|---|---|---|
| The screen | `windev.Device.tees`: every command the device runs also goes to the launcher's own `winfb.Frame`. At each quiet moment (every PSI process waits), and every 40 ms while a long run draws, the band of rows that changed goes to the viewer as one `R` (the first flush is `H` and `F`). The device's log and stream are untouched | OURS |
| Keys | The viewer's HID usage goes to its PS/2 set 2 bytes (`wininput.HOST`). The keyboard receiver's model, now fed a byte at a time (`wininput.KbdReceiver`, `receive_kbd` unchanged), applies the §3.1 table KEYS with Shift and Ctrl. The result is the stand-ins' codes (`espc.KEY_CODES`): JIS characters; Ctrl+letter its ASCII control code (control#"x" = 24); cr 13, keypad Enter key#enter, bs 8, del 127, tab, esc, the arrows, Insert key#help, Pause key#abort; F1-F12 pf#0-11, Shift-F1..F7 pf#12-18. Alt is not passed on | tables as H5 (OURS); codes OURS except key#lf (G2) |
| Mouse | Moves and buttons go to the H7s adapter (`win_kl0_host.Adapter`): a click is a down and an up; a second click of the same button whose down comes within 400 ms makes `ll`/`mm`/`rr`; moves are held, and the last is sent at the quiet moment unless a click carries the place. The viewer stamps each event (the `W` message before it, SDL's event time), so clicks are classified by when they were made. Option + left = middle | OURS (plan §3.2 gives double clicks to the handler process; its stand-in does not classify) |
| Events in | Each batch goes on the coordinator's event stream at the next quiet moment: `PsiSide.run`'s idle hook, the path `test_espc.py` `Window.later_run` uses. The launcher blocks there until the viewer has something | the pump of window M1 |
| A refusal | kl0 cannot go on after a refusal, so the batch that met it is dropped and the session is run again from the boot with the events before it (seconds). The viewer is redrawn | OURS |
| Rubout | `standard_io_window` `getl/2`: BS and DEL take the line's last code back, and with echo on rub out its character (`window_pane` `rub_out/1`, back across a wrapped row). They are not echoed | INFERENCE that the PSI's line read had one; body OURS |

**Recorded mode, the proof that the live path is the tested machine**
(`test_live.py`, in `../run_tests.sh`, about 40 s). The session scripts
`live_sessions/*.script` are a user's keys and mouse, in `wininput.py`'s
script lines plus `quiet` and `start`. They are compiled to the
viewer's raw events, written to an event file and read back, then run
through the launcher's `Session`. The frame checked is the one rebuilt
from the H, F and R messages the viewer was sent (`simpos_live.Peer`):

| Session | Pin | Exercises |
|---|---|---|
| `debugger_dialogue` | `test_boot_route.SCREEN_SHA` | mouse#rr, the menu item, shifted punctuation, RETURN, six batches |
| `debugger_rubout` | the same sha | Backspace across the wrapped goal, Delete, a rubout in the unechoed password |
| `system_menu` | `Window.MENU_PINS['boxed']` | a held move, flushed at the quiet moment |
| `pmacs_editor` | `Window.M10_PINS['pmacs']` | Ctrl letters as control codes (C-n, C-x C-s); the M10 harness's `start` |
| `manipulator_reshape` | `Window.SHOT_PINS['reshaped']` | the middle button; left clicks 500 ms apart; other batch boundaries than the pin's |
| `manipulator_hide` | `Window.SHOT_PINS['hidden']` | HIDE, the save area restored |
| `editor_menu` | `Window.M10_PINS['editor_menu']` | one right click: mouse#r once the double-click time has passed, not rr |

Each also checks that the viewer's frame equals the device's.
`test_live_keys` pins the translation table. `test_live_takeback` runs
the live loop on a stand-in viewer: a click on `pmacs` is refused and
taken back, and the next line reaches the debugger. `test_live_winview` runs
the real viewer headless (`SDL_VIDEODRIVER=dummy`, only if pkg-config
finds sdl2). Its new `--events` pushes `debugger_dialogue`'s events
into SDL's own queue, so they leave by winview's event code with its
time stamps. The launcher takes them as a user's, in 26 batches where
the script had 7. The viewer's own `--dump` equals `SCREEN_SHA`, and
the launcher's `--record` of it replays to the same frame.

**Injections, OBSERVED** (2026-11-26; each into the file, restored with
`cp` and checked with `cmp`; `test_live.py` run):

| | injection | fails |
|---|---|---|
| I1 | `wininput.key_event`: Shift lost on digits and punctuation (a wrong key mapping) | `_keys`, `_pins` (debugger_dialogue, debugger_rubout), `_winview` |
| I2 | `windev.Device.run`: the tee skips `glyph` (a dropped tee command) | `_pins` (all five sessions then: the viewer's frame is not the pin), `_winview` |
| I3 | `LiveAdapter`: Ctrl+letter sends the JIS code (the H7s adapter's way) | `_keys`, `_pins` (pmacs_editor) |
| I4 | `standard_io_window.esp`: BS and DEL kept in the line | `_pins` (debugger_rubout) |
| I5 | `Screen.flush`: the changed band sent one row short | `_pins` (all five sessions then), `_winview` |
| I6 | `sessions`: the take-back drops one event more | `_takeback` only |

I1-I5 were run before `test_live_takeback` existed, I6 after it, and
all six before `manipulator_hide` and `editor_menu` joined the pins.

**Screens** (`../docs/simpos/screens/live_*.png`): the stages of
`live_sessions/tour.script` (`live_system_menu`, `live_debugger`,
`live_manipulator` opened with Option + left, `live_moved` after MOVE)
and `tour_pmacs.script` (`live_pmacs`: a line typed above
`boot.init`'s first, then C-p C-e, a Backspace and `!`). Each is
winview's own `--dump` from a headless live run of the stage's events,
checked against an in-process replay of them (`python3 -I
tools/simpos_live.py shots psi/live_sessions/tour.script
docs/simpos/screens`). exshow's 1100 x 700 window does not fit the save
memory over the debugger's two windows. Started after them, it is
refused (winfb BADSAVE), so the Pmacs session starts from Login PIMOS,
as `--start login --program exshow` does.

**What was tested only headless.** Everything above ran with SDL's dummy
video driver, and no one has watched or typed into the window. Not
tested: a real display; a real keyboard (HID usages as SDL reports them
on macOS); the trackpad's buttons; and typing speed against kl0's.
The fitted or resized window is not tested either. When the window is
shrunk, SDL scales the 1-bit raster with its default (nearest)
sampling, so thin strokes of the 11 x 14 glyphs may drop out. A display
with 1,024 free rows, or an external monitor, shows every dot. Many
mouse moves are cheap to replay: 2,000 move-only batches added 0.4 s
to a take-back's replay. The protocol, the viewer's event code and the
launcher are the same code in both cases. The first interactive run is
the owner's.

**Not done.** The IFS-application half of M11. The system menu's other
eight items have no stand-in class. The graphics demos have no menu
entry. Double clicks are classified host-side, not in the handler
process. There is no meta# (Alt), and no key repeat beyond SDL's own.

### The Pmacs reconstruction (2026-11-27)

The spec is `../docs/simpos/subsystems/pmacs.md`. Every selector held code
sends to a Pmacs editor is now stood in. Before, mark/1, exchange_point/1,
next_term/1, push_to_yank/3, clear_input/1, cursor_with_mouse/1,
two_windows/1, find_file/2 and write_file/4 were refused. The behaviour
comes from the held manuals and call sites. Each body is labelled
RECONSTRUCTED-FROM-MANUAL, INFERENCE (Emacs analogy: the ESP manual calls
Pmacs "based on Emacs") or OURS, with its pmacs.md section.

What changed, by stand-in:
- `standard_editor.esp`: the methods; a yank buffer shared by every
  editor (a class slot); the bindings C-@, C-w, ESC w, C-y, C-v, ESC v,
  C-x C-x, C-x C-f, C-x C-w, C-x 2/1/o, mouse#l; kill_line/1 now pushes
  to the yank buffer.
- `pmacs_window.esp`: read_with_prompt/3 (the echo-line prompt),
  get_activate_window_count/2, exchange_window/1, two views,
  view_next_screen/1 and view_previous_screen/1 (the KWIC manual's
  ctrl-V and meta-V), mouse_cell/3 and left_click/3.
- `pmacs_display.esp`: a two-view redisplay. The one-view output is
  byte-identical, so every M10 pin holds.
- `editor_text_buffer.esp`: a mark, and term_region/3, which scans for
  ESP term ends past comments and quotes.
- `editor_command_interpreter.esp`: an ESC prefix and clear_input/1.
- `coordinator.esp`: a left click on the window already selected is
  given to it (left_click/3; `standard_io_window` has a no-op).

Three held papyrus editors (`held.py` MEMBERS my_syntax, my_delete,
my_point) run in `test_win_pmacs_syntax`, `_delete` and `_point`, with
OURS stubs for the papyrus classes not loaded. `test_win_pmacs_live` and
`_page_and_input` drive exshow's window with the defaults. Its frame is
pinned as `M10_PINS['pmacs_edit']` and replayed live by
`live_sessions/pmacs_edit.script`. The census grows by 30 rows, from 1,521
to 1,551, all of them inherited by pimos_window and pimos_window_editor;
no row changes outcome (test_fep_census). The injections are in pmacs.md
§6.

### MENDELS: the KL1 and ESP halves linked (2026-11-27)

`mendels.py` runs MENDELS (IFS No.61) on both machines at once: its KL1
half on PIMOS (ref/pimm, from the shell's prompt checkpoint), its ESP half
compiled by espc.py and run on kl0. They are linked as the real system
linked them, by the FEP's string I/O: PIMOS's 601 create_string_io
`mendel##...` reaches the held `pimos_string_io_invoker`, which makes an
instance of MENDELS's class, and each 620 io(Code, String) reaches that
class's `:io/4` (`fep_bridge.BridgeFep` with `BRIDGED=(601,)`; the rest
of the FEP stays `fep.py`'s with the sweep runner's services). The
furthest point, the route and the next stop are in
`../docs/ifs_apps.md` §6 item 1; the run is pinned by
`mendels_tapes/doc.json` and `test_mendels.py` (replay; the live run
opt-in with `MENDELS_LIVE=PROMPT.pkl`).

    python3 -I psi/mendels.py live PROMPT.pkl [--record TAPE] [--png F]
    python3 -I psi/mendels.py replay psi/mendels_tapes/doc.json
    python3 -I psi/mendels.py replay psi/mendels_tapes/doc.json --lenient \
        --key '>sys>user>MENDELuser' --png mendels_path.png     # the dialog frame
    python3 -I psi/mendels.py compile [-v]

The PSI side registers the user `pmpsi` with the password '' after the
mount, because the checkpoint's PIMOS sends that password in its 601s
(OBSERVED: with only BOOT's password, 601 is answered 5). Its keys are
the dialog's two lines, `>sys>user>MENDELuser` and `y` (`USER_LINES`).

**espc.py** (each KNOWN-PLAINTEXT on held cells):
- `repeat`: the goals after it become a `$or$` of one clause that begins
  `c5 n 00 00`, `c2 00 ff fe` (paragraph view_selector read/2's `$or$4/3`
  @d32, kappa2 kappaIVT_new_index `$or$0/1` @811; `split_repeat`).
- the built-ins freeze/2 (blt 3e) and melt/2 (3f), new rows of
  `kl0_opcodes.tsv` witnessed in paragraph (p_vwin.esp:63,
  p_MAIN.esp:407), and named FREEZE and MELT at blt 3e and 3f by the
  held KL0 microcode's mnemonic table (`asm/pu_sysinit.py`'s pin, 124 ->
  126 rows named); atom/1, heap_vector/2, object/3 and
  not_equal_string/2, whose rows were already witnessed.
- a component of the class being compiled is the slot `pkg##class$name`
  (qxt##name_table class new_expr/2 @4e).
The paragraph comparison goes from 1,289 to 1,432 predicates compared
(258 -> 263 exact), kappa2's from 13,133 to 14,406 (1,530 -> 1,777); no
exact row is lost (test_counts, test_kappa2 have the breakdown).

**kl0.py**: blt 3e freeze and 3f melt (`copy_fresh`: a copy with new
variables; the ESP manual's meaning, program-e.tex 1895-1939, PRINTED;
the form OURS). **esp_reader.py**: the infix `::` (PIMOS writes
`mendel::example1`; its priority OURS).

**Stand-ins** (each method labelled in its file):

| class | file | methods | labels |
|---|---|---|---|
| standard_io_buffer | `standin/standard_io_buffer.esp`, `mendels_simpos.py` | create/2; reset/1, reread/1, putl/2, putt/2, gett/2, getl/2 (models) | PRINTED: create and the ESP manual's Example 3 (program-e.tex 6733-6745); INFERENCE: reset, reread, the end-of-text term, putl's newline; OURS: the writer |
| library_utility | `standin/library_utility.esp`, `mendels_simpos.py` | create/2, set_output/2, load/2; get_packages_with_wildcard/3, get_classes/4, get_classes_with_wildcard/5 (models) | HELD-SOURCE: set_output/2 and load/2 (cil ds_bep.esp, a COPY of the class); INTERPOLATED: create/2 and the queries (USAGE) |

Labels, counted from the files (`% LABEL:` lines in the stand-ins,

**The temporary_menu step (2026-11-27).** The run now goes past
`temporary_menu:create/3`. The new stop is mendel_window's
`:create(#base, ...)`. `base` is MENDELS's own class, and its natures

`with_shared_window`) are SIMPOS window mixins whose code is not held.
The tape is re-recorded live from a copy of the prompt checkpoint: the
exchanges and strings are unchanged, only the stop and `where` move. What
was added:
- `standin/temporary_menu.esp`. Of its 17 methods, 9 are
  RECONSTRUCTED-FROM-MANUAL from TM-125 pp14-18 (create/3 with `title`
  and `items_list`, show, read: the item_id or `abort` when the mouse

    hover box, and abort on leaving or on another pop-up;
  - `menu_pane.esp` gains create/7 with `label_flag`, `inside/3` and

  `test_mendels.py` `Menus` runs it through two other held users (disc's
  system_data init/1 and euod's syntax_print goal/1). Screen:
  `../docs/simpos/screens/temporary_menu.png`.
- **espc.py**:
  - vectors of 7 to 255 elements (`b7 A 00 n`, KNOWN-PLAINTEXT);
  - float constants `I.D` (`pmi a8` and a tag-0a float cell, paragraph
    color_line_v @402);
  - X/Y on two registers (blt 43, the quotient over the first operand,
    color_line_v @3f6);
  - integer_to_floating_point/2 (blt 6c) and floating_point_to_integer/2
    (blt 6d), new rows of `kl0_opcodes.tsv` witnessed in paragraph
    c_bar_v.esp:249-253 (180 and 81 held uses). The held microcode's
    mnemonic table names them NtoSFL and NtoINT, so `asm/pu_sysinit.py`'s
    pin goes from 126 to 128 rows named.
  Float comparisons stay refused: blt 61 is FLTcmp in the table, and no
  comparison on floats is witnessed. Paragraph goes from 1,432 to 1,710

  have the breakdown). MENDELS's refusals go from 689 to 402.
- **kl0.py**: `Flt`, a single-precision float. Its precision is an
  INFERENCE: the cell holds 32 bits. Floats get add, subtract, multiply
  and divide, and blt 6c/6d (the integer back toward zero). A float mixed
  with an integer refuses.
- **mendels.py**: `font:kanji_16.font`, which is not held, is entered in
  `>sys>font` for test_11's file (OURS, `SUBSTITUTE_FONTS`).

What was added:
- **The window mixins** (`standin/basic_window.esp` and its

  clear_buffer/1), as_superior (the inferiors, read/3 and read/6 of the
  shared buffer), with_superior_input_buffer (input put in the
  superior's buffer), with_shared_window (assign_window/1, a no-op),

  as_output (write/2, write_line/2-4, the text kept as rows),
  as_markers (create_marker/2 and /7, get_marker_list/2). An inferior is
  drawn inside its superior and cut at its superior's inside
  (clip_inside/5). The composites, whose nature lists are INFERENCE from
  their names and the held lists of window.md §1.5:
  essential_scrolling_menu(_with_superior_input_buffer),
  essential_window_with_superior_input_buffer, multi_column_menu(_with_
  superior_input_buffer), temporary_multi_column_menu (TM-125 p8 lists

  standard_temporary_menu.

  `operator_pool.esp` with standard_io_buffer get_parsing_operator/2 and
  set_parsing_operator/2 (the pool kept, not consulted by the reader),
  standard_input_file gett/4, list_index put_contents/2,

  standard_io_window write_line/2, write_lines/2, erase_cursor/1 and
  set_superior/2 (kept only), window_pane create_frame/8.
- **kl0.py**: symbolizer get_atom_string/3 (held paragraph sends it,
  as_pred.esp:421 and g_window.esp:148) and get_quoted_atom_string/3
  (MENDELS system_io; the quoting OURS) as models.

  test-and-branch blt e8, both KNOWN-PLAINTEXT on held paragraph cells

(`../docs/simpos/screens/mendels_zone.png`, pinned by test_replay; the
live run's screen is the same, byte for byte). resume/0 returns, and
esp_go/3 runs exec/3, which calls the held phase/2 on the phase read back,
`loaded_library(normal([mendel::example1, ...]))`. **The new stop:**

    mendels: held mendel##mendel_main phase/2 has no clause for
    {loaded_library,{normal,[{::,mendel,example1},...]}} (call_esp/3 would
    retry the same message)

phase/2 (mendelmain.esp:379-568) has `loaded_library(normal)` and
`loaded_library(abnormal)` only; `phase(Class,_)` at :569 is not a clause
but the last body goal of the compiled1 clause above it (:568 ends in a
comma). The held PIMOS 4.0 load (`pimos src/library/unloader/load.kl1`:57,
`check_status(normal(_), ...)`) answers `normal(Modules)`, which is what
the KL1 half passes on (main.kl1:140-144); only MENDELS's own branch for
a missing mdllibrary.exe answers a bare `normal` (mdl_comp.kl1:43-54).
INFERENCE: MENDELS was written against a load that answered `normal`
(the `::` reading of the term is OURS, so this is not settled). When
phase/2 fails, esp_control/2 fails and call_esp/3's `repeat`
(esp_device.esp:31-42) retries with Inst!message unchanged: create/1 and
resume/0 again. OBSERVED before the stop was made: the retry backtracks
over all that the first pass bound, and the stand-ins keep pure data in
slots (menu items, text rows), which the ESP manual forbids ("The slot
can contain only atomic data and inpure data. Pure data has its value
released by backtracking", program-e.tex:1899; held MENDELS freezes its
own, esp_device.esp:39-40); every menu of the zone came back with its
items unbound, a menu draw failed silently, and the run backtracked into
create_window/1's re-entry clause to refuse at parts_normal_window
clear/1. That second pass says nothing about MENDELS, so `mendels.py`
stops where phase/2 fails, before anything is undone (`watch_phase`, OURS;
`PHASE_WATCH`). The watch fires whenever phase/2 fails as a whole; its
text says "has no clause for" because the heads were read for this term. The 21 exchanges and their strings are unchanged; the
tape is re-recorded live from a copy of the prompt checkpoint.

What was added:
- **pmacs_window as an inferior** (`standin/pmacs_window.esp` "THE

- **with_superior_input_buffer on a pmacs_window**: pmacs_window gets the
  attribute `input_to := self`, and with_superior_input_buffer's
  `superior` comes first in a class with both natures (mendel_pmacs). The
  form's input then goes to the superior's buffer, named as coming from
  the pmacs_window, so base's read/3 names the system window.
- **inferior_io_form put_codes/2**: the pane protocol's write. key#lf
  starts the next row, a row that reaches the last whole column goes on
  in the next, and a newline past the last row scrolls the rows up
  (INFERENCE, window_pane.esp's look; the wrap OURS).
- **mendels.py watch_phase** (above), OURS.

Open: making the stand-ins retry-safe (impure data in slots, as
program-e.tex:1899 requires) before any honest second pass. A Pmacs
window inside a superior has no keyboard input of its own and no Pmacs
screen (mode line, echo line). reshape/5 and set_text_size/3 on an
inferior pmacs_window set only its own slots (not handed to the form).

**The load answer step: phase/2 settled (2026-11-28).** The question
the system window step left: why the held phase/2 (mendelmain.esp:
382-389) has clauses for `loaded_library(normal)` and
`loaded_library(abnormal)` only, while PIMOS sends
`loaded_library(normal([mendel:: example1, ...]))`.

Answer, from the held sources:
- **PRINTED: the PIMOS manual says load:do/4 answers a bare `normal`.**
  PIMOS Manual V3.0, November 1991 (pimosman.tar.Z, Eman/e-cover1.tex),
  Eman/e-proginfo.tex:494-497: "The atom normal will be returned in the
  argument Status if the loading completes normally, and the atom
  abnormal will be returned otherwise". unload:do/5 beside it (:504-507)
  is documented as `normal(FullPathName)`, so the difference is the
  manual's, not a shorthand.
- **HELD: MENDELS names PIMOS 3.0.** install.e:22-24 ("OS Version: SIMPOS
  7.1, PIMOS 3.0"), dated 1992-07-17.
- **HELD: MENDELS's KL1 half reduces `normal(X)` to `normal` everywhere
  but here.** main.kl1:116-120 (compile1) and :135-139 (save_library)
  turn `normal(SaveObjNmL)` into a bare `normal` before the ESP half;
  :140-144 (load_library) passes load:do's Status as it is, matching the
  manual's bare atom.
- **HELD and OBSERVED: the PIMOS we run answers `normal(Loaded)`.** The
  held 4.0 source, src/library/loader/modtbl.kl1:274-277 check_flush
  (`Status = normal(Loaded)`, the `Package::Module` list), which load.kl1
  :57 check_status matches; the PIMOS 3.6 objects this repository runs
  (pimosobj.tar.Z object/pimm_*360E.sav, `ref/pimm/longrun_ipl.py`:302-320)
  give the same, OBSERVED.
- **Searched, nothing reduces it:** every `:- module load` and every
  load:do caller in pimos.tar.Z and the 348 IFS/ICOT archives (the only
  other `load` module is strtgysh's unrelated vload.kl1:5); MENDELS's
  KL1 and ESP halves; the V3.0 manual (read); man15.ps and man30.j.ps
  (IFS manuals/pimos) searched as PostScript strings only.

So it is a version mismatch: MENDELS was written to the V3.0 manual's
load:do/4, and the loader we run returns the module list. That PIMOS
3.0's own loader answered a bare `normal` is INFERENCE (its code is not
held). Note too that `::` is not an ESP system operator (program-e.tex:
7816-7850), so SIMPOS's reader most likely could not have read the term
PIMOS sends at all.

**The fix, an OURS adapter** (`mendels.adapt_load_answer`,
`LOAD_ADAPTER`): on the PSI side, after the exchange (the tape keeps
PIMOS's own string) and before `:io/4`, an io (620) whose string is
exactly `loaded_library(normal([Package:: Module, ...]))` is replaced by
the packet MENDELS's own held no-file branch makes PIMOS send
(mdl_comp.kl1:43-54 `Result = normal`), `io(0, "loaded_library(normal)")`,
OBSERVED byte for byte by a live run with no mdllibrary.exe served and
pinned in test_mendels (`LOAD_ANSWER_PACKET`). Any other
`loaded_library(...)` string is refused loudly. The held KL1 and ESP code
is not edited. The no-file route is not the main line: it departs from
install.e:60 and readme.e:200, which place mdllibrary.exe in SYSTEM.

**field_tree's "file not exist" is held behaviour.** The archive has no
`tree.field`, readme.e:198-202 lists SYSTEM as mdllibrary.exe, tree.key
and tree.obj only, and field_tree save/2 writes no file when the tree is
